# open_fastapi-audit — the HTTP estate, swept one reference at a time

Working plan, **2026-08-24**, against the working tree at `871b5e14` (+ uncommitted edits, which were
audited as they sit on disk). Unsettled work; **delete this file when the backlog is drained**.
`docs/` is for settled architecture only.

**No code was changed by this audit.** It is a read-only pass whose deliverable is this backlog.

## Why this exists separately from `open_python-audit.md`

That audit swept by SCOPE and produced 14 `fastapi`-category findings from a 17-rule calibration sheet.
This one sweeps by REFERENCE: each of the `fastapi` skill's 21 reference files was read IN FULL, then
applied to the whole estate as its own lane. The difference is depth — a general reviewer checks "is
there a health endpoint"; `health-checks.md` asks whether liveness is separated from readiness, whether
`degraded` is a third state, whether the chart's probe actually points at the path the app serves, and
whether `startup_complete`/`shutting_down` come from the lifespan.

| Lane | References read in full |
| --- | --- |
| `conventions` | `core-conventions.md + anti-patterns.md` |
| `di-lifespan` | `dependencies.md + production-patterns.md` |
| `errors` | `exception-handlers.md` |
| `health` | `health-checks.md` |
| `files-streaming` | `file-handling.md + streaming.md` |
| `pagination` | `pagination.md` |
| `authn-authz` | `authn.md + authz.md` |
| `observability` | `observability.md` |
| `k8s-microservices` | `kubernetes.md + microservices.md` |
| `limits-realtime` | `rate-limiting.md + websockets.md + cache.md` |

`database.md`, `redis.md` and `project-template.md` were read but given no lane, and that decision is
recorded rather than silent — see **Appendix C — the three references with no lane**.

## How this was produced (so you can trust or re-run it)

Ten auditors, one per lane, each instructed to read its reference file(s) in full before opening any
code, and each given the measured shape of the estate (15 FastAPI apps, ~240 routes, both sanctioned
service layouts, the split error contract) so it would not rediscover it. Every finding was handed to a
**separate adversarial verifier** briefed to REFUTE it, with the async/sync trap called out explicitly as
the most common false positive in this codebase: **a plain `def` route doing blocking work is CORRECT** —
FastAPI threadpools it — and only `async def` doing blocking work without `run_in_threadpool` is a
defect. 20 agents, ~3.2 M tokens, 1 047 tool calls.

**De-duplication was mandatory.** Each auditor grepped `open_python-audit.md` for the files it was about
to cite. 14 findings came back already filed there; they are listed in **Appendix B** by the id that
owns them and are NOT restated as new work.

**Verdicts.** `CONFIRMED` = reproduced exactly. `ADJUSTED` = real but re-framed or re-graded by the
verifier, and the corrected form is what appears here. 2 were refuted outright.

## Owner rulings (2026-08-26) — settled before the drain, not during it

Three of the five surviving highs were blocked on decisions rather than engineering. Asked and answered:

1. **The estate is AUTHENTICATED.** `CLAUDE.md:252` claimed *"No auth, no app middleware — the services
   assume localhost / trusted network"* while the chart defaults auth ON and `ingress.yaml:66` publishes
   `/api`. Both could not be true; the doc was the stale half and is corrected in the same change. So
   `compute` and `controlplane` get the door their nine siblings share, and `assist` / `search` /
   `media-clip` are gated. This settles 3 highs and 2 mediums.
2. **Rate limiting and body caps land as ONE SEAM** in `service_kit.middleware.register_middleware`
   (plus the media variant), not per route — so every service inherits them and a new route cannot be
   added uncapped. Closes 7 rows including two owned by `open_python-audit.md`.
3. **Drain order: this bookkeeping pass, then the two cheap highs** (`flows/validate`, `media-clip`),
   then fix-by-seam. The seam clusters are in the triage report: ~7 changes close ~29 rows.

Five findings are NOT drained here — their mechanism is ledgered in `open_python-audit.md`
(`MED-008`/`DUP-21`, `SKG-07`, `SKG-05`/`X11`, `X6`/`VS-13`). Fix them there and close both rows, or the
work is paid for twice.

## What this audit did NOT find (read this before the backlog)

The estate is disciplined on the mechanical rules, and several were verified clean by direct grep rather
than assumed:

- **Zero** `python-jose` (PyJWT throughout, with `governed/oidc.py:1` saying so in its docstring).
  **Zero** `ORJSONResponse` / `UJSONResponse` / `RootModel` / `json_encoders`. **Zero** bare `except:`.
  **Zero** mutable default arguments. **Zero** `lru_cache` on instance methods.
- **Pure-ASGI middleware carries the `scope["type"] != "http"` guard** — `catalog/api/body_limit.py:35`
  and `load_shed.py:66`. That is the crash-on-lifespan-message bug `anti-patterns.md` warns about, and
  both classes get it right.
- **`--workers` is deliberately unset in every dockerfile**, each with the reasoning written down.
  `notifications.dockerfile:80` goes further and explains it is a CORRECTNESS bound, not a sizing
  default: several uvicorn workers behind one port are several `ActorRuntime`s sharing one app-id.
- **No `SecretStr` interpolation leak.** Every `s3_secret_access_key: SecretStr` is read through
  `.get_secret_value()`, and the `f"Bearer {token}"` sites take a plain `str | None`.
- **Only 2 `assert`s in the whole estate**, both type-narrowing with a `# narrowed:` comment — the one
  use `writing-python` sanctions, not runtime validation that `python -O` would strip.

The problems concentrate in four places: **one blocking-IO door that the estate already fixed on its
twin**, **an unmetered compute surface**, **the observability of the HTTP plane**, and **the chart's
probe/drain wiring**.

---

## The findings

### High — 7

<details><summary><b>~~The medallion producer verifies bearers with blocking OIDC discovery + JWKS fetches inside `async def` — the ING-02 fix landed on ingest's twin and not on this one~~</b> <i>(authn.md + authz.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26 — ALREADY FIXED.** `produce_auth.py:130` and `:178` are `await verify_off_loop(verifier, raw)`; the hop is centralised in `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/oidc.py` and pinned by `tests/unit/test_invariants.py`. **Filed TWICE** — the `conventions`-lane copy below is the same defect; both are closed.

**Rule.** authn.md: JWKS/discovery I/O must not stall the request path; estate calibration: "A plain 'def' route doing blocking work is CORRECT … The SAME work inside 'async def' is a defect"

**Sites:** `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:124`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:169`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:58`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:134`

**Why it matters.** `OIDCVerifier.verify` is fully synchronous: oidc.py:186 opens `with httpx.Client(timeout=15.0)` for discovery and oidc.py:233 calls `provider.jwk_client.get_signing_key_from_jwt(token)`, which fetches the JWKS over blocking urllib on a cache miss or an unseen `kid`. Every other authn seam in the estate runs that call off the loop: service_kit/governed/deps.py:80, catalog/api/security.py:41, lineage/api/security.py:138 and annotator/api/security.py:43 are all plain `def` (FastAPI threadpool), and ingest/auth.py:173 explicitly wraps it — `token = await asyncio.to_thread(verifier.verify, raw)` with the comment "Off the loop: verify() does synchronous OIDC discovery + JWKS fetches (up to 15s) on a cold cache or key rotation — inline it stalled every in-flight request in the pod, probes included (open_python-audit ING-02)". The medallion producer is the ONE holdout, and it is the cascade head: `authorize_produce` gates POST /produce, POST /train, POST /ingest-media and GET /authorize, while `authenticate_subject` gates both promotion routes. DUP-03 already records that these two doors are ~120-line copies that "must be fixed together forever"; ING-02 was applied to one copy only, which is exactly the failure mode DUP-03 predicted.

**Fix.** Wrap both calls as ingest does: `token = await asyncio.to_thread(verifier.verify, raw)` at produce_auth.py:124 and `return (await asyncio.to_thread(verifier.verify, raw)).sub` at :169 — no signature change. Then close DUP-03 for real by moving the shared body into `service_kit.governed`, so the next fix cannot land on one copy again. Longer term give `OIDCVerifier` an async `verify` over a shared `httpx.AsyncClient` (ING-02's own stated long-term fix) and delete both threads.

**Evidence.**

```
produce_auth.py:58 `async def authorize_produce(` … :123-124 `try:` / `token = verifier.verify(raw)`; produce_auth.py:134 `async def authenticate_subject(` … :168-169 `try:` / `return verifier.verify(raw).sub`. Contrast ingest/auth.py:105 `async def authorize_ingest(` … :173 `token = await asyncio.to_thread(verifier.verify, raw)`. Failure: on an IdP signing-key rotation, one bearer-authenticated `POST /api/produce` blocks the producer's single event loop for up to 15 s — and that loop also serves `/bronze-arrival` (the cascade trigger), `/medallion-event`, `/promotions/*` and `/livez`+`/readyz`, so the liveness probe queues behind it and can kill the cascade head mid-rotation.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Reproduced at HEAD. `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:58` is `async def authorize_produce(` and line 124 is a bare `token = verifier.verify(raw)`; `:134` is `async def authenticate_subject(` and `:169` is `return verifier.verify(raw).sub`. `verify` is fully synchronous: `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/oidc.py:186` `with httpx.Client(timeout=15.0) as client:` (sync discovery) and `:233` `signing_key = provider.jwk_client.get_signing_key_from_jwt(token).key` (PyJWKClient, blocking urllib on a cold cache / unseen kid). I enumerated every `verify` call site in the estate: `lineage/api/security.py:190` (enclosing `def authenticate` at :138), `annotator/api/security.py:55` (`def authenticate` at :43), `catalog/api/security.py:171` (`def authenticate` at :41) and `service_kit/governed/deps.py:92` (`def authenticate` at :80) are ALL plain `def` — correct, FastAPI threadpools them — and `ingest/auth.py:176` is `token = await asyncio.to_thread(verifier.verify, raw)` with the ING-02 comment verbatim. The two medallion sites are the only `async def` ones, so the 'one holdout' claim is exact, not rhetorical. De-dup checked: `open_python-audit.md:239/1266` files ING-02 against `services/ingest/src/ingest/auth.py:151` + `ingest/api.py:127` only; the medallion sites appear in the audit solely under MED-010 (service-token duplication, low) and DUP-03 (the two doors are copies) — neither is this mechanism. Failure is reachable: `authorize_produce` gates `/produce`, `/train`, `/ingest-media` and `GET /authorize` (producer.py:164-175) and the producer's single loop also serves `/bronze-arrival`, `/promotion-held`, `/promotions/*` and the probes, so one bearer call during an IdP key rotation stalls the cascade head for up to 15 s including its liveness probe. Severity high is right — it is the same grade the audit gave the identical defect on the sibling door.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~`compute` and `controlplane` have no authn/authz code path at all — the hole X6 filed for `search`, in two more publicly-proxied services~~</b> <i>(authn.md + authz.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26 — `1e9acf06`.** Both now declare a settings class mixing in `GovernedAuthSettings`, ship a `security.py` over `service_kit.governed.deps`, and mount the dependency ON THE ROUTER (per route would miss `proxy.py`'s `{path:path}` catch-all). The chart feeds it: `governedAuth: true` for compute, the same env block in `controlplane.yaml`. Rung is READER — `warehouse#reader` is `... or writer or member from project`, so no authorized caller loses access. Pinned by `tests/unit/test_every_public_service_has_a_door.py`, whose chart assertion was proven non-vacuous by deleting the flag. **STILL OPEN, split out:** the project list is not yet FGA-FILTERED per tenant — an authorized caller still sees every project. That changes what an authorized caller sees, so it was deliberately not bundled with closing the exposure.

**Rule.** authn.md § Protected routes / authz.md § Coarse-grained authorization: apply the door at the router level; a service with no `GovernedAuthSettings` cannot bind the estate's OIDC/FGA env

**Sites:** `services/controlplane/src/controlplane/routes.py:36`, `services/controlplane/src/controlplane/__init__.py:8`, `services/compute/src/compute/routes.py:29`, `services/compute/src/compute/proxy.py:58`, `services/compute/src/compute/__init__.py:26`, `services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py:163`

**Why it matters.** Nine services mix in `GovernedAuthSettings` (or declare its byte-identical twin inline, as catalog/lineage/medallion/maintenance do). `compute` and `controlplane` declare neither, ship no `security.py`, and `make_service_app` adds only CORS/RequestID/Timing/SlashTolerance — no auth middleware. So on an `auth.enabled` estate every route in both is anonymous, and both are carried to the public edge by the gateway (`{prefix}/ray`, `{prefix}/projects`, `/api/serve`). `GET /api/projects/` returns every operator Project CR in the cluster — slug, team, workload type, k8s namespace and each tenant's live ingress host — which is exactly the estate-wide enumeration the catalog gates on `can_observe_events` at projects.py:273 and FGA-filters at warehouses.py:268. On the compute side the asymmetry is sharper: the chart deliberately turns Ray token auth ON (chart/templates/rayservice.yaml:54, `ray.auth.enabled` → `RAY_AUTH_TOKEN` injected into every Ray container), compute holds that token (ray_kit/auth.py:40 `auth_headers()`), and then proxies the dashboard to anonymous callers using it — jobs, actors, tasks, cluster state, driver logs and the whole Serve status API. X6 filed exactly this reasoning for `search` and named viewer/api/security.py:1 as the fix precedent; neither of these two appears in the audit's security epic.

**Fix.** Give both the door their siblings already share, at the ROUTER level so no route can be added ungated: make the settings classes mix in `GovernedAuthSettings`, add a `security.py` built on `service_kit.governed.deps.make_auth_deps` (the module viewer/flows/notifications already use — no new mechanism), and mount `routes.router` and `proxy.router` with `dependencies=[Depends(...)]`. For controlplane, FGA-filter the CR list on `can_administer`/`member` of `project:&lt;slug&gt;` so a tenant sees its own projects, mirroring catalog/api/v1/endpoints/projects.py. For compute, gate `/api/ray/*` and `/api/serve/*` on a reader rung against `settings.fga_root_object` — the tier flows/security.py already established for "may you look at / spend the estate's compute". Add the unit test X6 asks for, extended to all three: compute, controlplane and search must refuse an unauthenticated request when `LANCE_OIDC_ENABLED=true`.

**Evidence.**

```
controlplane/routes.py:36-38 `@router.get("/")` / `def list_projects(reader: ReaderDep) -> ProjectsResponse:` — no token, no checker; __init__.py:8 `app = make_service_app(title="controlplane", routers=[health.router, routes.router])`. compute/routes.py:29-30 `@router.get("/jobs")` / `async def ray_jobs(client: RayClientDep, settings: SettingsDep)`, plus seven siblings at :24/:34/:39/:44/:49/:54/:59, and proxy.py:58-59 `router.add_api_route(f"{prefix}/{{path:path}}", catchall, methods=_PROXY_METHODS, ...)`. `grep -rn GovernedAuthSettings services/compute services/controlplane` returns nothing. Failure: an anonymous `GET https://<edge>/api/projects/` enumerates every tenant's namespace and ingress host; an anonymous `GET https://<edge>/api/ray/jobs` and `GET https://<edge>/api/serve/applications/` read the shared Ray cluster's job and Serve state using compute's own `RAY_AUTH_TOKEN`.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Reproduced. `grep -rn GovernedAuthSettings services/compute services/controlplane` returns nothing (the nine mixin/inline-twin sites are viewer, ingest, annotator, flows, notifications + catalog/lineage/medallion/maintenance's inline copies); neither service ships a `security.py` (`ls` shows compute: dependencies/health/__init__/lifespan/proxy/pruner/routes; controlplane: health/__init__/k8s/routes/schemas/service). `controlplane/routes.py:36-38` is `@router.get("/")` / `def list_projects(reader: ReaderDep) -&gt; ProjectsResponse:` — no token, no checker — and `controlplane/__init__.py:8` is `app = make_service_app(title="controlplane", routers=[health.router, routes.router])`. `compute/routes.py:29-30` `@router.get("/jobs")` / `async def ray_jobs(client: RayClientDep, settings: SettingsDep)` plus seven ungated siblings, and `proxy.py:58` registers the `/api/serve/{path:path}` catchall. Exposure verified rather than assumed: gateway rows `(f"{prefix}/ray", ..., *compute)`, `(f"{prefix}/projects", ..., *controlplane)`, `("/api/serve", "/api/serve", *compute)` at gateway/__init__.py:163-181, and the Ingress sends `/api` to that gateway (chart/templates/ingress.yaml:66-72) with no auth middleware anywhere in the gateway. `ProjectDTO` (controlplane/schemas.py:4-13) really does carry `team`, `workload`, `namespace` and `url`, so the anonymous response enumerates every tenant's namespace and ingress host. The compute asymmetry is real too: `proxy.py:19-22` states 'Never widen this without an auth layer in front of /api' — an acknowledgement that no such layer exists, not a decision that one is unnecessary — and `ray_kit/auth.py:40 auth_headers()` attaches compute's `RAY_AUTH_TOKEN`, which the chart injects into every Ray container when `ray.auth.enabled` (chart/templates/rayservice.yaml:54ff), so anonymous callers read the token-protected dashboard through compute's own credential. Mitigating detail worth recording: `_PROXY_METHODS = ["GET", "HEAD"]` (proxy.py:22), so the Serve proxy is read-only — no `deploy` RCE path. De-dup: X6 (open_python-audit.md:162-170) names only `services/search`; the audit's compute/controlplane rows (COMPUTE-RAY-CLIENT-RETRY-STORM, COMPUTE-PROXY-CONTENT-ENCODING, CP-503-NEVER-FIRES, DUP-06, X8, …) contain nothing about authn/authz. Stands on its own at high.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~`open_reader`/`open_writer` fall back to the estate's catalog service credential when the request carries no bearer — the confused deputy their own docstrings name, with no non-request caller to justify it~~</b> <i>(authn.md + authz.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26 — `3bf79958`.** Owner ruling: never substitute, and delete the dead config. All
> three seams forward `caller_token` alone; `MEDIA_CATALOG_TOKEN` is removed from the media settings,
> from `explorer.yaml` (Secret + env) and from `values.yaml`.
>
> The finding's central claim was verified rather than trusted, and it held three ways: all eight call
> sites are request-scoped annotator routes; the credential had no other consumer in the tree; and the
> cited justification — "the publish saga, which outlives any request" — names a REAL saga that mints
> its own bearer from Dex (`annotator/projects/lakehouse.py:388`) and never touched this seam. The
> branch existed solely for the case it must not serve.
>
> Not overcorrected: the movers' `MEDALLION_CATALOG_TOKEN` stays and is pinned by a test, because they
> genuinely have no caller to forward.
>
> Worth keeping: `tests/unit/test_catalog_caller_token.py` already argued this principle in its own
> docstring — "whose stated reason does not apply to a request-scoped read" — and encoded the
> exception in its last parametrized case anyway. That exception was the hole.

**Rule.** authn.md § API keys ("Don't reuse this for end users") / authz.md: authorization scope must equal the caller's scope; `service_kit/governed/deps.py:130-138` states the exact rule this violates

**Sites:** `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/lancekit/reader.py:446`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/lancekit/writer.py:257`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/lancekit/reader.py:465`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/annotations/save.py:119`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/annotations/tags.py:114`

**Why it matters.** `service_kit.governed.deps.raw_bearer` exists precisely so a service reading Lance through the REST catalog forwards the CALLER's bearer, and its docstring gives the reason: "the catalog's own `authorize` checks one relation on one `table:` object and injects no row predicate, so a service token answers 200 for a caller with no grant at all". Both seams then undo it with `token=caller_token or settings.catalog_token`. `caller_token` is `RawBearerToken`, which is `None` whenever the request has no `Authorization` header — so an anonymous request does not fail closed, it is re-issued to the catalog under `MEDIA_CATALOG_TOKEN`, the chart-provisioned identity the values file itself tells operators to "grant … the writer rung on the annotations table" (chart/values.yaml:1261-1263). The stated justification is "callers with no request context (the publish saga, which outlives any request)" — but `grep -rn 'open_reader(\|open_writer('` over services/ and packages/ returns five call sites and every one is inside a request-scoped annotator route (save.py:78/119, tags.py:110/114, commit.py:88, wire.py:93). There is no saga caller in the tree, so the branch fires only for the case it must not serve. The zone BFF's `requireSession: true` on the annotations POST mitigates the browser path, but the annotator Service is reachable from any in-namespace pod (chart/templates/network-policy.yaml admits intra-namespace `podSelector: {}`), and chart/templates/ingress.yaml:18-20 records the estate's own position that these services must not rely on the edge for their gate.

**Fix.** Split the seam by caller kind rather than by nullability: keep `caller_token` REQUIRED on the request-scoped path (raise rather than substitute when it is absent and `settings.catalog_token` is set), and expose the service-credential path as a separate, explicitly named entry point (`open_reader_as_service(...)`) that no route may import — so a future saga has a home and a route cannot silently reach it. If the fallback must stay for auth-off dev, gate it on `not settings.oidc_enabled` rather than on `caller_token is None`, so a governed deployment cannot take it. Add a test that an anonymous POST to `/api/annotations/{doc}/{sid}/{cid}` against a catalog-mode annotator with `MEDIA_CATALOG_TOKEN` set answers 401, not 200.

**Evidence.**

```
reader.py:441-447 `transport = RestCatalogTransport(settings.catalog_uri, delimiter=settings.catalog_delimiter, token=caller_token or settings.catalog_token,)`; writer.py:253-259 the same expression; reader.py:465 `RestCatalogTransport(uri, delimiter=..., token=caller_token or settings.catalog_token)`. save.py:63 `caller_token: RawBearerToken = None` → :119 `writer = open_writer(..., caller_token=caller_token)`. Failure: on a governed release with `explorer.catalogToken` set, `POST http://<release>-annotator:8103/api/annotations/doc1/0/0` from any in-namespace pod with no Authorization header commits a `merge_insert` into the governed annotations table — the catalog authorizes the write as the media service identity, which holds the writer rung, and returns 200.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Reproduced, and the exposure is worse than stated. `reader.py:441-447` `transport = RestCatalogTransport(settings.catalog_uri, delimiter=settings.catalog_delimiter, token=caller_token or settings.catalog_token,)`; `writer.py:253-259` the same expression; `reader.py:465` `RestCatalogTransport(uri, delimiter=..., token=caller_token or settings.catalog_token)`. `caller_token` is `RawBearerToken` = `credentials.credentials if credentials is not None else None` (deps.py:130-138 / annotator/api/security.py:67-79), whose docstring states the exact rule the `or` breaks: 'a service token answers 200 for a caller with no grant at all'. The 'publish saga' justification is unbacked: all eight `open_reader/open_writer/open_catalog_reader` call sites are request-scoped annotator routes (save.py:78/119, tags.py:110/114, commit.py:88, wire.py:75/93, versions.py:75), and the publish saga has its OWN identity resolution that never touches these seams — `annotator/projects/lakehouse.py:69` `def publish_token(settings)`, precedence MEDIA_CATALOG_TOKEN → a freshly minted password-grant token → None. So the branch fires only for the case it must not serve. The write route is genuinely unauthenticated: `save.py:56-64` `def save_annotations(state, author: AuthorDep, ..., caller_token: RawBearerToken = None, ...)` — `AuthorDep` is `service_kit.media.deps.get_author` (deps.py:29-34), a trusted `X-User` header defaulting to `"anon"`, not a verified subject — and neither `annotations/router.py:14 APIRouter(prefix="/api")` nor `api/v1/router.py` carries `dependencies=`. Reachability is stronger than the finding claims: besides intra-namespace ingress, the gateway row `("/api/explorer/annotations", "/api/annotations", *annotator)` (gateway/__init__.py:143) plus the Ingress `/api` rule means an anonymous public `POST /api/explorer/annotations/doc1/0/0` reaches `save_annotations`, which then commits a `merge_upsert` authorized by the catalog as `MEDIA_CATALOG_TOKEN` — the identity chart/values.yaml:1261-1263 tells operators to 'grant … the writer rung on the annotations table'. The zone BFF's `requireSession: true` (frontend/microfrontends/annotator/src/routes/api/annotations/[...path]/+server.ts:18-22) guards only the browser path, exactly as stated. De-dup: nothing in open_python-audit.md touches `caller_token`/`catalog_token` fallback (SK-02/SK-03/SK-04 and DUP-07 cite these files for other mechanisms). High is correct.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~The medallion producer's OIDC door runs blocking discovery + JWKS fetches on the event loop — the ING-02 fix landed on the ingest copy of this duplicated function and never reached the medallion copy~~</b> <i>(core-conventions.md + anti-patterns.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26 — ALREADY FIXED, and a DUPLICATE.** The same defect as the `authn-authz` high above, filed once per lane. Closed by the same change.

**Rule.** anti-patterns.md: "`time.sleep`, `open()`, sync DB driver inside `async def` — blocks the loop, kills throughput" / core-conventions.md § Async vs sync path operations: "Never run blocking code inside an `async def`… Same rule applies to dependencies."

**Sites:** `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:124`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:169`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:58`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:134`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/oidc.py:186`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/oidc.py:233`

**Why it matters.** `authorize_produce` (line 58) and `authenticate_subject` (line 134) are BOTH `async def` FastAPI dependencies — they have to be, because `authorize_produce` awaits `_require_admin(...)` — and both call the synchronous `verifier.verify(raw)` inline. `OIDCVerifier.verify` reaches `_provider_for` → `_resolve`, which on a cold cache or past the `cache_ttl` does `with httpx.Client(timeout=15.0) as client: client.get(f"{discovery_base}/.well-known/openid-configuration")` (oidc.py:186) and then `provider.jwk_client.get_signing_key_from_jwt(token)` (oidc.py:233), a PyJWKClient JWKS fetch with its own 30 s default timeout. This is the identical mechanism the audit filed as ING-02 — and ING-02 IS FIXED at HEAD: services/ingest/src/ingest/auth.py:176 now reads `token = await asyncio.to_thread(verifier.verify, raw)` under a four-line comment citing `open_python-audit ING-02` by name. The fix did not travel because the two doors are two ~120-line copies of one function (audit DUP-03, HIGH), and only the ingest copy was edited. Every other `verifier.verify` call site in the estate is inside a plain `def` (annotator/api/security.py:43, catalog/api/security.py:41, service_kit/governed/deps.py:80, lineage/api/security.py:138), so medallion is the sole outlier — this is a missed site, not a house style. What makes it worse than ING-02: these two dependencies gate EVERY write door on the cascade head. `authorize_produce` guards `POST /produce` (produce.py:44) and `GET /authorize` (produce.py:21); `authorize_train` (line 174) and `authorize_ingest_media` (line 210) both delegate straight into it; `authenticate_subject` guards the promotion review doors (promotions.py:243, promotions.py:255). Dropping the bronze-write emit silently cancels the whole bronze→silver→gold run, so a producer that is wedged is not a slow producer, it is a cascade that does not happen. And `service_kit.probes` is mounted on the same app (producer.py:163), so `/livez` and `/readyz` queue behind the stall and the kubelet can restart the pod mid-request.

**Fix.** Apply the ING-02 fix verbatim to both sites: `token = await asyncio.to_thread(verifier.verify, raw)` at produce_auth.py:124 and `return (await asyncio.to_thread(verifier.verify, raw)).sub` at :169, carrying the same explanatory comment. Better, and what DUP-03 already asks for: collapse `authorize_produce` and `authorize_ingest` into one shared dual-auth dependency in `service_kit.governed` so a fix to the door can only ever land once. Longer term, give `OIDCVerifier` an async `verify` over a shared `httpx.AsyncClient` so the discovery/JWKS fetches are awaited rather than threaded.

**Evidence.**

```
services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:58 `async def authorize_produce(` … :119-126 `if settings.oidc_enabled and verifier is not None and authorization:` / `scheme, _, raw = authorization.partition(" ")` / `try:` / `    token = verifier.verify(raw)`.  services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:134 `async def authenticate_subject(` … :169 `        return verifier.verify(raw).sub`.  Contrast services/ingest/src/ingest/auth.py:172-176: `# Off the loop: verify() does synchronous OIDC discovery + JWKS fetches (up to 15s) on a` / `# cold cache or key rotation — inline it stalled every in-flight request in the pod,` / `# probes included (open_python-audit ING-02).` / `token = await asyncio.to_thread(verifier.verify, raw)`.  packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/oidc.py:174 `if cached is not None and (now - cached[0]) < self._ttl:` (so it recurs on every TTL expiry, not only at cold start); :186 `with httpx.Client(timeout=15.0) as client:`; :233 `signing_key = provider.jwk_client.get_signing_key_from_jwt(token).key`.  Wiring: services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce.py:44 `originator: Annotated[str | None, Depends(authorize_produce)],`; produce_auth.py:199 and :231 `return await authorize_produce(` (train + ingest-media delegate); services/medallion/src/medallion/api/promotions.py:243 and :255 `subject: Annotated[str | None, Depends(authenticate_subject)],`; services/medallion/src/medallion/producer.py:163 `app.include_router(health_router)`.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Reproduced exactly. `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:58` `async def authorize_produce(` and :134 `async def authenticate_subject(` are both coroutines (authorize_produce must be — it does `await _require_admin(fga_client, user=token.sub, obj=obj)`), and both call the synchronous verifier inline: :124 `            token = verifier.verify(raw)` and :169 `        return verifier.verify(raw).sub`. Neither has `asyncio.to_thread`/`run_in_threadpool`. The blocking mechanism is real: `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/oidc.py:174` `if cached is not None and (now - cached[0]) &lt; self._ttl:` → past the TTL it falls through to :186 `        with httpx.Client(timeout=15.0) as client:` and then :233 `            signing_key = provider.jwk_client.get_signing_key_from_jwt(token).key` (PyJWKClient JWKS fetch). So it recurs on every TTL expiry, not only cold start. The ingest twin IS fixed at HEAD — `services/ingest/src/ingest/auth.py:176` `            token = await asyncio.to_thread(verifier.verify, raw)` under a comment citing `open_python-audit ING-02` by name — so this is a missed site, not house style. The 'sole outlier' claim checks out: every other `verifier.verify` call site sits inside a plain `def` and is therefore correctly threadpooled — annotator/api/security.py:55 (enclosing `def authenticate` at :41... verified plain def), lineage/api/security.py:190 (enclosing `def authenticate(` at :138), catalog/api/security.py:171 (`def authenticate(` at :41), service_kit/governed/deps.py:92 (`def authenticate(...)` at :80). Wiring confirmed: produce_auth.py:199 and :231 both `return await authorize_produce(` (train + ingest-media delegate); promotions.py:243 and :255 `subject: Annotated[str | None, Depends(authenticate_subject)],`; producer.py:163 `app.include_router(health_router)` puts `/livez`/`/readyz` on the same loop. DE-DUP: not filed. `grep -n produce_auth open_python-audit.md` returns only MED-010 (token-compare duplication, produce_auth.py:76) and DUP-03 (the two doors are copies — a duplication finding, not a blocking-IO one); ING-02's sites are `services/ingest/src/ingest/auth.py:151`, `api.py:127` only. This stands on its own. High is right — the audit graded the identical mechanism HIGH on the ingest door, and this one gates the cascade head plus the health probes.


**FIXED 2026-08-25 — `8b69a501`.** The hop moved into `service_kit.governed.oidc.verify_off_loop`, and all three coroutine call sites (medallion `produce_auth.py` x2, ingest `auth.py`) now go through it — so the fix cannot fail to travel to a fourth door again. Pinned by two thread-identity tests in `tests/unit/test_produce_auth.py` and a CLAIM-LINT AST gate in `tests/unit/test_invariants.py` (`test_no_coroutine_verifies_a_bearer_on_the_event_loop`) that walks `services/` and `packages/` for `verifier.verify(...)` inside any `async def`.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~POST /api/flows/validate parses an arbitrary-size graph on the event loop with no auth and no cap — measured 3.06 s of loop block per 28.5 MiB request~~</b> <i>(rate-limiting.md + websockets.md + cache.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26 — `bc4539bc`.** `FlowGraph.nodes` carries `max_length`, so pydantic refuses at the boundary instead of building every node and consulting the ceiling afterwards. The `graph.py` check stays as defence in depth (exercised via `model_construct`) because a 422 cannot state the problem in the caller's vocabulary. Both `/validate` and `/catalog` gated at the READ rung; the module docstring that argued they should stay open is corrected.

**Rule.** rate-limiting.md: "apply to auth routes and expensive operations only" — an expensive route with no limit; compounded by the cap (MAX_GRAPH_NODES) being enforced AFTER the work it is supposed to bound

**Sites:** `services/flows/src/flows/routes.py:88`, `services/flows/src/flows/models.py:112`, `services/flows/src/flows/graph.py:41`

**Why it matters.** `async def validate(graph: FlowGraph)` takes no subject, no checker and no token — it is the only flows route with no gate, and the module docstring says so implicitly by naming only "/runs" as gated. `FlowGraph.nodes: list[FlowNode]` and `edges: list[FlowEdge]` carry NO `max_length`; `MAX_GRAPH_NODES = 256` is checked inside `validate_graph` at graph.py:41, i.e. after pydantic has already constructed every node. Both the parse and `validate_graph` run inline in an `async def`, so the cost lands on the flows event loop, not the threadpool. That means the 256-node ceiling — a number the models.py comment defends with careful grpc arithmetic — bounds what a run may EXECUTE but bounds nothing about what the door will PARSE. The blast radius is the whole service: `X8` in open_python-audit records that the `make_service_app` services expose liveness only and the chart points BOTH k8s probes at it, so a wedged loop is a wedged probe, and repeated requests cycle the pod that owns the durable Dapr workflow lane.

**Fix.** Two lines, and they belong at the door: put `Field(max_length=MAX_GRAPH_NODES)` on `FlowGraph.nodes` and `max_length=MAX_GRAPH_NODES * MAX_NODE_FAN_IN` on `.edges`, so pydantic refuses at parse time and the 422 arrives before the CPU is spent — the ceiling that models.py already reasons about then actually binds. Register `BodySizeLimitMiddleware` (lift it from `catalog/api/body_limit.py` into `service_kit`) in `make_service_app` with a fleet-appropriate cap. Then add `@limiter.limit("…/minute")` to `/validate` and `/catalog`, the two ungated rows.

**Evidence.**

```
`services/flows/src/flows/routes.py:88-93`: `@router.post("/validate", response_model=ValidateResponse)` / `async def validate(graph: FlowGraph) -> ValidateResponse:` / `problems = validate_graph(graph)` — no `security.CurrentSubject`, no `security.CheckerDep`, unlike `create_run` at :101 which takes both. `models.py:112`: `nodes: list[FlowNode] = Field(default_factory=list)` — no max_length. MEASURED on this checkout with `uv run`: a body of 500 000 minimal nodes serializes to 28.5 MiB (under the Ingress's 64 MiB `proxy-body-size`); `FlowGraph.model_validate_json` took 0.97 s and `validate_graph` a further 2.09 s, then returned `['graph has 500000 nodes, over the 256-node ceiling']` — the refusal arrives after 3.06 s of event-loop CPU. Reachable at the edge: gateway row `(f"{prefix}/flows", f"{prefix}/flows", *flows)` (gateway/__init__.py:174) behind the Ingress `/api` Prefix rule.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Reproduced on this checkout. `services/flows/src/flows/routes.py:88-92`: `@router.post("/validate", response_model=ValidateResponse)` / `async def validate(graph: FlowGraph) -&gt; ValidateResponse:` / `problems = validate_graph(graph)` — genuinely `async def`, no `security.CurrentSubject`, no `security.CheckerDep`, unlike `create_run` at :104-111 which takes both. `services/flows/src/flows/models.py:111-112`: `nodes: list[FlowNode] = Field(default_factory=list)` / `edges: list[FlowEdge] = Field(default_factory=list)` — no `max_length` on either. `services/flows/src/flows/graph.py:41-42`: `if len(graph.nodes) &gt; MAX_GRAPH_NODES: problems.append(f"graph has {len(graph.nodes)} nodes, over the {MAX_GRAPH_NODES}-node ceiling")` with `MAX_GRAPH_NODES = 256` (models.py:70) — checked AFTER pydantic constructed every node, exactly as claimed. MEASURED with `uv run python` against the real modules: 500 000 minimal nodes → 17.06 MiB body (the auditor said 28.5 MiB; their nodes carried more fields — both are well under the 64m ingress cap, and the cap admits ~1.8M nodes ≈ 11 s), `FlowGraph.model_validate_json` 0.78 s, `validate_graph` 2.29 s, result `['graph has 500000 nodes, over the 256-node ceiling']` — 3.07 s total before the refusal. Reachable: gateway row `(f"{prefix}/flows", f"{prefix}/flows", *flows)` at gateway/__init__.py:174 behind the `/api` Prefix rule (ingress.yaml:66). Probe claim verified verbatim at packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/probes.py:45-47: `async def livez()` / "# async, not sync def: the probe must run ON the event loop, never queued behind the blocking data-plane threadpool" — so a wedged loop is a wedged probe. Two precision notes that do not change the verdict: (a) `/catalog` (routes.py:82-83, `async def get_catalog()`) is ALSO ungated, so "the only flows route with no gate" is off by one; (b) FastAPI validates the body on the event loop for sync routes too, so only the 2.29 s `validate_graph` half is specifically an async-def defect — the parse half would block the loop either way. NOT a duplicate: open_python-audit.md's flows entries are FLOWS-REDOS-ON-LOOP (executor.py:238-253, caller-supplied regex on /runs), FLOWS-DURABLE-RUN-UNREADABLE (routes.py:113), FLOWS-422-BYPASSES-HIERARCHY (routes.py:66) and FLOWS-NODE-ESCAPE — none reaches routes.py:88 or the unbounded parse.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~GET /api/explorer/media-clip runs an unauthenticated ffmpeg libx264 transcode behind a process-global lock — 41 concurrent requests exhaust the viewer's threadpool while /livez stays green~~</b> <i>(rate-limiting.md + websockets.md + cache.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26 — `6f69a097`.** All three halves. Gated on `can_read_data` (a clip IS the
> media — the rung `pages.py` uses for image bytes, not `datasets.py`'s metadata rung). The global
> `threading.Lock` is a bounded semaphore with a NON-BLOCKING acquire: `MAX_CONCURRENT_BUILDS = 2`
> and a third caller gets `ClipBusyError` → 503 + `Retry-After` instead of a parked threadpool
> thread; the route is `async def` and sends the build to `asyncio.to_thread` explicitly. Cache key
> quantized to 250 ms, pinned in BOTH directions (windows within the quantum share a file; genuinely
> different ones must not collide, since serving the wrong media beats a cache miss).
>
> **Found on the way, and fixed at the shared layer:** `DomainError` could not carry response
> headers — `__init__` took `detail` alone — so `ServiceUnavailableError(..., headers=...)` would
> have TypeError'd AT RAISE TIME and turned the 503 into a 500, on the saturation path. The handler
> then dropped them a second time, building its own `JSONResponse` from status and body only. Both
> halves fixed with their own RED tests. This is on the rate-limit seam's path: `Retry-After` on a
> 429 is the whole meaning of that status.

**Rule.** rate-limiting.md: "apply to auth routes and expensive operations only" — a per-request subprocess transcode is the most expensive route family in the estate and carries no limit

**Sites:** `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/media.py:264`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/services/clips.py:35`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/services/clips.py:30`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/services/clips.py:96`

**Why it matters.** Cost per request: one full libx264 re-encode (`-preset veryfast -crf 23`, audio to libmp3lame) of up to `MAX_CLIP_S = 660.0` seconds of source media, with a 120 s `subprocess.run` timeout. The disk cache is keyed on `(handle.id, doc_id, int(lo*1000), int(hi*1000))`, so a caller who increments `hi` by one millisecond per request — `?lo=0&hi=1.001`, `1.002`, `1.003` — misses the cache on every single request while `evict_old_clips` churns the 50-file directory. The sharp part is the serialization: `_build_lock = threading.Lock()` is a module-level lock held for the whole `subprocess.run`, and `media_clip` is a plain `def` route, so every waiting request occupies one of anyio's 40 default threadpool tokens blocked on `.acquire()`. 41 concurrent requests exhaust the threadpool, and because the viewer's data routes are almost all sync `def` (media, thumbnail, chunk-frame, pages, atlas, chunks, topics, voice, graph), the entire viewer stops serving. `/livez` and `/readyz` are `async def` by explicit design (`service_kit/probes.py:47` — "async, not sync def: the probe must run ON the event loop, never queued behind the blocking data-plane threadpool"), so they keep answering 200 and Kubernetes never restarts the pod. `chart/values.yaml` runs the explorer trio at `replicas: 1`. The route has no auth (open_python-audit VS-03 confirms media.py:242 is one of the 25 ungated routes) and no limit.

**Fix.** Rate-limit `/api/media-clip` per subject with an `ip:` fallback — this is the single route in the estate where rate-limiting.md's "expensive operations" clause is least arguable. Independently, either bound the number of in-flight builds explicitly (a `threading.Semaphore` sized to available cores, refusing over-capacity with 429 + Retry-After the way `load_shed.py` already does for the catalog) or refuse to WAIT on the lock: `_build_lock.acquire(blocking=False)` and a 503 on contention keeps a request from pinning a threadpool worker for up to 120 s. Also quantize `lo`/`hi` to whole seconds before building the cache key so millisecond-jitter cannot defeat the cache.

**Evidence.**

```
`media.py:264-275`: `@router.get("/media-clip/{doc_id}")` / `def media_clip(doc_id, request, state, lo: Annotated[float, Query(ge=0)], hi: Annotated[float, Query(gt=0)], dataset=None) -> FileResponse:` — plain `def`, no `CheckerDep`, no `CurrentSubject`. `clips.py:30` `MAX_CLIP_S = 660.0`; `clips.py:34-35` `# One build at a time: clips are seconds-cheap and the box's CPU belongs to the model servers` / `_build_lock = threading.Lock()`; `clips.py:39-42` `clip_cache_path` → `CACHE_DIR / f"{cache_key}_{int(lo * 1000)}_{int(hi * 1000)}.mp4"`; `clips.py:63` `with _build_lock:` wrapping `subprocess.run(cmd, …, timeout=_FFMPEG_TIMEOUT_S)` at :92 with `_FFMPEG_TIMEOUT_S = 120`. `grep -c "CheckerDep|CurrentSubject" services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/media.py` → 0. Reachable at the edge via gateway row `("/api/explorer", "/api", *viewer)` behind the Ingress `/api` rule. Distinct from open_python-audit VS-03 (missing FGA gate on this and 24 other routes) and VS-09 (Host-header SSRF into ffmpeg on this same route): neither reasons about cost, the global lock, or threadpool exhaustion.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Every element reproduces at HEAD. `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/media.py:263-272`: `@router.get("/media-clip/{doc_id}")` / `def media_clip(doc_id: str, request: Request, state: StateDep, lo: Annotated[float, Query(ge=0)], hi: Annotated[float, Query(gt=0)], dataset: DatasetParam = None) -&gt; FileResponse:` — plain `def` (so the async/sync trap does NOT apply; the finding's mechanism is threadpool exhaustion, not loop blocking) and no `CheckerDep`/`CurrentSubject`. `services/viewer/src/viewer/services/clips.py:30` `MAX_CLIP_S = 660.0`; :33 `_FFMPEG_TIMEOUT_S = 120`; :34-36 `# One build at a time: clips are seconds-cheap and the box's CPU belongs to / # the model servers; serializing also makes the tmp+rename publish race-free.` / `_build_lock = threading.Lock()` — a module-level lock, and the comment is a recorded decision the finding correctly engages with rather than reports; :63 `with _build_lock:` wraps `subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=_FFMPEG_TIMEOUT_S)` at :92, with cmd carrying `-c:v libx264 -preset veryfast -crf 23 -c:a libmp3lame`. Cache-key claim reproduces: :43 `return CACHE_DIR / f"{cache_key}_{int(lo * 1000)}_{int(hi * 1000)}.mp4"` — millisecond granularity, so `?hi=1.001,1.002,…` misses every time; `_MAX_CACHED = 50` with `evict_old_clips` stat-sorting the directory. Silent-green probes verified: probes.py:45-47 `async def livez()` with the comment "async, not sync def: the probe must run ON the event loop, never queued behind the blocking data-plane threadpool". Single replica verified: chart/templates/explorer.yaml:70 `replicas: {{ (hasKey $svc "replicas") | ternary $svc.replicas 1 }}` and chart/values.yaml:1273-1275 declares viewer/search/annotator with no `replicas` key → 1. Reachable: router mounted `APIRouter(prefix="/api", tags=["media"])` (media.py:38) + gateway row `("/api/explorer", "/api", *viewer)` (gateway/__init__.py:142) + `/api` Prefix at ingress.yaml:66, so `/api/explorer/media-clip/{doc_id}`. Genuinely novel vs the audit: VS-03 (media.py:283/:161, the missing FGA gate on 25 routes) and VS-09 (media.py:276, Host-header SSRF into ffmpeg) name this same file but neither reasons about cost, the global lock, or threadpool exhaustion. One caveat that does not change the verdict: the attacker needs a doc_id that survives `rowid_for_doc(ds, declared.identity.doc_key, doc_id)` at media.py:288 — trivially obtainable from the ungated listing routes VS-03 already documents.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~Nothing pages on any HTTP surface: `rules.yml` has no `http.server` rule and no group for 8 of the 14 apps, and ingest's and flows' first-party counters are read by no rule at all~~</b> <i>(observability.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26 — `1da9489e`.** Three groups: `lance-http` (5xx RATIO per service — a count is
> meaningless without traffic — plus p95 and a bare `absent()` so neither can go silently blind),
> `lance-ingest` and `lance-flows`. Series names VERIFIED against the live store, not inferred: it is
> `http_server_duration_milliseconds_*`, because the OTLP→Prometheus exporter appends the unit and a
> rule naming `_seconds` can never fire. Every expression was replayed against GreptimeDB before being
> written — `histogram_quantile` included. Thresholds measured, not guessed (p95: lineage 500 ms,
> notifications 463 ms, maintenance 10 s by design → 15 s).
>
> The structural half is what stops recurrence:
> `test_every_FIRST_PARTY_INSTRUMENT_is_read_by_some_alert_rule` asserts the REVERSE direction the
> estate lacked — every first-party metrics module must be read by an alert group. `ingest/metrics.py`
> stated this gap in its own docstring and that sentence was true for as long as the file existed.
>
> Stated rather than hidden: no `flows_*` series exists yet (flows has never executed a run, and a
> counter that never incremented creates no table), so that rule is evaluable and unexercised.

**Rule.** observability.md: § Quick checklist item 6 / the reference's premise that the FastAPI instrumentor's RED metrics are the operator-facing signal — emitting them and never alerting on them leaves the estate unpageable

**Sites:** `chart/alerting/rules.yml:8-596`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/metrics.py:1-14`, `services/flows/src/flows/metrics.py:24-36`, `tests/unit/test_invariants.py:4046-4083`

**Why it matters.** `grep -n 'http_server\|http\.server\|ingest_\|flows_\|gateway\|compute_\|controlplane' chart/alerting/rules.yml` returns ZERO hits. Every one of the fourteen apps emits `http.server.*` request-count and duration series automatically (setup_otel wires the meter provider; the launcher plane gets the same via `OTEL_METRICS_EXPORTER=otlp`), the chart provisions a Perses "Fleet — RED" dashboard from them — and no rule turns any of it into a page. There is no alert group for gateway, compute, controlplane, viewer, search, annotator, ingest or flows: eight of fourteen apps, including the edge and every app a browser talks to. Worse, `ingest.runs`/`ingest.units` and `flows.runs`/`flows.nodes` were added specifically because Dapr's own workflow families report `status="success"` for a run that returned FAILED — and no rule reads them, so the fact they exist to carry still reaches nobody. The invariant suite cannot catch this: `test_every_ALERT_GROUP_is_either_first_party_or_declared_third_party` checks group→instrument only, never instrument→group.

**Fix.** Add a fleet-wide RED group to `chart/alerting/rules.yml` on the instrumentor's own series (5xx ratio and p95 duration per `service_name`, with the probe paths already excluded upstream by `OTEL_PYTHON_FASTAPI_EXCLUDED_URLS`), plus `lance-ingest` and `lance-flows` groups on `ingest_runs_total{lance_ingest_status="FAILED"}` and `flows_nodes_total{lance_flows_status="failed"}`. Then add the reverse invariant to `tests/unit/test_invariants.py`: every first-party metrics module must be named by at least one alert group, so a new instrument cannot land unpageable.

**Evidence.**

```
services/ingest/src/ingest/metrics.py:3-5 states the gap in its own docstring: "`chart/alerting/rules.yml` duly contains zero ingest rules: no page fires for a run that failed, a fan-out that stalled, or units that never landed." Concrete failure: the catalog begins returning 500 on every `POST /v1/table/{id}/describe` (an FGA outage, a bad namespace binding). The only rule in the `lance-catalog` group is `CatalogWritesShedSustained` on `catalog_writes_shed_total` — which counts 429s on Arrow-IPC WRITES and stays at zero throughout. No alert fires, on any surface, for a catalog that is 100% failing reads.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** `grep -n 'http_server\|http\.server\|ingest_\|flows_\|gateway\|compute_\|controlplane' chart/alerting/rules.yml` returns ZERO hits at HEAD. The seven groups are lance-lineage (:8), lance-catalog (:52), lance-medallion (:68), lance-infra (:114), lance-notifications (:140), lance-maintenance (:243), dapr-control-plane (:309), ray (:434), lance-observability (:527) — none for gateway, compute, controlplane, viewer, search, annotator, ingest or flows. The first-party counters are real and unread: services/flows/src/flows/metrics.py:26 `_runs = _meter.create_counter("flows.runs", ...)` and :31 `_nodes = _meter.create_counter("flows.nodes", unit="{node}", description="Flow nodes by terminal status — the service's only failure signal.")`, with no `flows_` string anywhere in rules.yml. ingest/metrics.py:3-5 states the gap in its own docstring verbatim: "`chart/alerting/rules.yml` duly contains zero ingest rules: no page fires for a run that failed, a fan-out that stalled, or units that never landed." The invariant blind spot is exactly as claimed: tests/unit/test_invariants.py:4025 `test_every_ALERT_GROUP_is_either_first_party_or_declared_third_party` compares `groups - _ALERT_GROUP_SOURCES - _THIRD_PARTY_ALERT_GROUPS` (group→instrument only) and :4049 parametrizes over `sorted(_ALERT_GROUP_SOURCES)` — neither direction asserts an instrument has a rule. High is right here and not for the other observability rows: this is the failure-detection chain for the whole estate, the chart already ships vmalert + Alertmanager for it, and the catalog scenario is concrete — the only lance-catalog rule is `sum(rate(catalog_writes_shed_total[5m])) &gt; 0`, a counter of 429s on Arrow-IPC WRITES, which stays at zero while every read 500s. Not filed in open_python-audit.md: its E10 observability epic covers log allow-lists, dropped lineage events and stale producer URIs — nothing about rules.yml coverage.

</details>

### Medium — 25

<details><summary><b>~~`GET /promotions/{instance_id}` returns a held promotion's contents to an unauthenticated caller — the sibling decision write refuses exactly that caller~~</b> <i>(authn.md + authz.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26 — ALREADY FIXED.** `promotions.py:284-304` now reads `gate = _fga_gate(request)` / `if gate is not None:` and raises on an empty subject; pinned by `services/medallion/tests/test_promotion_read_is_gated.py`.

**Rule.** authz.md § Service-layer integration (a single-object GET takes a route dep `require_permission(...)`); the read/write asymmetry pattern — a write gated, the equivalent read open

**Sites:** `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/promotions.py:263`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/promotions.py:251`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:161`, `services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py:157`

**Why it matters.** Real fail-open: `show()` skips the `can_promote` gate for a caller that presents no bearer, so stripping the Authorization header defeats the check that an authenticated non-holder would fail. Fix is `if gate is not None:` with `subject or ""` refused, mirroring `decide_promotion`. But the door is not enumerable — the instance id is `promotion-&lt;uuid4().hex[:12]&gt;` with no list route — and the disclosure is promotion metadata (project, source/destination dataset, quality-gate reasons, deadline), so it grades med rather than high.

**Fix.** Make the read take the same door as the write: refuse `subject is None` before touching the workflow client, and make the FGA gate unconditional once FGA is on — `if gate is not None: await gate(...)`, with the empty-subject case raising rather than silently passing. Better, hoist both onto the router: `router = APIRouter(tags=["promotions"], dependencies=[Depends(authenticate_subject_or_401)])` so the next promotion route cannot be added with the conditional. Keep AUTHZ-BEFORE-EXISTENCE — return the same 404 the write path uses rather than a 403 that confirms the instance id.

**Evidence.**

```
promotions.py:251-264 `@router.get("/promotions/{instance_id}")` / `async def show(... subject: Annotated[str | None, Depends(authenticate_subject)])` … `gate = _fga_gate(request)` / `if gate is not None and subject:` / `await gate(subject=subject, obj=promotion_object(spec))`. Failure: `curl https://<edge>/api/promotions/promotion-<run-token>` with no Authorization header returns 200 including `reasons` — the quality-gate findings on another tenant's silver→gold promotion — while `POST /api/promotions/promotion-<run-token>/decision` on the identical object answers 403.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The mechanism is real and reproduced. `promotions.py:251-264`: `@router.get("/promotions/{instance_id}")` / `async def show(... subject: Annotated[str | None, Depends(authenticate_subject)])`, then `gate = _fga_gate(request)` / `if gate is not None and subject:` — so with no Authorization header `authenticate_subject` returns `None` at `produce_auth.py:161-162` (`if not authorization: return None`) and the `can_promote` check is skipped entirely; the full `PromotionUnderReview` (project, from_dataset, to_dataset, `reasons`, approval_hours) is returned. The asymmetry is exactly as claimed: `decide_promotion` at `promotions.py:186-190` raises `PermissionDeniedError("a promotion decision must name the person who made it; sign in and retry")` on an empty subject. Reachability confirmed end to end: the router is mounted (`producer.py:175 app.include_router(promotions_router)`), the gateway carries `("/api/promotions", "/promotions", *medallion)` (gateway/__init__.py:162), the chart Ingress routes `- path: /api` to the fleet gateway (chart/templates/ingress.yaml:66-72), and the gateway performs no authn (its only middleware is the lineage sidecar-route 403 blocklist at :290-299). Not filed in open_python-audit.md — the promotion plane appears nowhere in it. ADJUSTED on grade and on one reachability claim: the id is not guessable at will. `instance_for` is `f"promotion-{token}"` and the token is `uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]` (services/produce.py:75, media_produce.py:109, train.py:144) unless a produce caller supplied their own idempotency key, and there is no list route — so this is not enumerable, and the leak is promotion METADATA (dataset names + failed-assertion strings), not corpus data. That makes it a credential-omission fail-open on a governed read whose exposure is capability-gated by a 48-bit token that does circulate in lineage events and inbox notifications.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~The annotator's interactive AI-assist plane is three routes with no subject, no checker and no catalog delegation — it spends model compute for any caller who can reach the pod~~</b> <i>(authn.md + authz.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26.** The router has a door: `APIRouter(prefix="/api", tags=["assist"], dependencies=[Depends(require_assist)])`, which resolves a verified `CurrentSubject`, takes the existing `CheckerDep`, and checks `can_write_data` on the unit's corpus table — the write rung, because a prediction is a proposed write that queues reviewer work. All three routes, including the two GETs whose disclosure is model-backend topology and a task's ontology. Fails CLOSED on a descriptor with no documents binding, and writes the audit row the plane never had. The dataset comes off the query string so one door covers the group; `dataset_handle` runs in the threadpool as the handler's own call does. `corpus_object`/`table_object` moved from `viewer/api/security.py` to `service_kit/media/authz.py` rather than being written a second time — the viewer's own docstring warned against exactly that. Pinned by `tests/unit/test_assist_plane_is_gated.py` (5 tests, all RED before).

**Rule.** authn.md § Protected routes: apply at the router level when every route in the group needs it — the stance `flows/security.py` already states for `POST /flows/runs`

**Sites:** `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/assist.py:295`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/assist.py:210`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/assist.py:288`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/assist.py:34`

**Why it matters.** Confirmed ungated at the service: assist's three routes take no verified subject, no FGA checker and no forwarded bearer, while projects/members/tasks/project_events beside them all use `annotator/api/security.py`. Correct the exposure: `/api/assist` has NO gateway row and the browser path is gated by `signedOut()` in `assist.remote.ts:60`, so this is reachable from in-namespace pods and port-forwards, not from the public edge — the flows parallel ('the moment the gateway carried /api/flows to a browser') does not apply here.

**Fix.** Gate the router, not the handlers: `router = APIRouter(prefix="/api", tags=["assist"], dependencies=[Depends(_deps.current_subject), Depends(require_assist)])`, where `require_assist` checks the corpus rung the annotations plane is governed by (`can_write_data` on the unit's table — an assist prediction is a proposed write) via the existing `CheckerDep`. `GET /assist/producers` and `GET /assist/generation-schema` disclose the estate's model-backend topology and a task's ontology, so they belong behind the same authenticated floor even if a looser relation is chosen for them.

**Evidence.**

```
assist.py:34 `router = APIRouter(prefix="/api", tags=["assist"])` — no `dependencies=`; assist.py:295-303 `@router.post("/assist/{doc_id}/{speech_id}/{chunk_id}")` / `async def assist(state: StateDep, doc_id: str, speech_id: int, chunk_id: int, body: AssistRequest, dataset: DatasetParam = None) -> AssistResult:` — the full signature, with no `CurrentSubject`, no `CheckerDep` and no `RawBearerToken`. `grep -n 'CurrentSubject\|CheckerDep' assist.py` returns nothing. Failure: from any in-namespace pod (including a Ray worker running a sealed runner's code), `POST http://<release>-annotator:8103/api/assist/<any-doc>/0/0` with `{"producer":"...","prompt":"..."}` reads that corpus unit and drives the model backend, with no tuple checked and no audit row written.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The code claim is exact. `assist.py:34` `router = APIRouter(prefix="/api", tags=["assist"])` with no `dependencies=`; `assist.py:288-296` `@router.post("/assist/{doc_id}/{speech_id}/{chunk_id}")` / `async def assist(state: StateDep, doc_id: str, speech_id: int, chunk_id: int, body: AssistRequest, dataset: DatasetParam = None) -&gt; AssistResult:`; `grep -n 'CurrentSubject\|CheckerDep\|RawBearerToken' assist.py` returns nothing, and the module's imports carry no `annotator.api.security` at all. It makes no catalog call — `dataset_handle` resolves locally and `_remote` POSTs to `backend_for(state.settings, body.producer, ...)` — so unlike the annotations routes there is no delegated gate either. The flows precedent is quoted verbatim and correct (`services/flows/src/flows/security.py:3-7`). What does NOT hold is the exposure half of the argument. There is no gateway row for `/api/assist`: the gateway's annotator row is `("/api/explorer/annotations", "/api/annotations", *annotator)` only, and the zone's own catch-all BFF (`routes/api/[...path]/+server.ts`) exports GET only and proxies the VIEWER, not the annotator. The browser reaches assist through a remote command that gates first — `lib/viewer/remote/assist.remote.ts:60` `if (signedOut()) return SIGN_IN_REQUIRED;`. So unlike flows, the edge never carried this to a browser; the reachable callers are in-namespace pods (chart/templates/network-policy.yaml grants plain intra-namespace ingress to non-store pods) and anyone port-forwarding. That keeps it a real defence-in-depth gap on a GPU-spending, corpus-reading route where every sibling router has a door, which is med — but the claim should not be sold as the flows situation.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~`POST /tasks/{task_id}/import` decodes a caller-supplied Arrow IPC payload and validates every row through Pydantic inline on the event loop~~</b> <i>(core-conventions.md + anti-patterns.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26.** `shapes, links = await run_in_threadpool(shapes_from_ipc, payload, ontology=ontology, taken_ids=taken)`. The route stays `async def` — it must, it awaits four actor round-trips — and the synchronous decode moves off the loop, the same shape `assist.py` and `project_events.py` already use. The Fix's pairing with a body cap is already satisfied: the annotator builds through `service_kit.media.middleware`, which now registers `BodySizeLimitMiddleware` at 32 MiB (verified on the built app), so the threadpool worker is bounded too. Pinned by `tests/unit/test_import_does_not_stall_the_loop.py`, which measures ELAPSED time against a concurrent ticker rather than counting ticks — pre-fix it recorded 0.50 s for a 0.30 s decode beside a 0.20 s ticker (serialized); post-fix, 0.30 s (concurrent).

**Rule.** core-conventions.md § Async vs sync path operations: "Use `async def` only when the body is genuinely async-compatible… Never run blocking code inside an `async def` — it works but kills throughput."

**Sites:** `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/tasks.py:452`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/tasks.py:402`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/projects/imports.py:82`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/projects/imports.py:143`

**Why it matters.** `import_annotations` is `async def` (correctly — it awaits four actor round-trips), but line 452 calls `shapes_from_ipc(payload, ontology=ontology, taken_ids=taken)` inline. That function is fully synchronous CPU work over the whole request body: `pa.ipc.open_stream(pa.BufferReader(payload)).read_all()` (imports.py:143, with a second `open_file` attempt on ArrowInvalid — so a file-framed payload is parsed twice), then `table.to_pylist()` materialising every row as a Python dict, then per-row `_shape()` + `_links()` Pydantic construction, then a second pass building `ShapeLike.model_validate(s.model_dump(mode="json"))` for every shape to run the ontology check. None of it yields. The payload is unbounded — `payload = await request.body()` with no cap, which is separately filed as ANN-12 (that finding is about MEMORY; this one is about the loop) — so the stall scales with whatever a client uploads, and the annotator process has one event loop serving the whole zone. While it runs, every other in-flight annotator request and the mounted `/livez`/`/readyz` probes are frozen. The intended shape is visible one directory over: the sibling Arrow-IPC route `services/annotator/src/annotator/annotations/wire.py:39` is a plain `def`, so FastAPI threadpools its identical pyarrow work for free. `import_annotations` cannot simply become `def` (it has actor awaits), which is exactly the case the reference's threadpool escape hatch exists for — and the same file's neighbours already use it (assist.py:307, project_events.py:460, both added by the ANN-01 fix).

**Fix.** `shapes, links = await run_in_threadpool(shapes_from_ipc, payload, ontology=ontology, taken_ids=taken)` at tasks.py:452 — `starlette.concurrency.run_in_threadpool` is already imported in this service's sibling modules. Pair it with ANN-12's body cap so the threadpool worker is bounded too; a threadpooled decode of an unbounded payload stops stalling the loop but still burns a worker and the pod's memory.

**Evidence.**

```
services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/tasks.py:401-402 `@router.post("/{task_id}/import", status_code=status.HTTP_200_OK)` / `async def import_annotations(task_id: TaskId, request: Request, checker: CheckerDep, subject: CurrentSubject) -> dict[str, Any]:`; :451-452 `payload = await request.body()` / `shapes, links = shapes_from_ipc(payload, ontology=ontology, taken_ids=taken)` — no run_in_threadpool on either line.  services/annotator/src/annotator/projects/imports.py:82 `def shapes_from_ipc(` (plain def); :99 `for index, row in enumerate(table.to_pylist()):`; :114 `lookalikes = [ShapeLike.model_validate(s.model_dump(mode="json")) for s in shapes]`; :143 `return pa.ipc.open_stream(pa.BufferReader(payload)).read_all()` with the `open_file` retry at :147.  Sibling doing it right: services/annotator/src/annotator/annotations/wire.py:38-39 `@router.get("/annotations/{doc_id}/{speech_id}/{chunk_id}")` / `def annotations(`.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Reproduced (line numbers off by one: the route is at 399/400, not 401/402). `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/tasks.py:399` `@router.post("/{task_id}/import", status_code=status.HTTP_200_OK)` / :400 `async def import_annotations(task_id: TaskId, request: Request, checker: CheckerDep, subject: CurrentSubject) -&gt; dict[str, Any]:` — genuinely `async def`, and it must be (it awaits `actor.get()`, `_authorize`, `_verified_project_state`, `actor.get_draft()`). :451-452 `    payload = await request.body()` / `    shapes, links = shapes_from_ipc(payload, ontology=ontology, taken_ids=taken)` — no threadpool on either line. The callee is fully synchronous CPU work over the whole body: imports.py:82 `def shapes_from_ipc(` (plain def, so nothing threadpools it here), :95 `    table = _read_table(payload)` → :143 `        return pa.ipc.open_stream(pa.BufferReader(payload)).read_all()` with the `open_file` retry at :147 (a file-framed payload is parsed twice), :99 `    for index, row in enumerate(table.to_pylist()):` with per-row `_shape` + `_links`, and :114 `        lookalikes = [ShapeLike.model_validate(s.model_dump(mode="json")) for s in shapes]` — a second full pass. The sanctioned shape is in this exact file's neighbours and post-dates the ANN-01 fix: assist.py:308 `    handle = await run_in_threadpool(dataset_handle, state, dataset)`, assist.py:317, project_events.py:460 `    await run_in_threadpool(_refuse_unknown_datasets, state, payload)`. The sibling Arrow route wire.py:39 `def annotations(` is plain def and correctly threadpooled. DE-DUP: ANN-01's sites are assist.py:217 and project_events.py:332/340/408 — this route is NOT among them. ANN-12 (med) names the same file+function but for MEMORY ('Arrow-IPC import reads and materializes an unbounded request body', sites tasks.py:335, imports.py:144 at old numbering); the loop-blocking mechanism is distinct and uncovered, though the two share a root fix. med is right: the stall is attacker-scalable via an uncapped body and freezes the pod's probes.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~/docs and /openapi.json are on in production for every served app, and the public front door serves an unauthenticated aggregated Swagger of the whole estate at /api/docs~~</b> <i>(dependencies.md + production-patterns.md + observability.md + kubernetes.md + microservices.md + rate-limiting.md + websockets.md + cache.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26.** Docs are opt-IN estate-wide. All 14 entrypoints now answer `openapi_url=docs_url=redoc_url=None` with no flag set — `make_service_app` (which had hard-coded all three on), the four bare constructors (gateway, viewer, search, annotator, the last three via a new `MEDIA_DOCS`), and the four that carried a `docs_enabled` flag defaulting to True, now False. The gateway's two catch-all branches — the sharp end, since `ingress.yaml` publishes `/api` — 404 behind `RASK_DOCS` rather than serving the merged schema and its sequential fan-out to an anonymous caller. One chart knob (`docs.enabled`, off) renders all six env names; `values-local.yaml` turns it on for local k3s. Pinned by `tests/unit/test_docs_are_closed_by_default.py` (14 apps, one clean subprocess each), `services/gateway/tests/test_docs_are_gated.py` (6, incl. the flag actually opening them), and `test_invariants.py::test_the_chart_TURNS_DOCS_OFF_by_default_and_ON_when_asked`.

**Rule.** production-patterns.md § Hiding docs in production: "if settings.ENVIRONMENT not in SHOW_DOCS_IN: app_kwargs['openapi_url'] = None  # disables /docs, /redoc, /openapi.json" — and "Auth-protect /docs instead of hiding it only if internal consumers need it in prod"

**Sites:** `services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py:325`, `services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py:327`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/main.py:166`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/main.py:95`, `services/search/src/search/main.py:62`, `chart/values.yaml:67`, `chart/templates/ingress.yaml:66`

**Why it matters.** Every factual claim reproduces, and the gateway half is genuinely UNFILED — X9 (open_python-audit.md:210/:1658) files only `make_service_app`, MAINT-14 (:214) only maintenance's flag, and GW-OPENAPI-SEQUENTIAL (:794) files the ~100 s fan-out latency but says nothing about it being an unauthenticated public path. X9's premise really is wrong at HEAD (annotator, viewer and search gate nothing). But `high` overstates the impact: what leaks is a route table and request/response schemas, not data and not credentials, and every documented route stays individually auth-gated (OIDC verifier + FGA, or the sidecar app-token). This is information disclosure plus a cheap amplification lever at the edge — med, matching the grade X9 and GW-OPENAPI-SEQUENTIAL already carry for their halves. The correct fix travels with the code: gate the gateway's two branches, and give annotator/viewer/search the `docs_url=... if _settings.docs_enabled else None` shape catalog/lineage/medallion/maintenance already have, then actually set the flags in the chart.

**Fix.** Gate the gateway's two branches first, since it is the only publicly-routed one: read a `RASK_DOCS` flag and return 404 for both `{prefix}/docs` and `{prefix}/openapi.json` when it is off, or put them behind the OIDC bearer the rest of the estate already verifies. Pass `docs_url=None, redoc_url=None, openapi_url=None` on the gateway/annotator/viewer/search `FastAPI(...)` calls under the same flag. Then flip the default: `docs_enabled: bool = Field(default=False, ...)` in catalog/lineage/medallion/maintenance configs and `make_service_app`, with the chart's dev values turning it on — a security default that every deployment must remember to disable is one nobody disables. A `tests/unit/test_invariants.py` case asserting `app.openapi_url is None` for every entrypoint under a prod-shaped env keeps it from drifting back.

**Evidence.**

```
services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py:325-327: `if request.url.path == f"{prefix}/docs":` / `return get_swagger_ui_html(openapi_url=f"{prefix}/openapi.json", title="rask API (gateway)")` — no token check, no dependency, and the preceding branch at :323 serves `await _merged_openapi(...)` the same way. Verified at HEAD by import: `annotator docs_url=/docs openapi_url=/openapi.json redoc=/redoc`, and identically for search, viewer and gateway. `chart/values.yaml:67` → `RASK_API_PREFIX: "/api"`; `chart/templates/ingress.yaml:66-72` → `- path: /api` / `pathType: Prefix` / backend `{{ include "rask.fullname" $ }}-gateway`. Failure condition: an unauthenticated `GET https://<release-host>/api/openapi.json` returns the merged route table, request/response schemas and parameter names of every backend the gateway fronts, and costs the gateway ten sequential upstream fetches at 10 s timeout each to produce.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py:322-327, at the very top of the `@app.api_route("/api/{path:path}", ...)` catch-all and before `_pick_route`: `if request.url.path == f"{prefix}/openapi.json":` / `return JSONResponse(await _merged_openapi(client, prefix, _distinct_targets(request.app.state.routes)))` / `if request.url.path == f"{prefix}/docs":` / `return get_swagger_ui_html(openapi_url=f"{prefix}/openapi.json", title="rask API (gateway)")` — no dependency, no token check. The gateway's only middleware is `@app.middleware("http") async def lineage_sidecar_guard` at :288, which prefix-matches `/api/lineage/&lt;sidecar-route&gt;` only; there is no `register_middleware` and no auth. Reachability confirmed: chart/values.yaml:67 `RASK_API_PREFIX: "/api"` and chart/templates/ingress.yaml:69-75 `- path: /api` / `pathType: Prefix` / backend `{{ include "rask.fullname" $ }}-gateway`. The 14-of-14 count reproduces: `grep -rn DOCS chart/ .docker/ scripts/` returns only `scripts/gen_openapi.py:31 _DOCS = _ROOT / "docs"` — no deployment path sets `LANCE_REST_DOCS`/`LINEAGE_DOCS`/`MEDALLION_DOCS`/`MAINTENANCE_DOCS`, and all four default `True` (catalog/core/config.py:36, lineage/core/config.py:184, maintenance/core/config.py:38, medallion/core/config.py:59). The three ungated apps are confirmed by their constructors: annotator/main.py:166 `app = FastAPI(title="lance-media annotator", lifespan=lifespan)`, viewer/main.py:95 and search/main.py:62 identically — no `docs_url`, no `openapi_url`, so FastAPI's `/docs` + `/openapi.json` + `/redoc` defaults stand.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~Four fleet apps install no catch-all, so any unhandled exception answers text/plain — a third error envelope no client can parse~~</b> <i>(exception-handlers.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26.** `register_handlers` installs an `Exception` catch-all — a fixed problem+json 500 with the traceback going to `log.exception` and nothing of the exception on the wire — so every app in the estate now shares one error envelope. And `make_service_app` also installs `install_problem_handlers`, so the four factory-only apps (compute, controlplane, flows, notifications) can map the `lance_namespace` errors their own governed kernel raises: an FGA outage now answers **503**, honouring the contract `governed/fga.py` documents, instead of a text/plain 500. Option B of the taxonomy question, chosen on precedent rather than preference — `ingest/__init__.py` already did exactly this on top of the factory and explains why in a comment, and doing it in the FACTORY also settles `open_python-audit` X11 (ingest's 422 differing from its three fleet siblings) by making all five one shape rather than by removing what made ingest right. Pinned by `packages/service-kit/tests/test_catch_all_handler.py` (5 tests, 4 RED before). **Two GAP tests in `test_adversarial_inbox.py` were rewritten, not deleted**: both named this missing catch-all as their cause, and both are now HALF closed — the envelope is guaranteed, the STATUS is not. An unusable subject and a state-store outage each still answer 500 where they should answer a named 4xx and a 503; those halves stay pinned under their original names with the residual spelled out.

**Rule.** exception-handlers.md: "One handler per exception class, registered in main.py" + "media_type=application/problem+json on every Problem-shaped response"

**Sites:** `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/__init__.py:144`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/exceptions.py:104`, `services/compute/src/compute/main.py`, `services/controlplane/src/controlplane/routes.py`, `services/flows/src/flows/routes.py`, `services/notifications/src/notifications/api/visibility.py:121`

**Why it matters.** Real and reproduced, but the load-bearing half is narrower than "a third envelope". Starlette's text/plain 500 is the framework default for ANY app with no `Exception` handler (the gateway is the same), so that half is not a rask-specific choice. What IS a rask defect is that the four `make_service_app`-only apps cannot map `lance_namespace` errors at all, and the shared kernel they call raises them: `governed/fga.py:507` `raise ServiceUnavailableError("authorization service unavailable")` with the docstring at :494 asserting "outage → ServiceUnavailableError → 503". In notifications that contract silently does not hold. It still fails CLOSED (refusal happens, nothing is disclosed, no door opens), so this is a misreported outage/mis-shaped envelope, not a high-severity exposure.
