The fix is one line per app (`install_problem_handlers`) — or, better, the same fix the three media apps already applied for the identical reason (viewer/main.py:96-101, annotator/main.py:167-177).

**Fix.** Register the `Exception` catch-all in `register_handlers` itself, mirroring `ns_errors.handle_unexpected_error` — `log.exception` the traceback, return a fixed `{type,title,status,detail:"Internal Server Error"}` problem+json 500. Separately, decide the taxonomy question SKG-05 raises at the library: either have `governed/fga.py`/`oidc.py`/`user_state.py` raise `service_kit.exceptions` (which every app can map, since `install_problem_handlers` sits beside `register_handlers` in the four dual-install apps anyway), or have `make_service_app` also install `install_problem_handlers` the way `ingest/__init__.py:77` already does.

**Evidence.**

```
packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/__init__.py:144-145 `register_handlers(app)` / `register_middleware(app, settings)` — no `install_problem_handlers`, no `add_exception_handler(Exception, ...)`. Reproduced against a real app built the same way: `A /boom 500 'text/plain; charset=utf-8' 'Internal Server Error'` and, raising `lance_namespace.UnauthenticatedError`, `A /ns 500 'text/plain; charset=utf-8' 'Internal Server Error'`. The same two routes on an `install_problem_handlers` app return `500 'application/problem+json' {"type":"https://lance.org/problems/internal",...}`.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** CONFIRMED at HEAD. `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/__init__.py:144-145` is exactly `register_handlers(app)` / `register_middleware(app, settings)` — no `Exception` handler, no `install_problem_handlers`. `exceptions.py:104` `def register_handlers(app: FastAPI)` registers exactly two: `:111 @app.exception_handler(DomainError)` and `:121 @app.exception_handler(RequestValidationError)`. The four apps named are correct — `services/compute/src/compute/__init__.py:26`, `services/controlplane/src/controlplane/__init__.py:8`, `services/flows/src/flows/__init__.py:18`, `services/notifications/src/notifications/__init__.py:53` all call `make_service_app` and none calls `install_problem_handlers` (grep over services/*/src confirms only annotator, catalog, ingest, lineage, maintenance, medallion×2, search, viewer do). Reproduced myself: a bare app + `register_handlers`, `raise RuntimeError` → `A 500 'text/plain; charset=utf-8' 'Internal Server Error'`; the same app raising `lance_namespace.ServiceUnavailableError` → `fga-outage: 500 text/plain; charset=utf-8 'Internal Server Error'`. The notifications reach is real: `api/visibility.py:121 allowed = await fga.batch_check(` and `governed/fga.py:39 from lance_namespace import ServiceUnavailableError`, raised fail-closed at `:507`. Note the same function's OTHER refusal (`visibility.py:118 raise ServiceUnavailableError(...)`, imported from `service_kit.exceptions`) IS handled and returns 503 problem+json — so one function answers the same class of failure two different ways. Not filed in open_python-audit.md (grep found no finding on a missing catch-all in the make_service_app apps; X11 is the dual-install 422 shape, not this).

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~viewer's page resolver reports a catalog or object-store OUTAGE as 404 Not Found, with the raw httpx/lance exception text in the client-visible detail~~</b> <i>(exception-handlers.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26.** Both branches raise `ServiceUnavailableError` (503) instead of `NotFoundError`: an `httpx.RequestError` resolving the table, and any driver failure opening the dataset. A 404 was terminal — the zone rendered "page not found" and nothing retried a condition that clears on its own — and the old message even said "catalog unreachable" while the class said the opposite. The details are now stable sentences naming the TABLE only; the httpx/lance text and the `s3://` location go to `logger.exception`. That second half was only reachable because of the first: `_problem` writes `str(exc)` into the body with no redaction at any status, and `ns_errors`' 5xx redaction never applied while the class chosen was a 4xx. The two cases that genuinely mean absent — a non-401/403 4xx from the catalog, and a describe with no `location` — stay 404, and are pinned so they cannot drift into a retryable status. Pinned by `services/viewer/tests/test_page_resolver_outages.py` (5 tests, 3 RED before).

**Rule.** exception-handlers.md: the hierarchy exists so each class maps to its own status (`ExternalServiceError` → 502, not `NotFoundError` → 404); and "Never include exception internals in the response body — those leak via logs only"

**Sites:** `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/pages.py:87`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/pages.py:110`

**Why it matters.** `_resolve` catches `httpx.RequestError` — connection refused, DNS failure, TLS error, timeout — and raises `NotFoundError`, a 404. `_open` catches bare `Exception` around `lance.dataset(location, storage_options=…)` and does the same, so a RustFS outage, expired vended credentials, or a corrupt manifest all report as 404. A 404 is terminal: the zone renders "page not found", the reader stops, and nothing retries — for a condition that is transient and would clear on its own. The file itself argues this exact point twelve lines below, refusing to launder 401/403 into "unknown table" because "the annotator lost real debugging time to exactly this laundering"; the same reasoning applies to the outage cases and was not applied. Both messages also interpolate the caught exception straight into `detail`, and `service_kit.exceptions._problem` puts `str(exc)` verbatim into the client body — so the browser receives the httpx connect error (internal host/port) or the lance/S3 driver message (bucket path, credential state). `ns_errors` deliberately redacts every 5xx detail for this reason; a 4xx keeps its message, so mislabelling the outage as 4xx is also what makes the leak reachable.

**Fix.** Raise `ServiceUnavailableError` (503, or 502 if a `BadGatewayError` is added) for `httpx.RequestError` and for a failed `lance.dataset` open, keeping 404 for the two cases that genuinely mean "absent": the catalog answering 4xx-not-401/403, and a describe with no `location`. Make the detail a stable sentence naming the table only, and move the exception into `log.exception` — the handler already logs 5xx with the traceback.

**Evidence.**

```
services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/pages.py:86-87 `except httpx.RequestError as exc:` / `raise NotFoundError(f"catalog unreachable while resolving {table!r}: {exc}") from exc`; :108-110 `except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — surface the cause, never a bare 500` / `raise NotFoundError(f"table {table!r} resolves to {location!r}, which is unreadable: {exc}") from exc`. The message text ("catalog unreachable") states the condition is an outage while the class chosen states it is a missing resource.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Verified verbatim at HEAD. `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/pages.py:86-87`: `except httpx.RequestError as exc:` / `raise NotFoundError(f"catalog unreachable while resolving {table!r}: {exc}") from exc` — `httpx.RequestError` is the base of ConnectError/ConnectTimeout/ReadTimeout/DNS failures, so every transient catalog outage is answered 404. `:108-110`: `return lance.dataset(location, storage_options=state.settings.storage_options)` / `except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — surface the cause, never a bare 500` / `raise NotFoundError(f"table {table!r} resolves to {location!r}, which is unreadable: {exc}")` — a RustFS outage, expired vended credentials or a corrupt manifest all report 404. The finding's own strongest support is on the page: :90-93 refuses to launder 401/403 into "unknown table" because "the annotator lost real debugging time to exactly this laundering", and 401 is correctly kept as `UnauthorizedError` at :95 — the same reasoning was simply not extended two lines up. The leak is reachable and violates a rule the estate states in its own words: `service_kit/exceptions.py` module docstring, "Keep the message STABLE; never interpolate a raw upstream exception into it (log that instead)", and `_problem()` at :96-102 does `"detail": str(exc)` with NO redaction at any status — so a browser receives the interpolated `s3://…` location and the httpx/lance driver text. (viewer installs both handler sets — main.py:96 `register_handlers(app)`, :101 `install_problem_handlers(app, logger)` — and `NotFoundError` is a `DomainError`, so the unredacted `register_handlers` path is the one that serves it; ns_errors' 5xx redaction at :98 never applies because the class chosen is a 4xx.) Not filed: open_python-audit.md's pages.py entries are VS-05 (blob materialization, :188/:189) and VS-12 (per-call httpx client, :84) — different lines, different mechanism.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~Seven hand-built problem+json bodies omit the numeric `code` the lance plane's own error model calls required~~</b> <i>(exception-handlers.md + health-checks.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26.** One builder, `ns_errors.problem_body(code, *, status, title, detail, slug)`, emits the full six-key envelope, and all seven sites go through it — body_limit, load_shed, maintenance_mode, produce (×2), ingest_media (×3), train. **Plus the two the finding did not list:** `install_problem_handlers`' own 422 and 500 handlers had the same hole, and they are the bodies a generated client is most likely to meet, on the same `/v1` routes — fixing the seven and leaving those would have left the contract test passing over a surface that still breaks clients. **DEVIATION from the suggested fix, deliberately:** the audit says to raise `lance_namespace` errors and delete the hand-built responses. Every one of these sites sets `Retry-After` (5s draining, 60s maintenance) and `install_problem_handlers`' handler builds a bare `JSONResponse` with no headers — raising would have traded a missing `code` for a missing `Retry-After`, which the reference names explicitly, and a generic handler cannot know which window applies. So the SHAPE moved to one place and each site kept its status and headers, which is what the cited rule actually asks. Pinned by `tests/unit/test_problem_bodies_carry_a_code.py` (8 tests), including one that the fix did not RENAME any existing body — deriving `type` from `title.lower()` silently rewrote `/validation`, `/payload-too-large`, `/throttling` and `/maintenance`, hence `slug`.

**Rule.** exception-handlers.md: "One handler per exception class, registered in main.py. Don't scatter try/except in routes." — the shape must come from one place, and here seven places rebuild it by hand

**Sites:** `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/body_limit.py:71`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/load_shed.py:81`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/maintenance_mode.py:32`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce.py:88`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce.py:100`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/ingest_media.py:52`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/train.py:53`

**Why it matters.** All seven sites verified and the required-ness of `code` is stronger than the finding claimed — but the two halves are not the same defect and should not be filed as one. The three CATALOG sites are the real one: they answer on `/v1/table/{id}/{create,insert,merge_insert}` and on every mutating `/v1` route, which generated Lance-Namespace clients parse — and `code` is a REQUIRED (no-default) field on that client's `ErrorResponse` pydantic model, so validating the body raises, it does not KeyError. The four MEDALLION sites are root-mounted non-spec doors (`/produce`, `/ingest-media`, `/train`), reached by the frontend and operators, never by a generated client — there the omission is envelope inconsistency only, made worse by produce.py:97's own comment claiming "parity with catalog/lineage errors" that it does not achieve. Also worth stating: the three catalog sites are pure-ASGI middleware that sit OUTSIDE `ExceptionMiddleware` and so genuinely cannot route through the installed handler — "seven places rebuild it by hand" is not avoidable there; adding the two keys is.

**Fix.** For the two pure-ASGI middlewares (which genuinely cannot raise, since they must answer before the body is buffered — the comments explain why and that reasoning is sound), have them build their body from a shared helper in `ns_errors` that takes `(ErrorCode, detail)` and emits the full envelope including `code` and `error`; `THROTTLING` for the shed, `INVALID_INPUT` (or a new spec code) for the 413. For maintenance mode and the four medallion routes, raise the corresponding `lance_namespace` error (`ServiceUnavailableError`, `InvalidInputError`, `InvalidTableStateError`) and delete the hand-built `JSONResponse`es — `install_problem_handlers` is already installed on all three apps and will render them with `code`. Pin it with a contract test asserting `code` is present on every 4xx/5xx body the catalog and medallion emit.

**Evidence.**

```
services/catalog/src/catalog/api/load_shed.py:83-91 `{"type": "https://lance.org/problems/throttling", "title": "Too Many Requests", "status": 429, "detail": …}` — four keys, no `code`, no `error`; body_limit.py:73-83 the same four for 413; maintenance_mode.py:34-39 the same four for 503; produce.py:90-95 (409) and :102-107 (503), ingest_media.py:54-59/:65-70/:76-82, train.py:56-62 all four-key. Compare packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/lakehouse/ns_errors.py:97-107, which emits six: `type,title,status,detail,code,error`.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** CONFIRMED at HEAD, all seven. `catalog/api/load_shed.py:83-91` `{"type": "https://lance.org/problems/throttling", "title": "Too Many Requests", "status": 429, "detail": …}` — four keys; `body_limit.py:73-83` the same four for 413; `maintenance_mode.py:34-39` the same four for 503; `medallion/api/produce.py:90-95` (409) and `:102-107` (503); `ingest_media.py:54-59/:63-70/:73-82`; `train.py:53-64` `_problem()` — all four-key. Contrast `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/lakehouse/ns_errors.py:99-107`, six keys incl. `"code": int(exc.code)` and the comment "Spec-0.9 ErrorResponse compatibility: `code` (required)". I verified that requirement against the generated client rather than the comment: `.venv/.../lance_namespace_urllib3_client/models/error_response.py:32` `code: Annotated[int, Field(strict=True, ge=0)] = Field(description=…)` — no default, while `error`, `detail`, `context`, `instance` are all `Optional[...] = None`. Not a duplicate of catalog-api-01 (that finding's sites are `endpoints/stores.py` and its mechanism is raising the wrong exception class); grep of open_python-audit.md for load_shed.py / body_limit.py / maintenance_mode.py / the medallion api modules returns nothing on this.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~Multipart uploads are spooled to disk in FULL before the routes' byte caps can run — the cap protects memory and nothing else, and the docstring claims otherwise~~</b> <i>(file-handling.md + streaming.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26.** The cap moved to the DOOR: `service_kit/media/middleware.py` now registers `BodySizeLimitMiddleware` at `MediaSettings.max_body_bytes` (32 MiB, tighter than the catalog's 256 and looser than voice's 25 MiB `_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES`, so the handler's own message still wins), covering viewer/search/annotator — the three apps that take uploads and the three the fleet factory's cap never reached. `voice.py`'s docstring no longer claims the read-cap avoids buffering; the read-cap stays as defence in depth. The disk half is bounded too: `lance.tmpVolume` renders `sizeLimit` (`security.tmpSizeLimit`, 2Gi) so a spooled part cannot fill the NODE and evict every pod on it. Pinned by `packages/service-kit/tests/test_media_body_cap.py` (4 tests) and `test_invariants.py::test_the_scratch_emptyDir_is_BOUNDED`.

**Rule.** file-handling.md § Uploads — UploadFile with chunked reads; § Anti-patterns ("await file.read() without a size cap on uploads — bounded only by client patience"; "Storing temp files ... container restarts wipe them mid-stream")

**Sites:** `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/voice.py:142`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/voice.py:134`, `services/search/src/search/api/v1/router.py:234`, `chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:980`

**Why it matters.** The mechanism is exactly as described and the docstring IS false — but the stated blast radius ("/tmp draws on node ephemeral storage") is not what breaks first on the public path, and the memory half of the claim is stronger than the code warrants. Correct framing: (a) the docstring sentence at voice.py:134-136 is wrong and must be deleted/rewritten — the file part is spooled whole before the handler is entered; (b) `await file.read(N+1)` DOES cap the endpoint's own memory at 25 MiB, so "the cap protects memory" is right and is the only thing it does; (c) the unbounded landing zone is real but reachable in full only by traffic that bypasses the gateway (direct :8101/:8102), because through /api/explorer the gateway's own `content=await request.body()` (gateway/__init__.py:341, already filed as GW-BUFFERS-REQUEST-BODY) buffers the same body in gateway MEMORY first and is the first thing to fall over. Graded med for consistency with GW-BUFFERS-REQUEST-BODY (med), which is the same unbounded-body class one layer out.

**Fix.** Put the cap where the bytes arrive, not where they are read: reuse `catalog.api.body_limit.BodySizeLimitMiddleware` (it is pure-ASGI, already handles both the declared-Content-Length fast reject and the chunked/lying-length streaming counter, and its `except*` note applies unchanged) on the viewer and search apps, wired into `service_kit/media/middleware.py:register_middleware` so both get it from one place; size it per-app at ~32 MiB rather than the catalog's 256 MiB. Keep the existing `read(cap+1)` as defence in depth. Then rewrite voice.py:134-136 to say what is actually true. Independently, set a `sizeLimit` on the `tmp` emptyDir in _helpers.tpl:980-982 so a spool cannot reach node-level eviction.

**Evidence.**

```
voice.py:134-136 — "The body read stops one byte past the size cap, / so an oversize upload 400s (in the service) without ever being buffered / whole." ; voice.py:142 — `file_bytes = await file.read(voice_service._MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES + 1)` (_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES = 25 MiB, voice_service.py:65; the 400 is raised at voice_service.py:571). starlette/formparsers.py:181-188 — `def on_part_data(...): ... if self._current_part.file is None: if len(self._current_part.data) + len(message_bytes) > self.max_part_size: raise MultiPartException(...) ... else: self._file_parts_to_write.append((self._current_part, message_bytes))` — the size check is in the `file is None` branch only. starlette/formparsers.py:230 — `tempfile = SpooledTemporaryFile(max_size=self.spool_max_size)` with `spool_max_size = 1024 * 1024`. chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:980-982 — `{{- define "lance.tmpVolume" -}} - { name: tmp, emptyDir: {} }`.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Reproduced at HEAD. voice.py:130-141 docstring: "The body read stops one byte past the size cap, / so an oversize upload 400s (in the service) without ever being buffered / whole." voice.py:142 `file_bytes = await file.read(voice_service._MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES + 1)`; voice_service.py:65 `_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES = 25 * 1024 * 1024`, the 400 at voice_service.py:571 `if len(file_bytes) &gt; _MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES:`. Search mirror confirmed at search/api/v1/router.py:234-236 with its own comment "Bound the read so a large multipart part can't be buffered whole before validation (mirrors the voice upload's cap)" — which is the same false premise. Starlette is 1.3.1 (.venv/.../starlette-1.3.1.dist-info) and formparsers.py:181-188 reads verbatim `def on_part_data(self, data, start, end): message_bytes = data[start:end]; if self._current_part.file is None: if len(self._current_part.data) + len(message_bytes) &gt; self.max_part_size: raise MultiPartException(...) ... else: self._file_parts_to_write.append((self._current_part, message_bytes))` — the size guard is in the non-file branch only; formparsers.py:230 `tempfile = SpooledTemporaryFile(max_size=self.spool_max_size)` with `spool_max_size = 1024 * 1024` (line 147). No BodySizeLimitMiddleware in viewer or search (grep over services/+packages/ returns catalog only). chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:980-982 `{{- define "lance.tmpVolume" -}} - { name: tmp, emptyDir: {} }` with no sizeLimit, included for the explorer pods at chart/templates/explorer.yaml:265 (mount at :262) — confirmed, and it is the ONLY emptyDir in the chart without a sizeLimit where one is warranted (otel-collector.yaml:454 does carry `sizeLimit: 2Gi`). Not filed in open_python-audit.md (grep of voice.py returns only VS-24's unrelated private-constant cite).

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~`GET /api/media` answers 200 with Content-Length 0 and then crashes mid-stream for a document whose media payload is NULL — two sibling helpers took opposite decisions on the same value~~</b> <i>(file-handling.md + streaming.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26.** The two helpers no longer disagree, because the question is asked once. `blob_size` became `payload_size`, which raises `NotFoundError("media not available")` for a null payload at the ROUTE — before any response object exists, which is the last point a status can still be chosen. Merged into the size probe rather than added beside it: `take_blobs` is object-store IO on a media route, and a separate guard would have doubled it while leaving the old zero branch unreachable from its only caller. `stream_blob_range`'s own null branch no longer RAISES — reaching it means the guard was bypassed, and by then the headers are on the wire, so it logs an error and yields nothing, the one outcome that cannot contradict what was already sent. `ty` rejected simply deleting the branch (`BlobFile | None` in a `with`), which is how that landed on the honest answer rather than a cast. The 416 branch was already correct and is pinned. Pinned by `services/viewer/tests/test_media_null_payload.py` (4 tests, 3 RED before) against a REAL pylance dataset with `blob_array([None])`. Also removed the `# type: ignore[attr-defined]` in the rewritten function — mypy syntax `ty` does not honour.

**Rule.** streaming.md § Stream bytes / file-handling.md § Generated content — a `StreamingResponse` generator must not raise after the headers are on the wire; the exception handlers are already out of reach

**Sites:** `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/media.py:348`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/media.py:65`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/media.py:120`

**Why it matters.** `blob_size` and `stream_blob_range` both handle the pylance-10.0.0 null-payload slot, and they disagree — each with a comment asserting its answer is the honest one. `blob_size` (media.py:114-127) says "Zero is the honest answer here — the callers use it for Content-Length and range satisfiability, and an absent payload is a zero-length body" and returns 0; `stream_blob_range` (media.py:58-76) says "a 404 is the honest answer and the one every other absence on this path already gives" and raises `NotFoundError`. The non-range branch of `media()` calls both. Request: `GET /api/media/{doc_id}` (no Range header) for a document row whose `binding.media_blob` cell is null. `total = blob_size(...)` returns 0 at media.py:321; no Range header, so control falls to media.py:348, which constructs `StreamingResponse(stream_blob_range(ds, ..., start=0, end=-1), headers={'Content-Length': '0', ...})`. Starlette's `stream_response` (responses.py:248-255) sends `http.response.start` FIRST and only then iterates the body. The generator's very first step raises `NotFoundError` — after the 200 and `Content-Length: 0` have already been written. `register_handlers`' DomainError→404 mapping cannot see it. The caller gets a 200 empty body where every sibling absence on this path gives a 404 problem+json, and the server logs an unhandled-ASGI traceback on every such request. Note the range branch is fine: `parse_range(hdr, 0)` returns None (0 &gt; -1) and answers a clean 416.

**Fix.** Decide the null-payload question once, at the route. In `media()`, probe the blob once and treat `None` the way the rest of the path does — raise `NotFoundError("media not available")` beside the existing `OSError` guard at media.py:322-326, before any response object is built. Then `stream_blob_range` can drop its own `None` branch (it becomes unreachable) and `blob_size`'s 0 stays honest for the sizes it is actually asked for. Pin it with a test that serves a row with a null blob column and asserts 404, not 200.

**Evidence.**

```
media.py:348-356 — `return StreamingResponse(stream_blob_range(ds, binding.media_blob, rowid, start=0, end=total - 1), media_type=mime, headers={"Content-Length": str(total), ...})`. media.py:65-67 — `blob = ds.take_blobs(column, ids=[rowid])[0]` / `if blob is None:` / `raise NotFoundError("no payload for this row")`. media.py:120-121 — `if blob is None:` / `return 0`. starlette/responses.py:249-250 — `await send({"type": "http.response.start", ...})` then `async for chunk in self.body_iterator:`.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Reproduced exactly at HEAD. media.py:58-67 `def stream_blob_range(...) -&gt; Iterator[bytes]:` … `blob = ds.take_blobs(column, ids=[rowid])[0]` / `if blob is None:` / `raise NotFoundError("no payload for this row")`, under a comment that says "a 404 is the honest answer and the one every other absence on this path already gives". media.py:114-121 `def blob_size(...)` … `if blob is None:` / `return 0`, under a comment that says "Zero is the honest answer here — the callers use it for Content-Length and range satisfiability". Both comments are recorded decisions, and they are recorded decisions that CONTRADICT each other on the same pylance-10 null slot — which is what makes this a defect rather than a documented shape. The caller is `def media(...)` at media.py:306 (sync def, correctly threadpooled — no async/sync issue here): `total = blob_size(ds, binding.media_blob, rowid)` at :321, no Range header ⇒ falls to media.py:348-355 `return StreamingResponse(stream_blob_range(ds, binding.media_blob, rowid, start=0, end=total - 1), media_type=mime, headers={"Content-Length": str(total), "Accept-Ranges": "bytes", "Cache-Control": "no-store"})`. `stream_blob_range` is a generator function, so nothing executes until Starlette iterates it, and starlette/responses.py:248-250 is `async def stream_response(self, send): await send({"type": "http.response.start", ...}); async for chunk in self.body_iterator:` — the 200 and `Content-Length: 0` are on the wire before the first `next()` raises NotFoundError, so register_handlers' DomainError→404 mapping cannot reach it. The range branch is indeed clean as the finding says (parse_range at media.py:105-111 returns None when `start &gt;= total` with total 0 ⇒ 416). Not filed in open_python-audit.md: the existing media.py findings are VS-03 (ungated routes), VS-09 (clip SSRF) and VS-20 (parse_range's three-way return) — none touches the null-payload divergence.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~Six first-party fleet Deployments render with no container securityContext at all — the chart's own `security.readOnlyRootFilesystem: true` never reaches the internet-facing pod~~</b> <i>(kubernetes.md + microservices.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26.** `fleet.yaml` and `controlplane.yaml` now include `lance.securityContext` plus the `lance.tmpMount`/`lance.tmpVolume` scratch pair, so all six (gateway, compute, controlplane, flows, ingest, notifications) render `runAsNonRoot`, `seccompProfile: RuntimeDefault`, `allowPrivilegeEscalation: false`, `drop: ["ALL"]` and a read-only rootfs with a writable `/tmp` — verified on the render, not inferred. The false sentence in `values.yaml` ("applied to every app container") is corrected in place rather than appended to. The guard critique is fixed the way the audit asked, and it mattered: the old hand-written `first_party` tuple named ten Deployments and omitted compute, controlplane, flows and ingest — the very pods with no hardening — so a gate whose purpose is coverage skipped them silently. The new gate DERIVES its subject list from the render and carries an explicit exclusion list instead, with the OTel collector named visibly because `security.infraContexts` stages infra pods deliberately ("flip after the §7a live checks") and a coverage gate must not pre-empt a sequenced decision. Pinned by `test_every_first_party_container_carries_the_HARDENING_the_chart_claims` and `test_a_read_only_rootfs_is_SURVIVABLE_on_every_first_party_pod` (both RED before). **Reference note:** the cited rule is not in `kubernetes.md` — its § Full Deployment YAML contains no securityContext block. The defect stands on the chart's own written policy, which is what the fix is measured against.

**Rule.** kubernetes.md: § Full Deployment YAML — securityContext hygiene (runAsNonRoot, drop ALL caps, seccomp RuntimeDefault, readOnlyRootFilesystem)

**Sites:** `chart/templates/fleet.yaml:69-73`, `chart/templates/controlplane.yaml:32-35`, `chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:958-971`, `chart/values.yaml:520-521`, `tests/unit/test_invariants.py:509`

**Why it matters.** The gap is real and exactly as described, but it is blast-radius hardening rather than an open door, and one of the two quoted 'false docstrings' is actually accurate. Corrected framing: `chart/values.yaml:516` claims hardening is "applied to every app container via the lance.securityContext helper" and that sentence is false — `grep -n securityContext chart/templates/fleet.yaml chart/templates/controlplane.yaml` returns nothing, and the render confirms rask-{gateway,compute,controlplane,flows,ingest,notifications} carry no securityContext while every lance-plane and web Deployment does. But `_helpers.tpl:952` scopes its own claim by enumeration — "applied to every APP container (our images: catalog/lineage/web/movers/compaction)" — and the fleet is not in that list, so the helper docstring is not the lie; values.yaml:516 is. Practical exposure is also narrower than stated: `.docker/gateway.dockerfile:79` is `USER app`, and a container that never runs as uid 0 holds no effective capabilities regardless of the missing `drop: ["ALL"]`, so 'the full default capability set' is nominal rather than effective. What genuinely remains: no admission-time runAsNonRoot, no RuntimeDefault seccomp, and a writable rootfs on the pod the Ingress routes /api to; plus the latent PSA-restricted admission rejection, which values.yaml:529-531 explicitly stages as a manual post-check step ('label the namespace ... ONLY after these boot green — the label itself is a kubectl step, the namespace isn't chart-owned'), so it is a known-sequenced item, not an unnoticed trap. The guard critique is fully correct: tests/unit/test_invariants.py:509 checks only ('livenessProbe','readinessProbe','preStop').

**Fix.** Add `{{- include "lance.securityContext" $root | nindent 10 }}` beside the existing `lance.preStop` include in fleet.yaml (line 85) and add both to controlplane.yaml's container. Add `{{- include "lance.tmpMount" ... }}` + `{{- include "lance.tmpVolume" ... }}` so readOnlyRootFilesystem is survivable (the fleet images write to /tmp for OTel and pyarrow the same way the lance ones do). Then extend the invariant test's `missing` list from `("livenessProbe", "readinessProbe", "preStop")` to include `"runAsNonRoot"` and `"seccompProfile"`, and — more importantly — replace the hand-maintained `first_party` tuple with a derivation over the rendered set minus the known subchart names, so a new first-party Deployment is covered by default instead of by remembering.

**Evidence.**

```
chart/templates/fleet.yaml:69-73 is the entire container preamble — `containers:\n  - name: {{ $name }}\n    image: ...\n    imagePullPolicy: ...\n    command: ["uvicorn"]` — with no securityContext anywhere in the 258-line file (grep 'securityContext' chart/templates/fleet.yaml returns nothing). Rendered with `helm template chart --set image.localImages=true ...`, the per-Deployment table reads: rask-gateway / rask-compute / rask-controlplane / rask-flows / rask-ingest / rask-notifications => readOnlyRootFilesystem NO, runAsNonRoot NO; release-name-catalog / -lineage / -bronze-to-silver / -silver-to-gold / -media-to-silver / -medallion-producer / -maintenance and all seven rask-web-* => yes, yes. _helpers.tpl:958-971 is the helper they do not call: `securityContext:\n  runAsNonRoot: true\n  allowPrivilegeEscalation: false\n  readOnlyRootFilesystem: {{ .Values.security.readOnlyRootFilesystem }}\n  capabilities:\n    drop: ["ALL"]\n  seccompProfile:\n    type: RuntimeDefault`.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** chart/templates/fleet.yaml:69-73 is the whole container preamble — `containers:` / `- name: {{ $name }}` / `image:` / `imagePullPolicy:` / `command: ["uvicorn"]` — and `grep -n securityContext chart/templates/fleet.yaml chart/templates/controlplane.yaml` returns nothing. I rendered the chart myself (`helm template rask chart --set image.localImages=true --set-string frontend.oidc.sessionSecret=... --set-string frontend.oidc.publicIssuer=... --set-string frontend.oidc.publicOrigin=...`, 34 Deployments) and tabulated: rask-gateway / rask-compute / rask-controlplane / rask-flows / rask-ingest / rask-notifications =&gt; readOnlyRootFilesystem NO, runAsNonRoot NO; rask-{catalog,lineage,maintenance,medallion-producer,bronze-to-silver,silver-to-gold,media-to-silver} and all seven rask-web-* =&gt; yes, yes. Exactly the split claimed. _helpers.tpl:958-971 is the uncalled helper, verbatim as quoted. Counter-evidence I found: _helpers.tpl:952 reads "applied to every APP container (our images: catalog/lineage/web/movers/compaction)" — an enumeration the fleet is absent from; and `.docker/gateway.dockerfile:79` `USER app`. open_python-audit.md contains exactly one `chart/` citation in 304 findings (line 164, explorer.yaml:140) — nothing on fleet.yaml or securityContext, so this is genuinely new.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~The Dapr sidecar's automatic 5xx retry is aimed at write doors whose idempotency key is optional, so one operator click can start four unrelated bronze→gold cascades~~</b> <i>(kubernetes.md + microservices.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27 (owner-approved).** Both halves of the rule, because each covers what the other misses. **(1)** `Idempotency-Key` is REQUIRED (422 without) on `/produce`, `/ingest-media`, `/train` and `POST /api/ingests`, constrained identically (`^[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,64}$`) so a key accepted by one head is accepted by all four; the `token = token or uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]` mints are deleted and the params typed `str`. **(2)** A new `writeRetry` policy (`408,429,502,503,504` — no 500) covers `medallion-producer` and `ingest`; every other app keeps `invokeRetry`, verified on the rendered CR. 502/503/504 mean the app was never reached, and these doors answer 503 with an explicit `Retry-After: 5`, so a retry is what they ASK for; a bare 500 means the app WAS reached and may have published before failing. **`/api/ingests` IS included** — owner call, against the finding's own analysis half: `ingest/api.py` did `key = idempotency_key or uuid.uuid4().hex` exactly like its siblings, so it converged only for callers who sent a key (the compute zone does; curl and `scripts/` do not). The models zone now mints a content-derived key (`train.remote.ts`), matching the compute zone's existing shape — content-derived, not random, or every replay would carry a fresh key and reintroduce the defect while satisfying the 422. Pinned by `tests/unit/test_write_doors_require_idempotency.py` (9 tests, all RED before). `services/ingest/tests/test_ingest_api.py::test_missing_key_does_not_collide_across_callers` asserted the DEFECT — two unkeyed POSTs getting two runs — and was rewritten to pin the refusal, not deleted.

**Rule.** microservices.md: § Inter-service comms — sync — retry policy must be paired with idempotency; § Anti-patterns — "Service emitting events with no ... idempotent consumers" / outbox + idempotent consumers

**Sites:** `chart/templates/dapr-resiliency.yaml:122-136`, `chart/templates/dapr-resiliency.yaml:180-183`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/produce.py:75`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce.py:96-107`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce.py:34`

**Why it matters.** The mechanism is real for three of the four doors, but one load-bearing piece of evidence is factually wrong and it changes the shape of the finding. FALSE: "No frontend sends the header (grep ... over frontend/ returns zero hits; only scripts/ingest-lane.sh sends one)." `frontend/packages/api/src/ingest.ts:125` is `if (request.idempotencyKey) headers['Idempotency-Key'] = request.idempotencyKey;` and `frontend/microfrontends/compute/src/routes/etl/+page.svelte:196` sends a DETERMINISTIC key derived from the request content. `/api/ingests` also has genuine server-side dedup (services/ingest/src/ingest/runs.py:4 'the token only made the run id'; api.py:227 refuses a reused key with a changed spec), and a sidecar replay carries the caller's headers unchanged, so that door converges correctly and must be struck from the list. What survives, and is correct: `/api/produce`, `/api/train` and `/api/ingest-media` mint `token = token or uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]` per attempt, no caller supplies a key (the models zone calls `/api/train` through `gatewayJSON` with no header — train.remote.ts:23), and the gateway sidecar replays a 5xx three times at 2 s intervals. Severity med not high: it needs a 5xx from an admin-gated door, and the estate's own 503 path deliberately says Retry-After: 5 — so a retry IS intended; the defect is that the intended retry is unsafe without the key the platform layer structurally cannot mint.

**Fix.** Two halves, and both are needed. (1) At the policy: give the write app-ids their own retry policy that matches only `408,429,502,503,504` and excludes 500 — or, cleaner, drop `medallion-producer` and `ingest` from the blanket `invokeRetry` target list and let their doors' explicit `Retry-After` contract be honoured by the CALLER, which is the only party that can hold a key stable across attempts. (2) At the door: make `Idempotency-Key` REQUIRED on `/produce`, `/train`, `/ingest-media` and `POST /api/ingests` (422 without one) rather than defaulting to a fresh uuid — an unkeyed write to a cascade head is not a safe default, and `token = token or uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]` is precisely the 'inventing a token here would defeat the entire idempotency argument' failure that tests/unit/test_publish_saga.py:377 already asserts against elsewhere. Then have the zone's remote function generate and persist a key per user action. Add a render+code invariant pairing the set of app-ids under `invokeRetry` with the set of routes that require a key.

**Evidence.**

```
chart/templates/dapr-resiliency.yaml:125-136 — `retries:\n  invokeRetry:\n    policy: constant\n    duration: 2s\n    maxRetries: 3\n    matching:\n      httpStatusCodes: 408,429,500-599`, applied at :180-183 as `{{- range $invoked }}\n      {{ . }}:\n        timeout: invokeTimeout\n        retry: invokeRetry` where `$invoked` is derived from the same `frontDoor`-or-singleTenant gate fleet.yaml uses, plus `medallion.producer.daprAppId`. services/medallion/src/medallion/services/produce.py:75 — `token = token or uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]`, under a docstring at :62-67 reading 'token is the caller's idempotency key ... token would double-fire the cascade head as two unrelated runs. A reused token converges instead ... Absent (the common fire-and-forget case) → a fresh random token.' services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce.py:34 — `idempotency_key: Annotated[str | None, Header(alias="Idempotency-Key", ...)] = None`.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** chart/templates/dapr-resiliency.yaml:125-136 verbatim, including `matching:` / `httpStatusCodes: 408,429,500-599` — and the surrounding comment shows the matching block was added to stop 4xx amplification, i.e. 5xx replay is deliberate. dapr-resiliency.yaml:180-183 applies `timeout: invokeTimeout` / `retry: invokeRetry` over `$invoked`, which includes `.Values.medallion.producer.daprAppId` (:172-174). services/medallion/src/medallion/services/produce.py:75 verbatim `token = token or uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]`, under the :62-67 docstring naming the double-fire outcome. services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce.py:34 verbatim `idempotency_key: Annotated[str | None, Header(alias="Idempotency-Key", min_length=1, max_length=64, pattern=r"^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$")] = None`, and :96-107 the 503 + `Retry-After: 5`. gateway __init__.py:146 `("/api/produce", "/produce", *medallion)` confirms the route is reachable. Counter-evidence: `grep -rn 'Idempotency-Key' frontend/` returns 5 hits, not zero — ingest.ts:88/89/125 and compute/etl/+page.svelte:196/379.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~The drain gate is wired to the cascade's trigger door but not to the door that does the work — `on_stage` accepts stage triggers while the mover is shutting down~~</b> <i>(kubernetes.md + microservices.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26 — ALREADY FIXED.** `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/events.py:59` carries `Depends(retry_when_draining)` in `on_stage`'s signature; landed in `264adf29`.

**Rule.** microservices.md: § Anti-patterns — a message handler must be retryable, and delivery must not race the pod's lifecycle; kubernetes.md: § Shutdown sequence — Dapr delivery does not consult the readiness probe

**Sites:** `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/events.py:45-62`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/bronze_arrival.py:38-56`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/draining.py:3-8`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/mover.py:50`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/mover.py:109`

**Why it matters.** The omission is reproduced exactly and is the finding's real contribution: `retry_when_draining` / `refuse_when_draining` appear at exactly five non-test call sites, all in medallion (bronze_arrival.py:22+:49, produce.py:13+:39, train.py:27+:73, ingest_media.py:13+:27), and `on_stage` — the subscription that runs the whole transform — has no drain dependency. Two corrections to the failure story. (1) It does NOT compound with finding 2: the movers are rendered by medallion.yaml:273, which DOES set `terminationGracePeriodSeconds`, so they get an explicit 30 s, not a silently-defaulted one; the '25 s into the drain' arithmetic is the same either way but the drift claim does not apply here. (2) 'a partially-written stage dataset behind it' overstates the damage — a Lance write commits atomically, so a SIGKILL mid-write leaves an uncommitted fragment for the orphan scan, not a half-visible stage. What holds: no ACK and no RETRY reach the broker because the sidecar dies with the pod, so recovery waits the 720 s ackWait (chart/templates/dapr-component.yaml:105 `- { name: ackWait, value: "720s" }`), and chart/values-prod.yaml:28 `moverReplicas: 1` means no sibling picks it up. A silent ~12-minute cascade stall per rolling deploy is a real availability mode, but it self-heals through the redelivery path the estate deliberately built, which is why med rather than high.

**Fix.** Add `drain: Annotated[dict[str, str] | None, Depends(retry_when_draining)] = None` to `on_stage` and return it unchanged when set, exactly as bronze_arrival.py:49-56 does — the mover already sets `app.state.shutting_down` in both directions (mover.py:50 and :109), so the flag is live and only the read is missing. Then sweep the rest: the honest fix is to make the gate structural rather than per-route — have `register_stage_route`/`DaprApp.subscribe` wrappers inject it, or add a router-level `dependencies=[...]` on every module that registers a sidecar-delivered route, so a new subscription cannot be added without it. Note the prerequisite for the ingest/compute/flows half: those three lifespans never set `shutting_down` (draining.py:52-55 says so explicitly), so the dependency would be inert there until their lifespans set the flag — do both in one change, or the sweep looks done and is not.

**Evidence.**

```
services/medallion/src/medallion/api/events.py:51-58 — `async def on_stage(\n    event: dict[str, Any],\n    dapr: DaprClientDep,\n    config: SettingsDep,\n    fga_client: FgaClientDep,\n    catalog_http: CatalogHttpDep,\n    _: Annotated[None, Depends(require_dapr_token)],\n) -> dict[str, str]:` — no drain dependency. Contrast services/medallion/src/medallion/api/bronze_arrival.py:49 — `drain: Annotated[dict[str, str] | None, Depends(retry_when_draining)] = None,` with the comment at :56 'B6: while this replica is draining it asks for REDELIVERY rather than handling the event.' `grep -rn 'retry_when_draining\|refuse_when_draining' services/ --include=*.py` (excluding tests) returns exactly five sites: bronze_arrival.py:22+:49, produce.py:13+:39, train.py:27+:73, ingest_media.py:13+:27 — all in medallion, none in the stage handler.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** services/medallion/src/medallion/api/events.py:51-58 verbatim: `async def on_stage(event: dict[str, Any], dapr: DaprClientDep, config: SettingsDep, fga_client: FgaClientDep, catalog_http: CatalogHttpDep, _: Annotated[None, Depends(require_dapr_token)]) -&gt; dict[str, str]:` — no drain dependency, and `@dapr_app.subscribe(...)` at :45-50 confirms it is the sidecar-delivered route. Contrast bronze_arrival.py:49 `drain: Annotated[dict[str, str] | None, Depends(retry_when_draining)] = None`. packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/draining.py:5-8 verbatim: 'Dapr's pub/sub delivery does not consult a readiness probe, so a pod that had begun shutting down kept accepting cascade triggers and run submissions, started work it could not finish, and took the run down with it.' `grep -rn 'retry_when_draining\|refuse_when_draining' services/ packages/ --include=*.py | grep -v /tests/` returns exactly the five sites claimed. Verified against chart/templates/medallion.yaml:273 (movers DO carry grace) and chart/values-prod.yaml:28. Not a duplicate: open_python-audit.md MED-002 covers the ADJACENT mechanism (the ack held across long GPU work → ackWait redelivery pileup at transform.py:225), not drain admission; nothing in the audit cites events.py or draining.py.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~No startupProbe on any fleet Deployment: a fixed 75-second boot budget for services whose lifespans do OIDC discovery, FGA provisioning and a Dapr actor handshake~~</b> <i>(kubernetes.md + microservices.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **MERGED 2026-08-26 — DUPLICATE.** Same mechanism and overlapping sites as the `health`-lane finding *"The six fleet pods have no startupProbe, and controlplane also missed the measured probe-timeout"*. Drain there, once.

**Rule.** kubernetes.md: § What NOT to do — "Long synchronous warm-up in lifespan startup without a startupProbe: k8s declares the pod dead before it's done loading"

**Sites:** `chart/templates/fleet.yaml:231-235`, `chart/templates/controlplane.yaml:56-59`, `chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:914-921`, `services/notifications/src/notifications/lifespan.py:143-156`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/actor_warmup.py:32`

**Why it matters.** uvicorn binds the listening socket only AFTER the lifespan's startup half completes, so nothing answers any probe during boot. fleet.yaml's livenessProbe is `initialDelaySeconds: 15, periodSeconds: 20` with no `failureThreshold`, so it defaults to 3 — a hard 15 + 3x20 = 75 s ceiling, after which the kubelet SIGKILLs the container into CrashLoopBackOff. The lance plane gives itself 300 s for the identical concern, and `lance.appProbes`'s comment states the measured reason in as many words: 'the FastAPI lifespan (Dapr secret fetch ~80s worst case + AGE pool + DDL + FGA provision) runs BEFORE uvicorn accepts connections, so nothing answers /livez during boot. Without this, liveness (armed ~70s in) SIGKILLs a still-initializing pod into CrashLoopBackOff exactly when a dependency is already slow.' The fleet's boots are not trivially fast either: notifications' lifespan builds an OIDCVerifier (which fetches discovery, and whose JWKS fetch has no explicit timeout — already filed as SKG-12), provisions or resolves an OpenFGA store, registers two actor types, pays a 10 s actor-proxy handshake (`WARMUP_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 10.0`) and probes the sidecar's actor state store; ingest and flows start a Dapr WorkflowRuntime. Concrete failure: on a cold `make k3s-up` or any node reboot, dex and OpenFGA come up alongside the fleet, so the discovery fetch is exactly the call that is slow — and the pod is killed for being slow to start because a dependency it needs is slow to start, which is the restart loop that blocks recovery.

**Fix.** Include `lance.appProbes`' startupProbe pattern in fleet.yaml and controlplane.yaml: a `startupProbe` on the same path with `periodSeconds: 10, failureThreshold: 30`. Cheapest correct form is to add the three lines beside the existing readiness/liveness blocks; better is to lift the whole probe triple into one helper the fleet and the lance plane share, so the boot budget cannot drift between planes again — that helper already exists and is called by five templates.

**Evidence.**

```
chart/templates/fleet.yaml:231-235 — `livenessProbe:\n  httpGet: {path: {{ $svc.healthPath | default "/api/health" }}, port: http}\n  initialDelaySeconds: 15\n  periodSeconds: 20\n  timeoutSeconds: 5` with no failureThreshold and no startupProbe anywhere in the file. Rendered check across all 34 Deployments: `startupProbe` present on release-name-{catalog,lineage,maintenance,medallion-producer,bronze-to-silver,silver-to-gold,media-to-silver,cloudnative-pg}; absent on rask-{gateway,compute,controlplane,flows,ingest,notifications} and on all seven rask-web-*. chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:920 — `startupProbe:\n  httpGet: { path: /livez, port: http }\n  periodSeconds: 10\n  failureThreshold: 30`, i.e. a 300 s budget the fleet does not get.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Reproduced exactly, including the rendered split. chart/templates/fleet.yaml:231-235 verbatim: `livenessProbe:` / `  httpGet: {path: {{ $svc.healthPath | default "/api/health" }}, port: http}` / `  initialDelaySeconds: 15` / `  periodSeconds: 20` / `  timeoutSeconds: 5` — no failureThreshold (k8s defaults it to 3) and no startupProbe anywhere in the file; controlplane.yaml:56-59 is the same shape. My own render of all 34 Deployments: startupProbe present on rask-{catalog,lineage,maintenance,medallion-producer,bronze-to-silver,silver-to-gold,media-to-silver} and rask-cloudnative-pg; absent on rask-{gateway,compute,controlplane,flows,ingest,notifications} and all seven rask-web-*. chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:914-921 carries the comment verbatim ('the FastAPI lifespan (Dapr secret fetch ~80s worst case + AGE pool + DDL + FGA provision) runs BEFORE uvicorn accepts connections, so nothing answers /livez during boot. Without this, liveness (armed ~70s in) SIGKILLs a still-initializing pod into CrashLoopBackOff') and gives that plane `periodSeconds: 10` / `failureThreshold: 30` = 300 s. The boot cost is real: services/notifications/src/notifications/lifespan.py:134-156 registers two actor types, then `await warm_actor_proxy_factory()` (actor_warmup.py:32 `WARMUP_TIMEOUT_SECONDS: Final = 10.0`) and `await probe_actor_state_store(...)`, all before `app.state.startup_complete = True`. Nothing in the chart or the code records a decision to withhold the startupProbe from the fleet. No audit coverage.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~`make_service_app` mounts no probes router, so four of the five services it composes have no /readyz at all and the chart uses a static liveness badge as the readiness probe~~</b> <i>(kubernetes.md + microservices.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** `make_service_app` takes `ready_check: ReadyCheck | None = None` and root-mounts `make_probes_router(ready_check)` unconditionally, so compute, controlplane, flows, ingest and notifications all answer `/livez` + `/readyz`; notifications' hand-rolled mount is gone and it passes `actor_plane_ready` through the parameter. **The sharper half is fixed too:** `fleet.yaml` pointed BOTH probes at `healthPath | default "/api/health"`, a STATIC badge — so readiness answered 200 while the pod drained. Readiness now asks `readyPath | default "/readyz"`, liveness `healthPath | default "/livez"`; `controlplane.yaml` renders its own container and carried its own copy of the conflated pair, fixed the same way. `/api/health` is untouched — it is the documented frontend badge. The gateway keeps `/healthz` for both (recorded exception, now explicit via `readyPath`) and, per the Fix's last sentence, **its probe reports the drain**: it returned `Liveness()` unconditionally, and it is the ingress every request passes through, so a rolling update kept it in rotation through SIGTERM. `arm_drain_on_sigterm` + a 503 branch fix that. Pinned by `tests/unit/test_fleet_probes.py` (13 tests, all RED before). The recorded-decision strand (that `make_service_app` supplies no `/api/health`) is untouched, as the finding asks.

**Rule.** kubernetes.md: § Full Deployment YAML — "Liveness — am I alive? No deps. Readiness — can I serve? Checks deps."

**Sites:** `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/__init__.py:136-158`, `chart/templates/fleet.yaml:222-223`, `services/compute/src/compute/health.py:19-21`, `services/notifications/src/notifications/__init__.py:34`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/probes.py:52-62`

**Why it matters.** Confirmed on the facts, but one strand is a recorded decision and should be dropped, and the strongest strand is missing. RECORDED, not a defect: that `make_service_app` supplies no /api/health route is stated twice in the estate's own comments — services/ingest/src/ingest/health.py:4 ('`make_service_app` does NOT supply a health route, every service mounts its own') and services/flows/src/flows/health.py:8. What is NOT recorded anywhere, and is the real finding: the drain-aware `service_kit.probes` router is unmounted on compute, controlplane, flows and ingest (only notifications root-mounts it, __init__.py:34), so those four expose no /livez and no /readyz at all. And the sharper point the finding misses — even where /readyz DOES exist, the chart never asks it: fleet.yaml:222 and :231 both point at `$svc.healthPath | default "/api/health"`, so notifications' `make_probes_router(actor_plane_ready)` — which reports shutting_down, starting, and a degraded actor plane — is probed by nothing. That is the estate paying for a readiness surface and then wiring the kubelet past it.

**Fix.** Give `make_service_app` a `ready_check: ReadyCheck | None = None` parameter and have it root-mount `make_probes_router(ready_check)` unconditionally — the router is dependency-free and the five services already set (or can trivially set) `startup_complete`/`shutting_down`. Then change fleet.yaml's readinessProbe path to `/readyz` while leaving liveness on `/livez` (or on the existing `healthPath`), matching `lance.appProbes`. Keep `/api/health` as the frontend-facing badge it is documented to be. The gateway is the one justified exception and already names it: `healthPath: /healthz` with a comment explaining that probing a proxied path would couple its readiness to an upstream — but even it should set `app.state.shutting_down` so its `/healthz` can report the drain.

**Evidence.**

```
packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/__init__.py:144-157 is the whole composition — `register_handlers(app)`, `register_middleware(app, settings)`, `for router in routers: app.include_router(router, prefix=settings.api_prefix)`, `if proxy_router is not None: app.include_router(proxy_router)`, slash middleware, `setup_otel(...)` — no probes router. chart/templates/fleet.yaml:222-223 and :231-232 both read `httpGet: {path: {{ $svc.healthPath | default "/api/health" }}, port: http}`; values.yaml sets `healthPath` for exactly one service (gateway, `/healthz`, line 329), so compute/flows/ingest/notifications/controlplane all get `/api/health` twice. services/compute/src/compute/health.py:19-21 — `@router.get("/health")\nasync def health() -> Liveness:\n    return Liveness()`.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/__init__.py:135-158 is the whole composition, read in full: `FastAPI(...)`, `register_handlers(app)`, `register_middleware(app, settings)`, `for router in routers: app.include_router(router, prefix=settings.api_prefix)`, `if proxy_router is not None: app.include_router(proxy_router)`, `app.router.redirect_slashes = False`, `add_middleware(SlashToleranceMiddleware, ...)`, `setup_otel(...)`, `return app` — no probes router. `grep -rn 'livez\|readyz' services/ --include=*.py | grep -v /tests/` finds mounts in viewer/main.py:103, search/main.py:70, lineage/main.py:168, annotator/main.py:225, maintenance/service.py:43 and notifications/__init__.py:34 — and none in compute, controlplane, flows or ingest. services/compute/src/compute/health.py:16-19 verbatim: `@router.get("/health")` / `async def health() -&gt; Liveness:` / `    return Liveness()` — a static 200. chart/values.yaml:334 sets `healthPath: /healthz` for the gateway only, so the other five get `/api/health` on both probes. probes.py:52-62 verbatim shows the three-stage gate (shutting_down → startup_complete → ready_check) that nothing in the fleet plane consults.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~controlplane has no preStop drain delay, and the invariant test written to prevent exactly that cannot see it~~</b> <i>(kubernetes.md + microservices.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** Both halves. `controlplane.yaml` carries `{{- include "lance.preStop" . }}` (landed with the securityContext in `dd225c35`), so the Deployment serving `/api/projects` no longer drops in-flight reads on every `helm upgrade`. And the gate is fixed rather than the tuple, as the finding asks: `test_every_first_party_deployment_is_hardened` now derives its subjects from the render via `_first_party_deployments` instead of naming ten fragments that omitted controlplane, compute, flows, ingest, maintenance, viewer, search and annotator — a gate whose own docstring argues "an every claim in prose is worth nothing" was making exactly that claim with a literal list. It also checks per CONTAINER now; the old version matched on document text, so a second container could satisfy the check for the first. PROVEN, not assumed: with controlplane's preStop temporarily removed the derived gate fails (`rask-controlplane/controlplane missing ['preStop']`) where the tuple version could not match the name at all. The `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` half the Fix mentions belongs to the separate Dapr-shutdown finding — no fleet Deployment sets one — and stays open there.

**Rule.** kubernetes.md: § Shutdown sequence — preStop closes the gap between endpoint removal and SIGTERM; § Full Deployment YAML lifecycle.preStop

**Sites:** `chart/templates/controlplane.yaml:32-60`, `tests/unit/test_invariants.py:496-500`, `chart/templates/fleet.yaml:80-85`

**Why it matters.** fleet.yaml carries `lance.preStop` with a comment naming the mechanism ('kube-proxy can still route to a terminating pod, so without it a rolling update drops in-flight requests... Pinned by tests/unit/test_invariants.py::test_every_first_party_deployment_is_hardened'). controlplane.yaml is a separate template for the same kind of workload and has no `lifecycle:` block at all — the container goes straight from `ports:`/`envFrom:`/`env:` to `readinessProbe:`. It is a first-party Deployment serving `/api/projects` through the gateway (the projects list every zone's navbar renders), so a `helm upgrade` drops in-flight project reads for the few seconds kube-proxy takes to notice the endpoint removal. The reason it survived is that the test's `first_party` tuple is a hand-maintained list of ten name fragments — `gateway, catalog, lineage, compaction, medallion-producer, bronze-to-silver, silver-to-gold, media-to-silver, web, notifications` — which omits controlplane, compute, flows, ingest, maintenance, viewer, search and annotator. The test's own docstring argues that 'an "every" claim in prose is worth nothing; this loop is what makes it true', and then makes the same kind of claim with a literal list.

**Fix.** Add `{{- include "lance.preStop" . | nindent 10 }}` (and the securityContext + grace from the findings above) to controlplane.yaml's container. Then fix the gate rather than the tuple: derive `first_party` from the rendered set by excluding the known subchart Deployment names (dapr-*, kuberay-operator, cloudnative-pg, kueue-*, openfga, dex, openbao, nats-box, rustfs-operator*, otel-collector) instead of enumerating ours, so the default for a new Deployment is 'checked' rather than 'invisible'.

**Evidence.**

```
chart/templates/controlplane.yaml:40-52 runs `ports:\n  - name: http\n    containerPort: ...` → `envFrom:` → `env:` → `readinessProbe:` with no `lifecycle:` in between; `grep -n lifecycle chart/templates/controlplane.yaml` returns nothing. Rendered: rask-controlplane is the only first-party Deployment in the release with `preStop NO` (all six other fleet rows and every lance/web row show `yes`). tests/unit/test_invariants.py:496-500 — `first_party = (\n    "gateway", "catalog", "lineage", "compaction", "medallion-producer",\n    "bronze-to-silver", "silver-to-gold", "media-to-silver", "web",\n    "notifications",\n)`.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Reproduced exactly. I read chart/templates/controlplane.yaml:31-61 in full: the container runs `- name: controlplane` → `image:` → `imagePullPolicy:` → `command: ["uvicorn"]` → `args:` → `ports:` → `envFrom:` → `env:` → `readinessProbe:` → `livenessProbe:` → `resources:`, with no `lifecycle:` block; `grep -n lifecycle chart/templates/controlplane.yaml` returns nothing. fleet.yaml:79-84 carries the contrasting comment verbatim ('Drain delay before SIGTERM: kube-proxy can still route to a terminating pod, so without it a rolling update drops in-flight requests. Matters most on the gateway, which is the ingress. Pinned by tests/unit/test_invariants.py::test_every_first_party_deployment_is_hardened') followed by `{{- include "lance.preStop" $root | nindent 10 }}`. My own render across all 34 Deployments: rask-controlplane is the ONLY first-party Deployment with `preStop NO` — the other five fleet rows and every lance/web row show yes. tests/unit/test_invariants.py:496-500 verbatim: `first_party = ("gateway", "catalog", "lineage", "compaction", "medallion-producer", "bronze-to-silver", "silver-to-gold", "media-to-silver", "web", "notifications",)`. The list is worse than the finding says: it also names "compaction", which matches no rendered Deployment (the compaction workload renders as `rask-maintenance`), so that entry is dead weight and maintenance is unchecked too — it happens to be hardened. controlplane serves `/api/projects` through the gateway, which the navbar renders on every zone, so the dropped in-flight reads are user-visible. No audit coverage of chart/templates/controlplane.yaml.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~The Dapr sidecar's shutdown is not bounded against the pod's drain window — daprd's 5 s default kills the publish path the app still needs while it drains~~</b> <i>(kubernetes.md + microservices.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** `rask.daprAnnotations` now emits `dapr.io/block-shutdown-duration`, derived from `lifecycle.sidecarBlockShutdownSeconds` — **all 16 sidecar pods**, verified on the render, not just the fleet. The grace period is raised 30 → 40 to budget the sidecar drain `microservices.md` adds to the no-sidecar formula ("+ ~10 s — Dapr flushes pending acks"), and the arithmetic is STATED in values.yaml the way `dapr-resiliency.yaml` states its own: `grace >= preStop + sidecarBlock + buffer`, with the block bounded on both sides — longer than preStop or the sidecar still goes first, shorter than grace or the kubelet SIGKILLs it mid-flush having made it decline to flush earlier. Three numbers, one values block, so they cannot drift. **The grace-period half the previous finding deferred here is included:** fleet.yaml and controlplane.yaml declared no `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` at all — eight other templates do — so the six fleet pods silently took the kubelet's 30 s default, a number nobody chose. Pinned by `tests/unit/test_sidecar_drain_budget.py` (4 tests, all RED before).

**Rule.** microservices.md: § Dapr + Kubernetes interplay — "What changes vs the no-sidecar deployment: terminationGracePeriodSeconds = preStop + in-flight + lifespan + buffer **+ sidecar drain time (~10 s — Dapr flushes pending acks)**"

**Sites:** `chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:194-221`, `chart/values.yaml:511-513`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/workflow.py:363`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/mover.py:104-115`

**Why it matters.** `rask.daprAnnotations` emits app-id, app-port, log-level, log-as-json, max-body-size, app-token-secret, sidecar resources and dapr.io/config — and nothing about shutdown. No `dapr.io/graceful-shutdown-seconds` and no `dapr.io/block-shutdown-duration` appears anywhere in the chart. daprd therefore takes its own 5 s default while the app container is still inside its 5 s preStop sleep plus whatever drain the grace period allows. Because the kubelet SIGTERMs every container in the pod simultaneously, the sidecar is gone before the app's drain window has even opened. Concrete failure: a mover or the producer that is mid-handler when the rollout starts finishes its Lance write and then calls `publish_event` to fire the next stage (workflow.py:363, and the same shape at :971 and :1058) against a sidecar that has already stopped — the write has landed, the cascade trigger has not, and nothing distinguishes that from a stage that simply had no successor. The estate has already paid for this class of mismatch once: `service_kit.dapr_publish` exists solely because 'the bare publish_event is unbounded, so a wedged sidecar hangs this activity forever' (workflow.py:357-358), which bounds the hang but does not make the publish succeed. It also silently undermines the drain gate on `/bronze-arrival`: a RETRY verdict has to travel back through the sidecar to reach the broker.

**Fix.** Add `dapr.io/block-shutdown-duration` to `rask.daprAnnotations`, set from the same `.Values.lifecycle` block that feeds preStop and the grace period so the three cannot drift — the sidecar should outlive the app's drain, not race it. A sensible derivation is `terminationGracePeriodSeconds - buffer`. Then raise `lifecycle.terminationGracePeriodSeconds` to accommodate the sidecar drain the reference budgets (~10 s) on top of the app's own, which is the same arithmetic the grace-period finding above needs anyway — do both in one change and state the formula in the values comment the way `dapr-resiliency.yaml:19-24` already states its own.

**Evidence.**

```
chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:198-221 is the complete annotation set: `dapr.io/enabled`, `dapr.io/app-id`, `dapr.io/app-port`, `dapr.io/log-level`, `dapr.io/log-as-json`, optional `dapr.io/max-body-size`, `dapr.io/app-token-secret`, the sidecar resource quad from `lance.daprSidecarResources`, and `dapr.io/config: "lance-tracing"`. `grep -rn 'graceful-shutdown\|block-shutdown' chart/` returns nothing. chart/values.yaml:511-513 budgets `preStopSeconds: 5` and `terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 30` with the comment 'terminationGracePeriodSeconds bounds preStop + the app's own drain' — the sidecar is not in the sum.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Reproduced exactly. I read chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:194-221 in full — `rask.daprAnnotations` emits `dapr.io/enabled`, `dapr.io/app-id`, `dapr.io/app-port`, `dapr.io/log-level`, `dapr.io/log-as-json`, an optional `dapr.io/max-body-size`, `dapr.io/app-token-secret`, `lance.daprSidecarResources`, and a gated `dapr.io/config: "lance-tracing"`, and nothing about shutdown. `grep -rn 'graceful-shutdown\|block-shutdown' chart/` returns zero lines across the entire chart, so daprd takes its own default. The ordering is as stated: preStop runs only on the app container, daprd has none, so daprd receives SIGTERM at t=0 and is gone around the moment the app's 5 s preStop (values.yaml:512) ends and its drain begins. chart/values.yaml:509-511 verbatim names the budget it omits the sidecar from: 'terminationGracePeriodSeconds bounds preStop + the app's own drain'. The publish path is real: services/medallion/src/medallion/workflow.py:356-370 wraps `publish_event` inside `async with DaprClient()`, under a comment (:357-358) explaining the wrapper 'is unbounded, so a wedged sidecar hangs this activity forever' — which bounds the hang, not the failure. One mitigation the finding does not name and I will: because the sidecar's death also blocks the ACK, the trigger is redelivered after the 720 s ackWait (dapr-component.yaml:105) rather than lost outright — so the outcome is a stall, the same shape as finding 4, which is why med is the right grade.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~GET /api/explorer/search drives unauthenticated GPU embedding + cross-encoder rerank, with an unbounded query string and a cache a random q always misses~~</b> <i>(rate-limiting.md + websockets.md + cache.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26 — `dd1b648a` + `63a8d581`.** Rate-limited (slowapi, per route, one shared
> `scope` across the three entry points so they cannot be used in rotation for three times the
> budget), inputs bounded at the model (`q`/`q_vec` `max_length`, REJECTED not clamped — silently
> truncating returns results for a question the caller did not ask), and the cache key normalized so
> case and internal spacing stop generating free misses. Pinned in both directions: "harbour" vs
> "harbor" must still be distinct, since serving one query's results for another beats a miss only in
> the wrong direction.
>
> **The unauthenticated half is NOT closed here and is not this file's to close** — the audit files it
> as `X6`/`VS-13` against `open_python-audit.md`, and its own guidance says fixing it here pays twice.

**Rule.** rate-limiting.md: "apply to auth routes and expensive operations only"; and "Always include an ip: fallback so unauthenticated requests can't bypass the limit" — there is no limit to bypass

**Sites:** `services/search/src/search/api/v1/router.py:114`, `services/search/src/search/main.py:62`, `services/search/src/search/services/spec.py:59`, `services/search/src/search/services/encoders/reranker.py:60`

**Why it matters.** The mechanism is real but the finding's dedup claim is false: it asserts "no finding covers search's total absence of an auth surface", and open_python-audit.md files exactly that TWICE, at the same lines it cites — `X6` (HIGH, security, E1): "`search` is the only explorer service with no authn/authz code path at all — the chart's estate-wide OIDC/FGA env has nothing to bind to", sites `services/search/src/search/core/config.py:15`, `services/search/src/search/main.py:56`; and `VS-13` (med): "The search service has no authn/authz at all yet accepts a raw SQL `where` expression ANDed into every query". What is genuinely new here is only the COST layer — the per-request GPU forward passes, the uncapped `q`, the uncapped corpus fan-out — layered on an exposure already filed HIGH. Re-file as a med cost/quota addendum to X6, not as an independent HIGH. Note also that `search_get` is a plain `def` (sync), so it is threadpooled and there is no event-loop block; the finding does not claim one, but the framing "saturate the estate's GPUs" is bounded by the threadpool's 40 tokens per replica.

**Fix.** Gate first (`search` is the only media service without `make_auth_deps`, and viewer/annotator both have it — this is drift, not a design), then rate-limit: `@limiter.limit` on all three `/search` routes keyed by subject with an `ip:` fallback, tighter on the `rerank=true` path since it is the GPU-multiplying one. Independently, cap the inputs at the model where the cost is set: `q: str = Field(default="", max_length=…)` and `q_vec` likewise, and cap the `corpus` fan-out list length in the route signature — `_clamp_n`/`_clamp_rerank_n` already establish that this model clamps rather than rejects, so the pattern is in place.

**Evidence.**

```
`services/search/src/search/core/config.py:15`: `class SearchSettings(Settings):` — compare `services/viewer/src/viewer/core/config.py` whose `ViewerSettings(Settings, GovernedAuthSettings)` gives it `api/security.py`. `services/search/src/search/main.py:62-69` registers `register_handlers`, `install_problem_handlers`, `register_middleware` (CORS only), `probes_router`, `api_router` — no auth dependency. `spec.py:59` `q: str = ""` with only a `_strip` validator; `spec.py:69` `rerank_n: int = 20` clamped to `max(1, min(v, 200))`. `encoders/reranker.py:60-69`: `def rerank(self, query, candidates)` → `body = {"model":…, "query":…, "documents": [f"<Document>: {c}{_SUFFIX}" for c in candidates]}` → `self._t.post("/v1/rerank", …)`. `router.py:162` `for dataset_id in dict.fromkeys(corpora):` — de-duplicated, uncapped. Reachable at the edge: gateway row `("/api/explorer/search", "/api/search", *explorer_search)` (gateway/__init__.py:140) behind the Ingress `/api` rule. Related but a different mechanism: open_python-audit VS-03 files the viewer's missing FGA gate; no finding covers search's total absence of an auth surface or the unmetered GPU cost.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Auth absence reproduces exactly: `services/search/src/search/core/config.py:15` `class SearchSettings(Settings):` — inherits `service_kit.media.config.Settings` only, no `GovernedAuthSettings`; `ls services/search/src/search/api` → `dependencies.py __init__.py v1` — no `security.py`; `services/search/src/search/main.py:62-70` registers `register_handlers`, `install_problem_handlers`, `register_middleware(app, get_search_settings())`, `probes_router`, `api_router` and no auth dependency. (The comment at main.py:64-66 mentioning "The OIDC verifier raises lance_namespace's UnauthenticatedError" is inherited rationale — "Same installer the catalog has always used" — search itself has no verifier: `grep -rn "verifier|oidc|Bearer" services/search/src/search/` returns only those two comment lines.) Cost claims reproduce: `spec.py:59` `q: str = ""` with only a `_strip` field_validator (`return v.strip()`) — no `max_length`; `spec.py:69` `rerank_n: int = 20`; `encoders/reranker.py:60-70` `def rerank(self, query, candidates)` → `body = {"model":…, "query":…, "documents": [f"&lt;Document&gt;: {c}{_SUFFIX}" for c in candidates]}` → `self._t.post("/v1/rerank", body, into=RerankResponse)`; `router.py:162` `for dataset_id in dict.fromkeys(corpora):` with the docstring at :158 conceding "Sequential, deliberately … Concurrency here would need a bounded pool" — de-duplicated, uncapped. Cache-miss claim reproduces: `result_cache.py:53-58` `version_signature` opens `lance.dataset(...)` per searchable table on every request ("paying it on every request (hit or miss) is a good trade"), and `query_hash` folds `q`, so a varied `q` misses every time. Reachable: gateway row `("/api/explorer/search", "/api/search", *explorer_search)` at gateway/__init__.py:140, behind ingress.yaml:66's `/api` Prefix — note this contradicts the ingress template's own comment at chart/templates/ingress.yaml:20-23 ("there are deliberately NO root-absolute /api/explorer|/api/search rules: that side door let unauthenticated edge traffic reach the media services directly"), which the broad `/api` Prefix rule reopens through the gateway.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~The atlas points_cache bounds by entry COUNT while holding multi-MB Arrow payloads — the exact defect the search cache was fixed for, never carried across~~</b> <i>(rate-limiting.md + websockets.md + cache.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-26 — `4ce3a08d`.** The eviction is now one shared function
> (`service_kit.media.cache_bounds.evict_to_bounds`) used by BOTH twins, rather than the fix reaching
> one of them a second time — which is the actual defect the finding describes. Count is the lookup
> bound, bytes the memory bound. Also covers a case neither original loop handled: an entry larger
> than the whole ceiling no longer empties the cache on its way to not fitting.

**Rule.** cache.md: a cache must be bounded in the dimension that actually costs — the estate's own search cache states the rule ("a hit row carries transcript text, so 256 entries was a count, not a cap; #141") and the atlas cache still violates it

**Sites:** `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/atlas.py:51`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/atlas.py:126`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/media/state.py:56`

**Why it matters.** `points_cache` memoizes the full Apache Arrow IPC `/points` payload — coordinates, codes and keys for every projected row of a corpus — keyed `(dataset id, space name, table version)`. The key is correct (version-keyed, so a rewrite invalidates by construction, exactly like the search cache) and eviction is oldest-first. The bound is the problem: `while len(cache) &gt;= _POINTS_CACHE_MAX` with `_POINTS_CACHE_MAX = 12`, and the comment at :50 acknowledges "each is multi-MB" without turning that into a byte ceiling. Failure scenario: an estate with several corpora and several declared vector spaces — `available_modes` derives one space per declared embedding key, so `semantic`/`visual`/`scene` on four corpora is already 12 distinct keys — each projecting a few million rows. At ~100 MB per Arrow payload that is 1.2 GB of resident bytes in a pod the chart gives `replicas: 1`; the viewer container OOM-kills and every explorer route dies with it. The identical cache one module over (`search_cache`, same `AppState`, same file) already carries `search_cache_bytes = 64 * 1024 * 1024` alongside its count bound precisely because someone measured this; the fix was applied to one of the two twins.

**Fix.** Give `points_cache` the byte bound its sibling has: store `(payload, len(payload))` as `search_cache` does, add a `MEDIA_POINTS_CACHE_BYTES` setting beside `search_cache_bytes` in `service_kit/media/config.py`, and evict while `len(cache) &gt;= _POINTS_CACHE_MAX or total &gt; max_bytes` — reusing `result_cache.run_cached`'s two-bound loop rather than writing a third eviction policy. Also return an oversized single payload uncached, the rule result_cache.py:135 already encodes ("the cache must never hold a single object that busts its own ceiling").

**Evidence.**

```
`atlas.py:50-51`: `#: Max memoized /points payloads before oldest-first eviction (each is multi-MB).` / `_POINTS_CACHE_MAX = 12`. `atlas.py:122-128`: `body = cache.get(key)` / `if body is None:` / `body = build_points(declared, requested, ds)` / `while len(cache) >= _POINTS_CACHE_MAX: cache.pop(next(iter(cache)))` / `cache[key] = body` — count only. Contrast `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/media/state.py:57-63`, whose comment for the sibling field says "bounded by settings.search_cache_size AND settings.search_cache_bytes", and `media/config.py:69,72` `search_cache_size: int = Field(default=256…)` / `search_cache_bytes: int = Field(default=64 * 1024 * 1024…)`. Not filed in open_python-audit: `grep -n "atlas|points" open_python-audit.md` returns no finding on this cache.
```
