**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Reproduced verbatim. `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/atlas.py:50-51`: `#: Max memoized /points payloads before oldest-first eviction (each is multi-MB).` / `_POINTS_CACHE_MAX = 12` — the comment itself concedes the entries are multi-MB and still bounds by count. `atlas.py:112-129`: `def atlas_points(state: StateDep, space: SpaceParam = None, dataset: DatasetParam = None) -&gt; Response:` … `cache = state.points_cache` / `key = (handle.id, requested.name, ds.version)` / `body = cache.get(key)` / `if body is None:` / `body = build_points(declared, requested, ds)` / `while len(cache) &gt;= _POINTS_CACHE_MAX: cache.pop(next(iter(cache)))` / `cache[key] = body` — count only, no byte accounting anywhere. The twin asymmetry is exact: `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/media/state.py:54-64` documents `points_cache: dict[tuple[str, str, int], bytes]` beside `search_cache`, whose comment says "bounded by settings.search_cache_size AND settings.search_cache_bytes", and `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/media/config.py:63-72` carries the measured rationale — "Entries are NOT uniformly small — a hit row carries display.body (transcript text), so a full page of hits can run to megabytes; the byte ceiling below is the real memory bound, the entry count is just the lookup bound (#141)" — with `search_cache_size: int = Field(default=256, ge=0, …)` and `search_cache_bytes: int = Field(default=64 * 1024 * 1024, ge=0, …)`. The #141 lesson demonstrably was not carried to the sibling cache in the same AppState. Single replica confirmed (explorer.yaml:70 defaults to 1). Not in open_python-audit.md: the only atlas.py entry is VS-18 (bare `dict[str, Any]` return types at atlas.py:81/:218); `grep -n "points_cache" open_python-audit.md` → no hits. med is right — reaching 12 resident multi-hundred-MB payloads needs a fairly large declared corpus×space matrix, so it is a plausible OOM rather than a demonstrated one.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~Every object-store, S3 and Kubernetes leg in the estate is untraced — no botocore or urllib3 instrumentor exists, and 8 of the 14 apps open no span of their own~~</b> <i>(observability.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** `opentelemetry-instrumentation-botocore` and `-urllib3` are declared in `service-kit` and instrumented in `setup_otel` under the same `suppress(ImportError)` guard the grpc/aiohttp legs use — botocore covers every boto3 client the estate builds (vending, warehouses, records, `storage/client.py`) because boto3 is built on it; urllib3 covers the Kubernetes leg, which speaks it directly and no other instrumentor reaches. **DEVIATION on the manual-span half, and it is broader than asked:** the Fix names four routes (`drop_table`, `create_table`, `register/undrop`). Instead the span went on `catalog.services.native.call` — the single seam every spec operation reaches the backend through, where `method(*args)` IS the Lance/pyarrow/S3 work. That covers all 54 operations instead of four, covers a new one the day it is added, and stays on the right side of `observability.md`'s anti-pattern table: wrapping a route body duplicates the FastAPIInstrumentor span, while this is the business operation inside it. Pinned by `packages/service-kit/tests/test_object_store_is_traced.py` (12 tests, all RED before), including two that assert against a REAL in-memory exporter rather than grepping source — a grep stays green if an instrumentor is installed but never takes effect. The `instrument_lance_if_available()` extension the Fix cross-references remains open under its own separate finding.

**Rule.** observability.md: § Manual spans inside routes — only for business operations; § When auto-instrumentation is enough ("child spans from instrumented libraries … automatically parented under the request span")

**Sites:** `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/otel.py:106-161`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/tables.py:348-457`, `services/controlplane/src/controlplane/k8s.py:25`, `uv.lock`

**Why it matters.** The facts hold; the grade does not. This is diagnostic blindness (longer MTTR), not an open door, a leak, a production outage mode, or silent wrong data — the estate's own severity rubric puts that at med. Two sub-claims also need trimming: (a) `grep -o 'opentelemetry-instrumentation-[a-z0-9-]*' uv.lock | sort -u` returns NINE rows TOTAL (aiohttp-client, asgi, dbapi, fastapi, grpc, httpx, logging, psycopg, requests), not "nine plus asgi/dbapi/psycopg"; (b) the claim of "manual spans in five services (ingest, medallion, notifications, flows, maintenance)" is wrong — `start_as_current_span` outside tests appears in FOUR files only (notifications/api/fanout.py:130, medallion transform.py:518, medallion media_produce.py:110, medallion produce.py:91, maintenance sweep.py:286). ingest and flows never OPEN a span; they set attributes on the live Dapr-SDK activity span (`span = trace.get_current_span()` at ingest/workflow.py:1136 and flows/activities.py:59), and both carry comments explaining that opening a third span on the same hop would make the trace worse — a recorded decision, not an omission. The surviving core is exact and unfiled elsewhere: no botocore and no urllib3 instrumentor exists in the lock, boto3 is live at catalog/core/vending.py:195+251, catalog/services/warehouses.py:42, service_kit/lakehouse/records.py:59 and storage/client.py:73, pyarrow's S3FileSystem (objectfs.py:45) is C++ and unreachable from Python instrumentation at all, and the eight apps named open no span (verified per-service: gateway, compute, controlplane, catalog, lineage, viewer, search, annotator all return 0 files matching start_as_current_span|get_current_span).

**Fix.** Add `opentelemetry-instrumentation-botocore` and `opentelemetry-instrumentation-urllib3` to `packages/service-kit` base deps and instrument them in `setup_otel` under the same `with suppress(ImportError)` guard the grpc/aiohttp legs use. For the Arrow/Lance legs Python cannot reach, wrap the business operation instead: give `drop_table`, `create_table`, `register/undrop` and the viewer/search read paths a `tracer.start_as_current_span("catalog.table.drop")`-style span around the multi-leg body, exactly as `medallion/services/transform.py:518` already does. Extend `instrument_lance_if_available()` to viewer/search/annotator (see the separate finding).

**Evidence.**

```
services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/tables.py:348 `async def drop_table(...)` performs, in one request: `run_in_threadpool(protection.get_protection, ...)` (:371, S3), `run_in_threadpool(native.call, ns, "describe_table", ...)` (:381, S3), `run_in_threadpool(trash.put, ...)` (:409, S3), `run_in_threadpool(native.call, ns, "deregister_table", ...)` (:410, S3), `run_in_threadpool(protection.clear_protection, ...)` (:419, S3), `run_in_threadpool(maintenance_policies.delete_policy, ...)` (:428, S3), `await emit_write_event(...)` (:435), `await fga_deps.revoke_ownership(...)` (:450) and `await emit_control(...)` (:451). A `POST /v1/table/{id}/drop` that takes 8 s against a degraded RustFS renders as ONE `POST /v1/table/{id}/drop` span with one aiohttp child (FGA) and one gRPC child (Dapr) — six S3 round-trips and ~7.8 s are unattributed, so an operator cannot distinguish a slow object store from a slow FGA from a slow catalog. Same shape at services/controlplane/src/controlplane/service.py:42 (the already-filed CP-INGRESS-N-PLUS-ONE per-project k8s call), which is invisible for the same reason.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** otel.py:106-161 installs exactly fastapi, httpx, logging, requests, grpc (both variants) and aiohttp-client; `grep -o 'opentelemetry-instrumentation-[a-z0-9-]*' uv.lock | sort -u` → 9 rows, no botocore, no urllib3. The drop_table shape reproduces exactly: services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/tables.py:349 `async def drop_table(` then six object-store legs — :371 `await run_in_threadpool(protection.get_protection, ...)`, :381 `await run_in_threadpool(native.call, ns, "describe_table", ...)`, :409 `await run_in_threadpool(trash.put, ...)`, :410 `await run_in_threadpool(native.call, ns, "deregister_table", ...)`, :419 `await run_in_threadpool(protection.clear_protection, ...)`, :428 `await run_in_threadpool(maintenance_policies.delete_policy, ...)` — with no span opened anywhere in the handler. (These are the sanctioned run_in_threadpool pattern, correctly used; the defect is the absence of instrumentation, not the threading.) controlplane/k8s.py:25 `self._api = client.CustomObjectsApi()` drives the kubernetes client over urllib3, uninstrumented. otel.py:118-123 reasons about exactly this class for `requests` ("the uninstrumented half is the EXPENSIVE half") and boto3 does not use requests, so the reasoning was never extended — accurate. `grep -n 'botocore\|urllib3' open_python-audit.md` finds only SK-03 (an unclosed urllib3 ApiClient, a resource leak — a different mechanism), so this is not a duplicate.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~`_THIRD_PARTY_ALERT_GROUPS["lance-catalog"]` is a false claim, and it exempts the catalog's only alert from the phantom-metric gate~~</b> <i>(observability.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** `lance-catalog` moved into `_ALERT_GROUP_SOURCES` (`api/load_shed.py` + `core/lineage_emit.py`), so its one rule — the write-capacity ceiling — is checked by the phantom gate again. Both halves of the false claim confirmed first: `catalog.writes.shed` IS a first-party counter, and no `lance-catalog` rule references any `http_server_*` series. Source discovery is generalised from the `metrics.py` filename assumption to the instrument FACTORY CALL, as the Fix asks. **THE GATE FOUND A SECOND MISCLASSIFICATION THE AUDIT DID NOT NAME:** `ray` also claimed "Ray's own metrics", while two of its five rules query `ray_control_probes_total` and `ray_control_jobs_known` — both ray-kit instruments. That group is genuinely MIXED, which neither classification could express, so the exemption is now by NAMESPACE (`_EXTERNAL_SERIES_PREFIXES`) with a carve-out (`_OWNED_DESPITE_EXTERNAL_PREFIX`) that keeps a typo in `ray_control_*` catchable — rather than splitting an operator-facing alert group that runbooks and dashboards reference. **And a third bug, in the gate's own matcher:** its regex covered `counter|up_down_counter|histogram|observable_gauge` but not plain `create_gauge`, so ray-kit's `ray.control.jobs_known` was reported as a phantom. Fixed in all four alternations. Pinned by `test_no_group_is_called_THIRD_PARTY_while_a_first_party_instrument_emits_it` (RED against the original claims, and it names both groups). `make alert-rules-check`: 36 rules, SUCCESS.

**Rule.** observability.md: § Quick checklist item 6 (metrics an operator pages on must actually exist) — the estate's own gate for this is disabled by a misclassification

**Sites:** `tests/unit/test_invariants.py:4012`, `chart/alerting/rules.yml:52-67`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/load_shed.py:49-54`

**Why it matters.** The classification says the catalog "ships no metrics.py — its rules ride the shared HTTP server metrics that service_kit.setup_otel's FastAPI instrumentation emits automatically, not a first-party instrument." Both halves are false. The catalog's only rule queries `catalog_writes_shed_total`, and that IS a first-party instrument — it just lives in `api/load_shed.py` rather than a file called `metrics.py`, which is what the (grep-shaped) claim was built on. And no catalog rule references any `http_server_*` series (verified: zero hits in rules.yml). The consequence is exactly the failure `test_every_first_party_ALERT_names_a_metric_the_service_actually_EMITS` was written to prevent: because `lance-catalog` sits in the third-party map, it is skipped by that parametrized test, so a typo in the one rule that guards the catalog's write-capacity ceiling would pass `promtool check rules`, `promtool test rules` and every chart invariant, and simply never fire.

**Fix.** Move `"lance-catalog"` from `_THIRD_PARTY_ALERT_GROUPS` into `_ALERT_GROUP_SOURCES` mapped to `("services/catalog/src/catalog/api/load_shed.py", "services/catalog/src/catalog/core/lineage_emit.py")` — both create counters the group could legitimately query. Then generalise the source discovery from the hardcoded `metrics.py` assumption to "any module in the service that calls `create_counter`/`create_histogram`/`create_observable_gauge`", so the next first-party instrument outside a file named metrics.py is not silently reclassified as third-party.

**Evidence.**

```
chart/alerting/rules.yml:55 `expr: sum(rate(catalog_writes_shed_total[5m])) > 0` against services/catalog/src/catalog/api/load_shed.py:50 `_writes_shed = _meter.create_counter("catalog.writes.shed", unit="{request}", ...)`. Rename the rule's metric to `catalog_writes_shedded_total` and every gate in the repository stays green while the alert becomes unfireable.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Both halves of the classification string at tests/unit/test_invariants.py:4012 are false against HEAD. It reads: "the catalog ships no metrics.py — its rules ride the shared HTTP server metrics that service_kit.setup_otel's FastAPI instrumentation emits automatically, not a first-party instrument". But services/catalog/src/catalog/api/load_shed.py:49-53 is a first-party instrument: `_meter = metrics.get_meter("lance.catalog")` / `_writes_shed = _meter.create_counter("catalog.writes.shed", unit="{request}", ...)` — and a second one exists at catalog/core/lineage_emit.py:58. The absence of a file literally named `metrics.py` (`find services/catalog -name metrics.py` → nothing) is the only thing the claim is true about, and it is not the property that matters. The second half is refuted by the same grep as finding 2: no catalog rule names any `http_server_*` series. Consequence reproduces: chart/alerting/rules.yml:55 `expr: sum(rate(catalog_writes_shed_total[5m])) &gt; 0` is the group's only rule, and because `lance-catalog` sits in `_THIRD_PARTY_ALERT_GROUPS` it is excluded from the `@pytest.mark.parametrize("group_name", sorted(_ALERT_GROUP_SOURCES))` at :4048 — so renaming the expr to `catalog_writes_shedded_total` leaves every gate green and the alert permanently unfireable, which is precisely the `outbox_oldest_age` phantom the gate's own docstring (:3992) says it exists to prevent. Med is right: it disables one gate over one rule. Not in open_python-audit.md.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~No `server_request_hook` anywhere: `X-Request-ID` is minted, echoed to the client, and joined to no span and no log — and only 5 of 14 apps mint one at all~~</b> <i>(observability.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** `setup_otel` installs a `server_request_hook` and passes it to `instrument_app`, so `X-Request-ID` reaches the span of every one of the fourteen apps. In the INSTRUMENTATION rather than another middleware, which is the point: viewer, search and annotator deliberately run no `BaseHTTPMiddleware` RequestID pair — it buffers the response body and would break `/api/explorer` Range streaming — and `media/middleware.py`'s own docstring names this exact seam as the remedy, so the three apps that consciously traded correlation away get it back without touching what they traded it for. The gateway now mints the id (`RequestIDMiddleware`), so ONE value spans the whole trace instead of each service inventing its own; a caller-supplied id is preserved, not replaced. PII stays off the span: the hook reads an ALLOWLIST (`x-request-id`, `dapr-caller-app-id`) rather than a denylist, because the reference's rule is absolute and a header added upstream tomorrow must be excluded by default. Pinned by `packages/service-kit/tests/test_request_id_reaches_the_span.py` (9 tests, all RED before), including one that reads the attribute off a REAL exported span and four that prove no authorization/cookie/user header can reach one. The `make_lance_service_app` half the Fix cross-references belongs to open_python-audit DUP-12 and stays open there.

**Rule.** observability.md: § Request hooks (custom attributes from headers) — the reference's worked example is literally `if rid := headers.get("x-request-id"): span.set_attribute("request.id", rid)`

**Sites:** `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/middleware.py:42-47`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/otel.py:106`, `services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py:340`

**Why it matters.** The core is confirmed and the fix the finding proposes is the one the estate's own code already names — but leg (b) misreports the explorer trio as an oversight when it is a RECORDED DECISION with a stated reason. packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/media/middleware.py:1-8 is explicit: "Deliberately NO ``BaseHTTPMiddleware``-based RequestID/Timing middleware: ``BaseHTTPMiddleware`` fully buffers the response body, which would break the ``/api/explorer`` ``StreamingResponse`` Range streaming (206 partial-content video seeking). If per-request IDs/timing are ever needed, use a pure ASGI middleware that passes through streaming bodies, or wire it via OpenTelemetry's ASGI instrumentation — not ``BaseHTTPMiddleware``." viewer/main.py:102, search/main.py:69 and annotator/main.py:178 all DO call a `register_middleware` — the media one — so they are not simply unmiddlewared; they consciously traded the request id for streaming. That makes the finding's recommendation stronger, not weaker: the docstring points at the exact seam (`server_request_hook` on the OTel instrumentation) that would restore correlation without BaseHTTPMiddleware. Restate the finding as: setup_otel installs the FastAPI instrumentor with no hook, so the id the estate hands the caller reaches neither span nor log — and the estate's own middleware docstring already prescribes the hook as the remedy.

**Fix.** Add a `server_request_hook` in `setup_otel` that reads `x-request-id` off `scope["headers"]` and sets `request.id`, plus the caller app-id where present, and pass it to `instrument_app`. Mint the id at the gateway (`services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py`, alongside the header filtering at :340) so one value spans the whole trace, and move `RequestIDMiddleware` into the shared boot the nine lance apps also run (the `make_lance_service_app` that DUP-12 already calls for). Keep the hook to that: subject/email must stay off spans, per the reference's PII rule — the estate is currently clean there and should stay so.

**Evidence.**

```
packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/middleware.py:43-46 `request_id = request.headers.get(_REQUEST_ID_HEADER) or uuid.uuid4().hex; request.state.request_id = request_id; response = await call_next(request); response.headers[_REQUEST_ID_HEADER] = request_id`. Concrete failure: a user reports "the request that returned `X-Request-ID: 4f3c9a…` took 30 s". Querying GreptimeDB `opentelemetry_traces` for `4f3c9a` returns zero rows; querying `opentelemetry_logs` returns zero rows. The id is a dead token — the only path from it to the trace is guessing by wall-clock and route.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** `grep -rn 'server_request_hook\|client_request_hook' services/ packages/` → no hits at HEAD. packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/otel.py:106 `FastAPIInstrumentor.instrument_app(app, tracer_provider=tracer_provider, meter_provider=meter_provider)` — no hook argument. middleware.py:43-46 reproduces verbatim: `request_id = request.headers.get(_REQUEST_ID_HEADER) or uuid.uuid4().hex` / `request.state.request_id = request_id` / `response.headers[_REQUEST_ID_HEADER] = request_id`, and `grep -rn request_id services/ packages/ --include=*.py` returns hits ONLY inside middleware.py itself (lines 10, 31, 36, 38, 43, 44, 46) — nothing reads it. Gateway confirmed: services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py builds a bare `FastAPI` and calls `setup_otel(app, service_name="gateway")` at :285 with no `register_middleware`, and the proxy at :341 forwards `headers = [(k, v) for k, v in request.headers.raw if ...]` — pass-through only, minting nothing. So the failure holds: an `X-Request-ID` a user quotes matches zero rows in `opentelemetry_traces` and zero in `opentelemetry_logs`. open_python-audit.md files middleware.py only for DUP-20 (two `register_middleware` under one name) and SK-22 (CORS ordering) — different mechanisms, not a duplicate.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~Five governed collection listings filter against a single `fga.list_objects` call the OpenFGA server silently truncates — and mint their page cursor from the truncated list~~</b> <i>(pagination.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27 (owner-approved: option A, the flag).** `list_objects` gained `LIST_OBJECTS_SERVER_CAP` and now logs `openfga_list_objects_possibly_truncated` — the guard `list_users` has carried since it was written, and the asymmetry is how the unguarded half stayed invisible. It also returns an `ObjectListing(objects, truncated)` instead of a bare list, so the truncation travels to the CLIENT rather than only to an operator's log — which is what `pagination.md` actually requires. All seven call sites updated; a `NamedTuple` so the old `set(...)` idiom fails LOUDLY rather than silently producing a two-element set. **The carrier differs by route and is dictated, not chosen:** `tables`/`namespaces` return `lance_namespace` SPEC models, so the flag rides the spec's own `context` map ("arbitrary context ... custom to the specific implementation") rather than a new body field that would have broken conformance; `models` and the estate-bindings route own their envelopes and get a real field; `list_warehouses` returns a BARE LIST with no envelope, so it sets an `X-Authorization-Truncated` header rather than take a breaking response-shape change for a diagnostic bit. Same key name throughout. Pinned by `packages/service-kit/tests/test_list_objects_truncation.py` (7 tests, RED before). The `batch_check`-over-the-page rewrite the finding calls "better" was NOT taken — it inverts filter-then-page into page-then-filter and returns ragged pages across five routes; the truncation is now visible, so that change can be made on evidence.

**Rule.** pagination.md: anti-pattern 'page-size with no upper bound' inverted — an invisible SERVER-side bound with no continuation token; and 'Return `pages`, `has_next`, `has_prev` in the envelope; clients don't compute them' (the client cannot even learn the answer was cut).

**Sites:** `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/fga.py:542`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/fga.py:543`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/tables.py:190`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/namespaces.py:667`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/models.py:100`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/warehouses.py:268`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/warehouses.py:337`

**Why it matters.** The mechanism is real and correctly located, but it is a scale-gated silent undercount of the caller's OWN entitled rows — not an open door, a leak, or a live outage. It hides data from an entitled caller (fail-closed direction), and only past OpenFGA's 1000-object server cap, which nothing in this estate is near today. Grade med, alongside SKG-04, which files the same class of hazard on the two sibling paths. The de-duplication claim is CORRECT: SKG-04's cited lines (fga.py:547, :905) are a consistent ~80-line offset from today's list_users cap check (:627) and revoke-page-walk warning (:986) — i.e. SKG-04 covers the two paths that DO log, and `list_objects` is a third, untreated one.

**Fix.** Give `list_objects` the same treatment `list_users` already has: take a `max_results` argument, compare `len(response.objects)` against it, and either log `openfga_list_objects_possibly_truncated` AND return a truncation flag the callers propagate, or — better for a listing that is then paginated — replace the intersection with `fga.batch_check` over the page the route is about to return, so authorization is evaluated on the O(page_size) items actually being served instead of on an unbounded pre-fetch. The five call sites then check `page` rather than the estate.

**Evidence.**

```
packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/fga.py:541-543 — `async def _do_list_objects() -> list[str]:` / `response = await client.list_objects(ClientListObjectsRequest(user=subject, relation=relation, type=object_type, context=condition_context(context)))` / `return list(response.objects)`. Contrast fga.py:597-599 — `Caveat: the ListUsers API has no pagination, and the server truncates silently at its ``listUsersMaxResults`` / ``listUsersDeadline`` limits (OpenFGA defaults: 1000 subjects / 3s)`. Consumer: services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/tables.py:190 `allowed = set(await fga.list_objects(client, user=token.sub, relation="can_read_data", object_type="table"))`, then :193 `response.tables, response.page_token = _paginate(response.tables, page_token, limit)`. Failure: with 1200 readable tables, `GET /api/catalog/v1/table?limit=100` returns 100 rows and a `page_token`; paging to exhaustion yields 1000, not 1200, with no error and no flag — and because `_paginate` (tables.py:204 `names = [name for name in names if name > page_token]`) keysets over the truncated list, the missing 200 are unreachable by any cursor.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** CONFIRMED at HEAD. packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/fga.py:541-543 — `async def _do_list_objects() -&gt; list[str]:` / `response = await client.list_objects(ClientListObjectsRequest(user=subject, relation=relation, type=object_type, context=condition_context(context)))` / `return list(response.objects)` — one request, no continuation, no cap check, no truncation log. Contrast the sibling 60 lines down: fga.py:596-599 documents `Caveat: the ListUsers API has no pagination, and the server truncates silently at its ``listUsersMaxResults``… limits (OpenFGA defaults: 1000 subjects / 3s)`, backed by `LIST_USERS_SERVER_CAP = 1000` (fga.py:558) and `log.warning("openfga_list_users_possibly_truncated", …)` (fga.py:627). All five consumers verified verbatim as allow-list intersections: tables.py:190, namespaces.py:667, models.py:100, warehouses.py:268, warehouses.py:337, each `allowed = set(await fga.list_objects(...))` followed by a membership filter. `grep -rn 'LIST_OBJECTS_MAX|listObjectsMaxResults' chart/ services/ packages/ .docker/` → zero hits, so the server default stands. The cursor half also holds: tables.py:193 `response.tables, response.page_token = _paginate(response.tables, page_token, limit)` and `_paginate` (tables.py:204 `names = [name for name in names if name &gt; page_token]`) keysets over the already-filtered list, so a truncated row is unreachable by any page token. Not the async trap (all five are `async def` awaiting an async FGA client). No audit entry covers list_objects truncation.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~`GET /v1/table/{id}/history` takes an unvalidated `limit: int = 50` that drives one object-store transaction read per version — and `limit=-1` defeats the bound entirely, returning all-but-one version~~</b> <i>(pagination.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** `limit: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1, le=200)] = 50`, exactly as the Fix asks. `ge=1` is the load-bearing half: the implementation slices `versions()[:limit]`, so `?limit=-1` meant all-but-the-last rather than "no limit" — a bound a minus sign turned inside out, which is worse than no bound because the caller got a plausible answer. `le=200` makes the ceiling the route's own docstring describes actually true, and matches the sibling annotator route. Deeper history should get the keyset cursor rather than a raised ceiling, as the Fix notes. Pinned by `services/catalog/tests/test_history_limit_bound.py` (4 tests, all RED before) — two declarative on the parameter's constraints and two behavioural, asserting a 422 the caller can read rather than a silently clamped page.

**Rule.** pagination.md: anti-pattern 'Page-size with no upper bound (`page_size: int = 20`) — `?page_size=999999` exhausts memory and bandwidth. Fix: `Field(le=100)`'; and the `PaginationParams` rule that page params are validated at the boundary (`Query(ge=1, le=N)`), not clamped downstream.

**Sites:** `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/versions.py:55`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/versions.py:88`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/services/dataplane.py:1285`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/services/dataplane.py:1295`

**Why it matters.** The unvalidated parameter and the `limit=-1` slice quirk are both real and correctly quoted, but the cost is bounded by the table's ACTUAL version count, not by `limit`: `versions()[:limit]` cannot manufacture reads that do not exist, so `?limit=100000` on a 5-version table does 5 reads. The defect is therefore 'the declared bound is absent and unenforceable' plus 'the negative value silently inverts the bound', not a caller-controlled amplification. Grade med — the same grade the audit gave ANN-08, the identical shape in the annotator (one Lance snapshot per version), which at least caps at 200 where this caps at nothing.

**Fix.** Declare it as the estate's other bounded params already are: `limit: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1, le=200)] = 50`. The `ge=1` alone kills the negative-slice inversion; the `le` makes the docstring's stated bound real. If deeper history must be reachable, add the keyset cursor the sibling routes use (`page_token` = the last version number, `version &lt; page_token`) rather than raising the ceiling — the reference's 'cap with MAX_OFFSET; surface a use-cursor message' applies verbatim.

**Evidence.**

```
services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/versions.py:55 — `    limit: int = 50,` (whole signature, lines 48-56, declares no `Query`). versions.py:88 — `rows = await run_in_threadpool(dataplane.table_history, ns, so, segments, limit)`. services/catalog/src/catalog/services/dataplane.py:1285 — `versions = sorted(dataset.versions(), key=lambda v: int(v["version"]), reverse=True)[:limit]`, then :1287 `for entry in versions:` and :1295 `txn = dataset.read_transaction(version)`. Failure: `GET /api/catalog/v1/table/bronze$pages/history?limit=-1` against a 10 000-version table issues 9 999 serial S3 GETs on one pinned threadpool worker; the response either times out at the gateway or returns megabytes of commit log. The route's own docstring at versions.py:78-79 names this exact scenario as the thing `limit` exists to prevent.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** CONFIRMED at HEAD. services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/versions.py:55 — `    limit: int = 50,` — no `Annotated`, no `Query()`, no `ge`/`le`; the surrounding signature (lines 48-56) declares every other param as a typed dep. versions.py:82 — `rows = await run_in_threadpool(dataplane.table_history, ns, so, segments, limit)` (correctly threadpooled — NOT the async trap). services/catalog/src/catalog/services/dataplane.py:1284 `versions = sorted(dataset.versions(), key=lambda v: int(v["version"]), reverse=True)[:limit]`, then `for entry in versions:` → `txn = dataset.read_transaction(version)` serially, one object-store read each. `limit=-1` → `[:-1]` keeps every version but the OLDEST — verified by reading the slice, and the docstring at versions.py:78-79 (`limit`` bounds the per-version transaction reads — a table with 10k versions must not turn a UI page into 10k object-store round trips.`) states the constraint the signature does not enforce. Route is reachable via the gateway row `("/api/catalog", "", *catalog)` and is reader-gated (`require_can_get_metadata`). No audit entry cites catalog versions.py; ANN-08 is a different service and a different function.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~`GET /projects/{project_id}/tasks?include=details` fans out one Dapr actor round trip per task with no page parameter and no project-level task ceiling~~</b> <i>(pagination.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** `limit: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1, le=200)] = 100` plus a keyset `cursor` on the sorted task id, sliced BEFORE the `asyncio.gather` — so the bound is on the actor ROUND TRIPS, not just the response body. That distinction is the whole finding: `Semaphore(16)` bounds concurrency, not count, and sixteen at a time ten thousand times is still ten thousand round trips. `next_cursor` rides the response alongside `counts`/`total`/`may_publish`, so the tail stays reachable rather than merely slow. `include` is now `Literal["details"] | None`, so `?include=detials` 422s instead of silently returning the bare index. **`may_publish` is unchanged and pinned:** the actor computes it from the FULL index, so the publish precondition cannot move with whichever page the UI is reading — a page that changed it would be a worse bug than the one fixed. Pinned by `tests/unit/test_project_tasks_pagination.py` (5 tests, all RED before), including one that counts actual actor calls — a response trimmed to `limit` while still asking every actor would look identical from outside and fix nothing.

**Rule.** pagination.md: 'Pick the right strategy' — a list endpoint over a collection that grows needs a page parameter; and the `PaginationParams` rule that the bound is declared on the route, not left to the caller's restraint.

**Sites:** `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/project_events.py:527`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/project_events.py:532`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/project_events.py:547`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/project_events.py:565`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/projects/project_actor.py:196`

**Why it matters.** The route accepts `project_id`, `checker`, `subject` and `include: str | None = None` — no `limit`, no `cursor`, no `offset`. The project actor's `list_tasks` returns the ENTIRE index (`index = await self._load_index()` → `{"tasks": index, ...}`), and with `include=details` the handler gathers one `_task_proxy(tid).get()` per task id. The `asyncio.Semaphore(16)` bounds CONCURRENCY, not COUNT — this is the very site ANN-03 cites as the exemplar other routes should copy, so ANN-03 explicitly does not cover it, and its fix (gather with a semaphore) is already applied here. What is missing is the page. The send door caps one call at 1000 tasks (`items: list[SendItem] = Field(min_length=1, max_length=1000)` at :153, plus the `len(payload.items) * consensus_n &gt; 1000` check at :465), but that is PER SEND; nothing caps a project's cumulative task count, and `consensus_n` multiplies each sent item into several tasks. Growth driver: tasks accumulate one per sent item per consensus replica, forever, for the life of an annotation project. `include` is also an unvalidated bare `str | None` where a `Literal['details'] | None` or StrEnum belongs — `include=detials` silently returns the index instead of a 422.

**Fix.** Add `limit: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1, le=200)] = 100` and a `cursor` keyed on the sorted task id (the index is already a dict the handler sorts at :565, so a keyset over `task_id &gt; cursor` is one line), slice BEFORE the `asyncio.gather`, and return `next_cursor` in the response alongside the existing `counts`/`total`/`may_publish`. Type `include` as `Literal["details"] | None` so a typo 422s. The publish precondition (`may_publish`) is computed inside the actor from the full index and is unaffected by slicing the details fan-out.

**Evidence.**

```
services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/project_events.py:527-533 — `@router.get("/{project_id}/tasks")` / `async def list_project_tasks(project_id: ProjectId, checker: CheckerDep, subject: CurrentSubject, include: str | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:` — no page parameter. :547 `listing = await _project_proxy(project_id).list_tasks()`; :559 `gate = asyncio.Semaphore(16)`; :565 `pairs = await asyncio.gather(*(_detail(tid) for tid in sorted(listing["tasks"])))`. services/annotator/src/annotator/projects/project_actor.py:196-201 — `index = await self._load_index()` / `return {"tasks": index, "counts": _counts(index), "total": len(index), ...}` — the whole index, unbounded. Failure: a project that has accumulated 20 000 tasks over twenty 1000-item sends answers `GET /api/annotator/projects/<id>/tasks?include=details` with 20 000 Dapr sidecar RPCs in one request; at 16-way concurrency and ~5 ms per actor turn that is ~6 s of sidecar load and a JSON body of every task document, on the annotator queue page's primary read.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Reproduced exactly at HEAD. services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/project_events.py:527-532 — `@router.get("/{project_id}/tasks")` / `async def list_project_tasks(project_id: ProjectId, checker: CheckerDep, subject: CurrentSubject, include: str | None = None) -&gt; dict[str, Any]:` — no limit/cursor/offset. :547 `listing = await _project_proxy(project_id).list_tasks()`; :559 `gate = asyncio.Semaphore(16)`; :565 `pairs = await asyncio.gather(*(_detail(tid) for tid in sorted(listing["tasks"])))` — one `_task_proxy(tid).get()` per task id, concurrency-bounded, count-unbounded. services/annotator/src/annotator/projects/project_actor.py:192-201 `async def list_tasks` → `index = await self._load_index()` → `return {"tasks": index, "counts": _counts(index), "total": len(index), …}` — the entire index. The ANN-03 exclusion claim is correct: ANN-03's own fix text says 'Apply the pattern already present at `project_events.py:507`' (today's :559 semaphore), i.e. it cites this route as the exemplar and does not file it; ANN-03's sites are the send/publish/saga loops. Reachability confirmed: the zone calls it directly through its own door — frontend/microfrontends/annotator/src/lib/projects/remote/projects.remote.ts:184 `annotatorJSON(`/projects/${projectId}/tasks${details ? '?include=details' : ''}`)` — the gateway has no `/api/annotator` row, but the annotator BFF door reaches it, so the queue page does hit this path. The `include: str | None` typo-silently-degrades observation also holds (no Literal/StrEnum, so `include=detials` returns the index with a 200).

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~`GET /objects` drains the whole boto3 `list_objects_v2` paginator into memory — no `max_keys`, no continuation token on the wire~~</b> <i>(pagination.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** The route exposes S3's own cursor instead of swallowing it: `max_keys: Query(ge=1, le=1000) = 1000` (the protocol's own ceiling, not an invented number) and `continuation_token`, with `next_continuation_token` added to `S3Listing` — and to its TS twin in `lakehouse/src/lib/storage/storage.ts`, as the Fix asks, declared `optional` so a minute's deploy skew between the two sides cannot make the validator reject a valid listing. The body switched from `get_paginator(...).paginate(...)` to a single `list_objects_v2` call: slicing a fully-drained paginator would have satisfied a `max_keys` parameter while still reading the whole prefix into memory, which is the defect wearing the fix's clothes. Pinned by `services/viewer/tests/test_objects_listing_page.py` (4 tests, all RED before), one of which asserts the handler makes exactly ONE S3 call and fails loudly if a paginator reappears. An existing double in `test_viewer_object_authz.py` implemented only `get_paginator` and was updated with it.

**Rule.** pagination.md: 'Pick the right strategy' — S3's own listing API is natively cursor-paginated; consuming that cursor internally and exposing none is the 'unbounded fetch-all' the whole reference exists to prevent. Also the `Page[Item]`/`CursorPage[Item]` envelope rule: `S3Listing` carries no `next_token`.

**Sites:** `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/objects.py:227`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/objects.py:233`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/objects.py:250`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/objects.py:136`

**Why it matters.** The route's only query parameter is `prefix`; there is no `max_keys`, `limit`, `continuation_token` or `page_token`. The body iterates `paginator.paginate(...)` to exhaustion, accumulating every `CommonPrefixes` entry and every `Contents` key into two Python lists, and `S3Listing` (objects.py:136-145) has fields `bucket`, `prefix`, `prefixes`, `objects` — no cursor to hand back. `Delimiter="/"` bounds the listing to one level, which is why this is survivable in dev, but the levels that matter in this estate are exactly the flat ones. Growth drivers, all monotonic: a Lance table's `data/` prefix holds one object per fragment and nothing compacts it out of the listing (`services/maintenance` exists precisely because fragments accumulate); `_versions/` holds one manifest per commit; `_transactions/` one per commit; and the medallion cascade commits per stage per run. The three sibling findings on this file are all different mechanisms — VS-01 is `async def` doing blocking boto3 on the loop, VS-15 is `download_object` buffering a whole object, VS-10 is the `Content-Disposition` header — none of them is the missing page.

**Fix.** Expose S3's own cursor rather than swallowing it: add `max_keys: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1, le=1000)] = 1000` and `continuation_token: str | None = None`, switch from `get_paginator(...).paginate(...)` to a single `client.list_objects_v2(Bucket=..., Prefix=..., Delimiter='/', MaxKeys=max_keys, ContinuationToken=...)` call, and add `next_continuation_token: str | None` to `S3Listing` (its TS twin `S3Listing` in `storage.ts` needs the same field). The AWS token is already opaque, so this is the reference's cursor strategy with no cursor to invent.

**Evidence.**

```
services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/objects.py:228-234 — `async def list_objects(checker: CheckerDep, subject: CurrentSubject, settings: SettingsDep, bucket: BucketName, prefix: Annotated[str, Query(description='Key prefix to list under (delimiter "/").')] = "") -> S3Listing:` — `prefix` is the only knob. :243 `paginator = client.get_paginator("list_objects_v2")`; :250 `for page in paginator.paginate(Bucket=_registered_bucket(bucket), Prefix=prefix, Delimiter="/"):` with `objects.append(...)` inside and no break. :136-145 `class S3Listing(BaseModel):` / `bucket: str` / `prefix: str` / `prefixes` / `objects` — no token field. Failure: browsing to `GET /api/explorer/objects?bucket=rask-lakehouse&prefix=silver/features.lance/data/` on a table with 400 000 fragments materializes 400 000 `S3Object` models (~80 MB of JSON) in one response, after ~400 sequential ListObjectsV2 round trips; the storage browser page hangs and the viewer pod's RSS spikes with no request-level bound to stop it.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Reproduced at HEAD, with one stale detail in the evidence worth recording. The pagination defect is exactly as described: services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/objects.py:227-233 — the signature's only knob is `prefix: Annotated[str, Query(description='Key prefix to list under (delimiter "/").')] = ""`; no max_keys/limit/continuation. :250 `for page in paginator.paginate(Bucket=_registered_bucket(bucket), Prefix=prefix, Delimiter="/"):` accumulates `prefixes.extend(...)` and `objects.append(S3Object(...))` to exhaustion with no break. objects.py:136-145 `class S3Listing(BaseModel):` / `bucket` / `prefix` / `prefixes` / `objects` — no cursor field to hand back. NOT the async trap and NOT a duplicate of VS-01: VS-01 (blocking boto3 inside `async def`) has since been FIXED — the loop now lives in an inner `def _blocking()` returned via `return await run_in_threadpool(_blocking)` at objects.py:267 (commit df52e874, 'fix(async): the five confirmed blocking-on-the-event-loop defects'). So the residual failure is memory/latency on one pinned threadpool worker, not loop starvation. Reachable through the gateway: `("/api/explorer", "/api", *viewer)` rewrites `/api/explorer/objects` → viewer `/api/objects`; the route is browse-gated (`_require_browse`). The three sibling audit entries on this file (VS-10 Content-Disposition, VS-11 `_creds`, VS-15 download buffering, VS-22, VS-24) are all different mechanisms — none files the missing page. Med is right: the growth drivers named (a Lance table's flat `data/` prefix, `_versions/`, `_transactions/`) are genuinely uncompacted, though the specific 400k-fragment / 80MB figure is illustrative, not measured.

</details>

### Low — 28

<details><summary><b>~~The estate's highest-privilege router — raw OpenFGA tuple write/delete — is gated by nine hand-written calls instead of one router-level dependency~~</b> <i>(authn.md + authz.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** `router = APIRouter(prefix="/v1/access", tags=["access"], dependencies=[Depends(estate_gate)])`, and the nine hand-written `_estate_gate(...)` calls are gone. The gate returns `None` now; handlers that need the client take a separate `EstateFgaClient` dependency, so authorization and injection stop being one call every handler has to remember. **BOTH halves, as the Fix asks:** the router dependency fixes the SHAPE (route ten inherits the gate), and `tests/unit/test_access_router_gate.py` fixes the ENUMERATION by walking the router's actual routes — so a gate quietly dropped, or a route added to a different router, is caught by something that counts rather than something that reads. A third test pins that hoisting did not weaken the check: still `can_observe_events` on the FIXED root object, still 501 with FGA off, still fail-closed 503 when unwired. Three tests RED before. The refactor moved real behaviour, so `test_access_admin.py` was updated rather than patched around: the tests that asserted the gate THROUGH a handler now assert it on the gate itself — through a handler they would have silently stopped testing anything while continuing to pass.

**Rule.** authn.md § Protected routes: "Apply at the router level when *every* route in the group needs the same check — cheaper to read and harder to forget"

**Sites:** `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/access_admin.py:61`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/access_admin.py:145`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/fga_deps.py:573`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/fga_deps.py:578`

**Why it matters.** Correct as written and worth doing — hoist `can_observe_events` on `fga_root_object` into `APIRouter(dependencies=[...])` (or add a contract test asserting every `/v1/access` route refuses an unauthenticated/ungranted caller) — but it is a latent-maintenance hazard with all nine routes verified covered at HEAD, so it grades low.

**Fix.** Convert `_estate_gate` into a FastAPI dependency returning `None` and hang it on the router — `router = APIRouter(prefix="/v1/access", tags=["access"], dependencies=[Depends(estate_gate)])` — keeping a separate injected `FgaClientDep` for the handlers that need the client object, then delete the nine call sites. Do the same for `/v1/events` (one route today) and `/v1/stores`, whose gate catalog-api-02 already says is missing. Pair it with catalog-api-02's proposed contract test so both halves — the shape and the enumeration — hold.

**Evidence.**

```
access_admin.py:61 `router = APIRouter(prefix="/v1/access", tags=["access"])`; access_admin.py:145 `async def _estate_gate(request: Request, settings: Settings, token: CurrentToken) -> OpenFgaClient:` … :158 `await fga_deps.require_relation(client, settings, token, relation="can_observe_events", obj=settings.fga_root_object)`; nine separate handlers each open with `client = await _estate_gate(request, settings, token)`. fga_deps.py:573-578 `object_id = request.path_params.get("id")` … `if object_id is None:` … `# Other id-less routes (collection lists, health) need only authentication.` / `return`. Failure: a tenth `/v1/access` route added without the manual line inherits only the 401/503 floor — any authenticated project member could then reach it, and on a tuple-mutating route that is a total authorization bypass.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Every factual element checks out. `access_admin.py:61` `router = APIRouter(prefix="/v1/access", tags=["access"])` — bare, no `dependencies=`; `_estate_gate` at `:145` ends in `await fga_deps.require_relation(client, settings, token, relation="can_observe_events", obj=settings.fga_root_object)`; the nine `@router.&lt;verb&gt;` decorators are at :162/:291/:302/:308/:324/:365/:394/:435/:470 and `_estate_gate(request, settings, token)` is called by hand at :181, :261 (inside `_mutate_tuple`, covering both POST and DELETE /tuples), :315, :339, :378, :402, :449, :490 — so all nine are currently covered, exactly as the finding itself concedes. The router-wide `authorize` really does no-op here: `fga_deps.py:569` `object_id = request.path_params.get("id")` then `:573-576` `if object_id is None:` … `# Other id-less routes (collection lists, health) need only authentication.` / `return` (the finding cited 573/578; the lines are 569/575 at HEAD). I also confirmed no contract test enumerates the router's routes — `tests/unit/test_access_admin.py` asserts the gate per behaviour, never over the route table. But the finding states its own verdict: 'the defect is structural, not a live hole.' There is no request today that produces a wrong response, no leak and no outage; it is a hardening/maintainability change against a hypothetical tenth route. Under this estate's grading that is low, not med.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~The viewer's corpus-list filter fans out N single `check` calls where `fga.batch_check` is the estate's own pattern for the same question~~</b> <i>(authn.md + authz.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** `list_datasets` makes ONE `fga.batch_check` over the candidate set instead of one `checker(...)` per corpus. The old comment reasoned about serial-vs-concurrent, which is the right analysis of LATENCY and the wrong question — `asyncio.gather` makes N calls cheap, not fewer, and this is the first call every zone makes on page load. **Option (b) of the Fix, generalised:** rather than widening the `FgaChecker` Protocol (which is deliberately one relation on one object so a route cannot enumerate through it), the raw client is injected — but via `AuthDeps.get_fga_client` in `service_kit.governed.deps` rather than a viewer-local accessor, so lineage and notifications can converge on the same seam instead of each reaching into `request.app.state`. Fail-closed identically to `get_checker`: `None` when FGA is off, 503 when enabled-but-unwired. **The table-less rule is unchanged and still keyed on `settings.fga_enabled`** — my first version keyed it on `client is None`, which is the same thing in production and NOT the same thing under test, and a existing test caught it. Pinned by `services/viewer/tests/test_datasets_batch_check.py` (2 tests, RED before), one counting round trips because no latency measurement can. `test_viewer_dataset_authz.py` was updated, not patched around: the assertions about the object/relation/subject now run with authz ON, because with it off there is nothing to ask and they were observing a permissive call that carried no decision.

**Rule.** authz.md § Beyond `check`: "Prefer `batch_check` over many `check`s when filtering — same network round-trip cost as one call"; § When to use which: a filtered list = service + `batch_check` on the candidate set

**Sites:** `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/datasets.py:123`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/datasets.py:125`

**Why it matters.** `list_datasets` is the first call every zone makes on page load. It issues one `checker(...)` per corpus through `asyncio.gather`, so latency is fine but the round-trip count is N per user per page load — N OpenFGA requests over the SDK's aiohttp pool where one BatchCheck would do. The estate answers the identical question with `batch_check` twice already: lineage/api/fga_deps.py:259 (`DatasetFilter.visible`, "batch-checks can_get_metadata") and notifications/api/visibility.py:114 ("one round-trip over the whole candidate set, never one check per object"). The inline comment here reasons only about serial-vs-concurrent and never engages with `batch_check` existing in the module it already imports. Same family as ING-05, catalog-api-12 and F-LIN-13, but a different mechanism (concurrent fan-out, not a sequential loop) at a site none of them names.

**Fix.** Replace the gather with one `fga.batch_check(client, user=subject, relation=READ_METADATA, objects=[corpus_object(settings, s.id, t) for s, t in pairs if t])`, keeping the `table is None -&gt; not settings.fga_enabled` rule as a pre-filter. `FgaChecker` is deliberately narrow (one relation, one object) so it cannot express this — either widen the Protocol with a `batch` method in `service_kit.governed.deps` (which lineage and notifications would also consume) or inject the raw client for the filtering path, as the catalog does at tables.py:190.

**Evidence.**

```
datasets.py:118-125 `async def _may_see(dataset_id: str, table: str | None) -> bool:` … `return await checker(user=subject, relation=READ_METADATA, obj=corpus_object(settings, dataset_id, table))` / `permitted = await asyncio.gather(*(_may_see(s.id, table) for s, table in pairs))`. Failure: a registry of 40 corpora issues 40 OpenFGA Check requests on every `/api/explorer/datasets` load, per user — where `fga.batch_check` (already in `governed.fga` and used by two sibling services for the same relation) makes it one.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Reproduced. `datasets.py:118-125`: `async def _may_see(dataset_id: str, table: str | None) -&gt; bool:` … `return await checker(user=subject, relation=READ_METADATA, obj=corpus_object(settings, dataset_id, table))`, then `permitted = await asyncio.gather(*(_may_see(s.id, table) for s, table in pairs))` — one OpenFGA Check per corpus per page load. `fga.batch_check` exists in the module's own dependency (`packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/fga.py:475`) and two siblings answer this exact question with it: `lineage/api/fga_deps.py:259` `allowed = await fga.batch_check(client, user=self._token.sub, relation="can_get_metadata", objects=[...])` and `notifications/api/visibility.py:114`. The site comment (`datasets.py:114-116`, 'Checked CONCURRENTLY: a registry of a dozen corpora would otherwise pay a dozen serial round-trips') is a recorded decision about serial-vs-concurrent and genuinely does not engage with batching, so it is not a ruling that refutes this. De-dup: ING-05, catalog-api-12 and F-LIN-13 are different files and the sequential-loop mechanism; VS-02 cites `datasets.py:123` but for blocking Lance/S3 on the loop (already partly addressed — `pairs = await run_in_threadpool(_collect)` at HEAD). Low is right: `gather` already collapses the latency, so the cost is N HTTP requests to OpenFGA per user per page load, not N serial round-trips — a throughput nit, no wrong answer.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~An `http://` OIDC issuer in production surfaces as an opaque 401 on every request, and the "logged distinctly" the code promises does not exist~~</b> <i>(authn.md + authz.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** Both halves the Fix names. An `http://` issuer with `oidc_allow_insecure` false is now a startup `ValueError` in `GovernedAuthSettings._validate_governed_auth` — where a misconfiguration belongs, beside the invariants it already checks — instead of a per-request 401 indistinguishable from an expired token. Mirrored in **catalog's inline twin**, which `governed/settings.py` says must stay byte-identical: a rule enforced in one of the two is precisely the silent split that docstring warns about, and the catalog is the service that owns the tuple store. `_require_https` stays as the runtime backstop for `jwks_uri`, which comes from DISCOVERY and so cannot be seen at construction — and it now emits the `oidc_insecure_url` warning its comment had claimed for as long as the module imported no logging at all. The claim was made true rather than deleted, because the behaviour it described was the right one. Pinned by `packages/service-kit/tests/test_oidc_insecure_issuer.py` (6 tests, 2 RED for the real defects and the rest guarding the dev escape hatch, the FGA-off case and the https path). Two `services/flows` tests used an `http://` fake issuer and now use `https://` — the scheme was incidental to what they exercise (a missing verifier).

**Rule.** authn.md § Boundary error mapping / § Putting it together: a misconfiguration must be distinguishable from a caller verdict; `GovernedAuthSettings._validate_governed_auth` is the estate's own "fail fast at construction rather than fail open at request time" precedent

**Sites:** `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/oidc.py:114`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/oidc.py:107`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/settings.py:53`

**Why it matters.** `_require_https` raises `UnauthenticatedError`, which every call site maps to 401 "Invalid or expired token". Its own comment says the failure is "surfaced at verify time as an opaque auth failure but logged distinctly: an http issuer/jwks in production is a misconfiguration" — but there is no `log` call anywhere in oidc.py (the module imports no logging at all). So an operator who ships `LANCE_OIDC_ISSUER=http://…` without `LANCE_OIDC_ALLOW_INSECURE` gets a service where every valid bearer answers 401 with the same body a genuinely expired token gets, and nothing in the logs distinguishes them. This fails closed, which is why it is low — but it is the one place in an otherwise exemplary module where the comment asserts a behaviour the code does not have, and `GovernedAuthSettings` already validates the adjacent invariants at construction (issuer+audience present when OIDC is on; OIDC on when FGA is on), which is where this belongs too.

**Fix.** Two lines. Add the scheme check to `GovernedAuthSettings._validate_governed_auth` so an `http://` issuer with `oidc_allow_insecure` false is a startup ValueError rather than a per-request 401 — and mirror it in catalog's inline twin, which settings.py:4-7 says must stay byte-identical. Keep `_require_https` as the runtime backstop for the `jwks_uri` case the settings cannot see, and give it the `log.warning` its comment already claims — or delete the claim.

**Evidence.**

```
oidc.py:107-114 `def _require_https(url: str, *, label: str, allow_insecure: bool) -> None:` … `if urlsplit(url).scheme != "https":` / `# Configuration error, surfaced at verify time as an opaque auth failure but` / `# logged distinctly: an http issuer/jwks in production is a misconfiguration.` / `raise UnauthenticatedError(...)`. `grep -n 'log\.\|logging' packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/oidc.py` returns nothing. Failure: with `LANCE_OIDC_ENABLED=true` and an `http://` issuer, every signed-in user's request gets `401 Invalid or expired token`, the pod stays Ready, and the only signal an operator has is the same 401 a genuinely bad bearer produces.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Reproduced exactly. `oidc.py:107-114`: `def _require_https(url: str, *, label: str, allow_insecure: bool) -&gt; None:` … `if urlsplit(url).scheme != "https":` / `# Configuration error, surfaced at verify time as an opaque auth failure but` / `# logged distinctly: an http issuer/jwks in production is a misconfiguration.` / `raise UnauthenticatedError(f"OIDC {label} must use HTTPS (set LANCE_OIDC_ALLOW_INSECURE=true for dev IdPs)")`. `grep -n 'log\.\|logging' packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/oidc.py` returns only the two comment lines (:26, :113) — the module imports no logging and makes no log call, so the comment asserts a behaviour that does not exist. Worse than 'no signal': the raise propagates out of `_resolve` → `verify` into every caller's `except Exception: audit("authn", FAILURE, reason="invalid_token")` (deps.py:93-95, annotator/api/security.py:56-58, catalog/api/security.py, lineage/api/security.py), so the misconfiguration is actively mislabelled as a caller verdict in the audit stream. The construction-time precedent is real and does not cover it: `GovernedAuthSettings._validate_governed_auth` (settings.py:52-59) validates only issuer+audience presence and the FGA⇒OIDC implication — nothing about the URL scheme. Failure as stated: `LANCE_OIDC_ENABLED=true` with an `http://` issuer and no `LANCE_OIDC_ALLOW_INSECURE` gives every valid bearer a 401 'Invalid or expired token' while the pod stays Ready. Fails closed, so low is the right grade.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~21 routes across 7 services declare `response_model=` identical to the return annotation — the redundant form the reference reserves for when the two DIFFER~~</b> <i>(core-conventions.md + anti-patterns.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** All removed — **29 sites, not 21**: eight more had appeared since the audit was written, which is the argument for a gate rather than a one-time sweep. `tests/unit/test_response_model_is_not_redundant.py` walks every route in `services/` with AST and refuses any `response_model=` that restates its own return annotation, so the thirtieth cannot arrive quietly. The sanctioned uses are untouched and verified: three `response_model=None` (a different statement — it suppresses serialization rather than restating it) and the two genuine widenings where the wire shape really does differ from the return type (`gateway /healthz` returning a `JSONResponse` under `response_model=Liveness`, and `flows/routes.py`). The audit's own cost note is repeated in the test docstring rather than quietly improved on: this is cosmetic. FastAPI infers the identical response field from the annotation alone and runs the identical dump-and-revalidate either way, so the claimed per-row saving on the bounded listings does not exist. What it buys is that `response_model=` means something again where it appears.

**Rule.** core-conventions.md § Return type or `response_model`: "When possible, include a return type… **When to use `response_model` instead**: If the return type differs from what should be sent over the wire" / anti-patterns.md: "Return a Pydantic model AND set `response_model=` to the same class — Model gets constructed twice (validate + serialize)"

**Sites:** `services/ingest/src/ingest/api.py:58`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/api.py:365`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/api.py:423`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/api.py:469`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/api.py:554`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/queue_health.py:105`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/pages.py:140`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/stores.py:79`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/stores.py:87`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/stores.py:151`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/members.py:96`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/members.py:113`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/members.py:152`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/projects.py:69`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/projects.py:193`, `services/lineage/src/lineage/api/v1/endpoints/dlq.py:60`, `services/lineage/src/lineage/api/v1/endpoints/dlq.py:88`, `services/lineage/src/lineage/api/v1/endpoints/demo.py:135`, `services/flows/src/flows/routes.py:82`, `services/flows/src/flows/routes.py:88`, `services/flows/src/flows/routes.py:226`

**Why it matters.** Purely cosmetic redundancy: 21 decorators restate the return annotation, which the reference reserves for the case where the wire shape differs. Correct to remove for readability, but it is a style cleanup with no runtime effect — FastAPI infers the identical response field (and runs the identical dump+revalidate) from the return annotation alone, so the claimed per-row validation cost on the 500-bounded listings does not exist.

**Fix.** Delete the `response_model=` argument at all 21 sites and let the existing return annotation own the response field — no behaviour change, one fewer dump+revalidate per response. Leave `flows/routes.py:98` and the three `response_model=None` sites in medallion alone; those are the sanctioned uses. If the double construction on the list endpoints is worth removing too, the reference's other branch applies: keep `response_model=` and return the plain `dict`/list the handler assembled instead of constructing the model first.

**Evidence.**

```
services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/pages.py:140-147 `@router.get("/pages", response_model=PageListing, summary="Pages in a bronze page dataset")` / `async def list_pages(` … `limit: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1, le=500)] = 100,` / `) -> PageListing:`.  services/ingest/src/ingest/api.py:58-59 `@router.get("/sources", response_model=list[SourceDescriptor])` / `async def list_sources() -> list[SourceDescriptor]:` with body `return describe_sources()`.  services/lineage/src/lineage/api/v1/endpoints/dlq.py:60-66 `@router.get("", response_model=DlqBacklog)` / `async def list_dlq(` … `limit: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1, le=500)] = 100,` / `) -> DlqBacklog:`.  services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/stores.py:79-82 `@router.get("/stores", response_model=StoreRegistry, summary="Every registered object store")` / `async def list_stores(state: UserStateStoreDep) -> StoreRegistry:` / `    return StoreRegistry(stores=[...])`.  Counter-examples proving the estate knows the distinction: services/flows/src/flows/routes.py:97-113 `response_model=RunState` with `) -> RunState | JSONResponse:`; services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce.py:36 `response_model=None,` with `) -> dict[str, str] | JSONResponse:`.  `grep -n 'response_model' open_python-audit.md` returns nothing.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The factual half reproduces: I opened all 21 cited lines and every one names the same type as the return annotation — e.g. ingest/api.py:58 `@router.get("/sources", response_model=list[SourceDescriptor])` / `async def list_sources() -&gt; list[SourceDescriptor]:`; viewer pages.py:140 `@router.get("/pages", response_model=PageListing, ...)` / `) -&gt; PageListing:`; lineage dlq.py:60 `@router.get("", response_model=DlqBacklog)` / `) -&gt; DlqBacklog:`; catalog stores.py:151 `    response_model=dict[str, list[Store]],` / `async def stores_by_tier(state: UserStateStoreDep) -&gt; dict[str, list[Store]]:`. The counter-examples are real too: flows/routes.py:97 `    response_model=RunState,` with `) -&gt; RunState | JSONResponse:` at :113, and medallion produce.py:36 `    response_model=None,`. But the COST argument — the load-bearing part of the writeup, and the whole reason it claims a per-row penalty on 500-item listings — is false. When `response_model` is omitted, FastAPI's `APIRoute.__init__` receives a `DefaultPlaceholder` and derives the response field from the return annotation (`get_typed_return_annotation(endpoint)`), producing the identical `response_field`; `serialize_response` then runs the same `field.validate(_prepare_response_content(content))` dump-and-revalidate either way. Deleting `response_model=X` from these 21 decorators changes zero runtime behaviour and saves zero validations. There is also no reachable wrong response, latency or leak — the evidence rule's 'concrete failure' is unmet.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~`GET /{project_id}/tasks?include=` is a fixed-set query value typed as bare `str` — a typo silently returns the wrong response shape with 200~~</b> <i>(core-conventions.md + anti-patterns.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** `include: TaskInclude | None`, a `StrEnum` — which is what `core-conventions.md` names for fixed-set query values ("auto-documents as a dropdown in /docs and beats Query(pattern=…)"), and what the estate already does one service over with `InboxFilter`. The pagination fix earlier this session had closed the set with a `Literal`, which 422s a typo just as well — but it made this route the outlier rather than the rule, so the gate was tightened to require the StrEnum and the type follows the reference. The branch compares against the MEMBER (`include is not TaskInclude.DETAILS`) rather than the string: a StrEnum compares equal to its value, so the literal form kept working and would have left the enum decorative — a renamed member would have kept that branch silently correct against the old spelling. Pinned by `tests/unit/test_project_tasks_pagination.py::test_include_is_a_STRENUM_not_a_bare_string` (RED against the Literal). One member today, deliberately still an enum: a second projection is the obvious next request.

**Rule.** core-conventions.md § Constrained query values via `StrEnum`: "For fixed-set query values (sort order, status, format) use `StrEnum` — auto-documents as a dropdown in `/docs` and beats `Query(pattern=…)`"

**Sites:** `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/project_events.py:532`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/project_events.py:548`

**Why it matters.** `include: str | None = None` accepts any string; the body's only use of it is `if include != "details": return listing` (line 548). `"details"` is the single legal non-null value, so this is a fixed-set query value wearing `str`. The concrete failure is a silent wrong answer: `GET /api/annotator/projects/{id}/tasks?include=detail` (or `Details`, or `full`) returns **200 with the bare `task_id → state` index** and no `details` or `missing` keys at all — the caller asked for the queue UI's payload, got the publish-precondition index, and has nothing in the response saying its parameter was ignored. A `StrEnum` makes the same request a 422 naming the legal values, and renders as a dropdown in `/docs` instead of a free-text box. The estate already has 23 `StrEnum` classes and uses one for exactly this job one service over — `services/notifications/src/notifications/api/inbox.py:55` declares `state: Annotated[InboxFilter, Query(description=_STATE_HELP)] = InboxFilter.ALL`. This route is the outlier, not the rule.

**Fix.** Declare `class TaskInclude(StrEnum): details = "details"` beside the route (or reuse an existing enum module) and change the signature to `include: Annotated[TaskInclude | None, Query(description="`details` fans out to each task's own actor for the full document.")] = None`, then compare `if include is not TaskInclude.details`. No behaviour change for correct callers; a mistyped value becomes a 422 instead of a quietly truncated body.

**Evidence.**

```
services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/project_events.py:527-533 `@router.get("/{project_id}/tasks")` / `async def list_project_tasks(` / `    project_id: ProjectId,` / `    checker: CheckerDep,` / `    subject: CurrentSubject,` / `    include: str | None = None,` / `) -> dict[str, Any]:`; :547-548 `listing = await _project_proxy(project_id).list_tasks()` / `if include != "details":` / `    return listing`.  The house pattern it diverges from: services/notifications/src/notifications/api/inbox.py:55 `state: Annotated[InboxFilter, Query(description=_STATE_HELP)] = InboxFilter.ALL,`.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Reproduced at the cited lines. `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/project_events.py:527` `@router.get("/{project_id}/tasks")` / :528 `async def list_project_tasks(` / :532 `    include: str | None = None,` / `) -&gt; dict[str, Any]:`, and the parameter's only use is :548 `    if include != "details":` followed by `        return listing`. `"details"` is the single legal non-null value — the docstring itself says "``include=details`` additionally fans out to each task's OWN actor" — so it is a fixed-set query value typed as free text. The failure is reachable and silent: `?include=detail` returns 200 with the bare `task_id → state` index and no `details`/`missing` keys (those are only added by the :575 `return {**listing, "details": details, "missing": missing}` path), with nothing in the response saying the parameter was ignored. The house pattern one service over is confirmed: notifications/api/inbox.py:55 `    state: Annotated[InboxFilter, Query(description=_STATE_HELP)] = InboxFilter.ALL,`. DE-DUP: not in open_python-audit.md — the project_events.py entries there are ANN-01, ANN-05 (:368), ANN-19 (:499), ANN-16 (:158) and the N+1 finding at :457/464/467; none touch `include`. low is right: caller-side misuse, no data leak, no wrong data for a well-formed client.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~`get_settings()` is called directly inside a dependency and across service code, so `app.dependency_overrides` silently is not the seam — and 200+ test files monkeypatch module internals instead~~</b> <i>(dependencies.md + production-patterns.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** `get_namespace(request, settings: SettingsDep)` — the body call to `get_settings()` is gone, so `app.dependency_overrides` reaches it like every other route. Same for `on_control_event`, the Dapr control route in `catalog/api/dapr.py`. The two helper functions the Fix also names (`namespace_for_top_ns`, `assert_no_warehouse_bound_namespace`) already took `settings` as a parameter and needed nothing. **The startup-wiring call at `dapr.py` is left alone AND annotated**, which is the Fix's own instruction: it runs where there is no request and therefore no dependency graph, so overrides cannot reach it by construction — the comment now says that, so the monkeypatch a test needs there reads as a deliberate boundary rather than the default habit. Pinned by `services/catalog/tests/test_settings_di_seam.py` (3 tests, all RED before), the last of which drives a real request through FastAPI's own resolution — the only proof that an override actually reaches the dependency. The audit's honesty about scope is preserved: there is no current victim, no test is green on an unexecuted branch, and this is the kind of latent seam defect that is cheap now and expensive the first time someone trusts the override.

**Rule.** dependencies.md § Overriding dependencies in tests: "`app.dependency_overrides` swaps a dep at the registration key — no monkey-patching of internals, no global state"; production-patterns.md § Key decisions: "Settings — One `BaseSettings` per domain", injected via DI

**Sites:** `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/dependencies.py:152`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/dependencies.py:161`, `services/catalog/tests/test_transform_door.py:64`, `services/medallion/tests/test_promotion_resume.py:31`, `tests/unit/test_dapr_auth.py:102`, `services/catalog/tests/test_cascade_backfill.py:98`

**Why it matters.** The mechanism is real and unfiled (no `dependency_overrides` or `get_namespace` hit anywhere in open_python-audit.md; line 1517's `media/state.py:42` note is the sibling case, as the finding says). But the load-bearing evidence does not connect: `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/transforms.py` contains ZERO `NamespaceDep` references, so the app built at test_transform_door.py:59-61 (`FastAPI()` + `include_router(transforms.router)`) resolves `get_namespace` on no route at all — the claim that "every route on that app that resolves NamespaceDep still reads the real env-derived settings" is vacuous for the one test cited. And the stated failure is hypothetical: `grep -rn 'NamespaceDep|get_namespace' services/catalog/tests/` returns nothing, and test_transform_door.py is the ONLY catalog test that touches `SettingsDep` at all, so no test today is silently green on an unexecuted branch. The per-service direct-call counts are also off — `grep '= get_settings()'` over `src` gives medallion 25, lineage 6, catalog 5, maintenance 4, and 0 for viewer/annotator/search (they use `get_viewer_settings`/`get_search_settings`). What survives is a latent DI-seam defect with no current victim: low.

**Fix.** Give `get_namespace` the settings it needs through the injector — `async def get_namespace(request: Request, settings: SettingsDep) -&gt; LanceNamespace` — and drop the `get_settings()` call at :161; FastAPI already caches `get_settings` per request, so the routes that also declare `SettingsDep` pay nothing extra and an override now reaches both. Apply the same to `namespace_for_top_ns`, `assert_no_warehouse_bound_namespace` and `catalog/api/dapr.py:66,81`. For code with no `Request` to hang a dependency on (the Dapr workflow activities in medallion, which is most of its 26), that is genuinely out of DI's reach and monkeypatching is the honest answer — but say so at those sites, so the remaining `monkeypatch.setattr(..., "get_settings", ...)` calls read as a deliberate boundary rather than as the default habit.

**Evidence.**

```
services/catalog/src/catalog/api/dependencies.py:152 `async def get_namespace(request: Request) -> LanceNamespace:` … :160-161 `default_ns: LanceNamespace = request.app.state.namespace` / `settings = get_settings()`, followed by `if not settings.warehouses_enabled: return default_ns`. Against services/catalog/tests/test_transform_door.py:64 `application.dependency_overrides[SettingsDep.__metadata__[0].dependency] = lambda: settings`. Failure condition: a test (or a `dependency_overrides`-based fixture) that flips `warehouses_enabled` or `root` to exercise warehouse routing gets the real process settings inside `get_namespace`, so the binding lookup, the immutable-positive cache and the fail-closed deactivation 403 at :95-106 are never entered — the test passes green while the branch it names is not executed.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** services/catalog/src/catalog/api/dependencies.py:152-161 reproduces exactly: `async def get_namespace(request: Request) -&gt; LanceNamespace:` … `default_ns: LanceNamespace = request.app.state.namespace` / `settings = get_settings()` / `if not settings.warehouses_enabled: return default_ns`. It takes only `request` — no `SettingsDep` — while the alias sits eleven lines above at :31 `SettingsDep = Annotated[Settings, Depends(get_settings)]`, and `get_settings` is process-global: catalog/core/config.py:388-391 `@lru_cache` / `def get_settings() -&gt; Settings:` / `return Settings()`. So an override installed at test_transform_door.py:64 `application.dependency_overrides[SettingsDep.__metadata__[0].dependency] = lambda: settings` is invisible inside `get_namespace`. 82 `NamespaceDep` references across 14 endpoint modules would be affected if such a test were written. Violates dependencies.md § Overriding dependencies in tests ("`app.dependency_overrides` swaps a dep at the registration key") — the key exists and one code path routes around it.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~RequestIDMiddleware mints a correlation id that nothing reads: no ContextVar, no logging middleware, no log field, and the public front door does not run it at all~~</b> <i>(dependencies.md + production-patterns.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27 (owner-approved: finish the chain).** `service_kit/context.py` adds `request_id_ctx` + `current_request_id()`; the middleware sets it and `reset`s in a `finally` — the half `production-patterns.md` marks critical, because without it an id survives onto the NEXT request on a reused worker, which correlates the wrong things while looking correct. A `logging.Filter` on `setup_logging`'s one handler stamps `record.request_id` and the format carries `[%(request_id)s]`, so it reaches every module in every service — libraries included — which a `LoggingMiddleware` could not. **Beyond the Fix: `RequestIDMiddleware` is now PURE ASGI.** It had to be. Registering a `BaseHTTPMiddleware` on the lance plane would have buffered Arrow-IPC responses, and it is exactly why viewer/search/annotator had a RECORDED exemption — whose docstring named the remedy: "use a pure ASGI middleware that passes through streaming bodies".
