It rewrites only the response START message, never a body chunk, so that exemption is now retired and **all twelve apps carry one id**, verified by importing each. Pinned by `packages/service-kit/tests/test_request_id_context.py` (5 tests, all RED before), including one that proves the id does not leak into the following request. The audit's own scoping is kept: correlation was never absent — `LoggingInstrumentor` already injects `otelTraceID`/`otelSpanID` — what was dead was the REQUEST id specifically.

**Rule.** production-patterns.md § Middleware → Request-scoped data via `ContextVar`: "use a `ContextVar` … `request_id_ctx.reset(token)` — critical"; § Logging: the `LoggingMiddleware` that puts `request_id` into every record's `extra={...}`; § Middleware recommended order CORS → Request ID → Timing → Logging

**Sites:** `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/middleware.py:43`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/middleware.py:44`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/middleware.py:46`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/middleware.py:14`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/__init__.py:76`, `services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py:280`

**Why it matters.** Every factual claim reproduces and it is unfiled (open_python-audit.md mentions RequestID once, at :1485, only as an inventory item in the service-kit layout survey). But `med` overgrades it. Correlation is NOT absent from the estate — otel.py:108 `LoggingInstrumentor().instrument(set_logging_format=True, logger_provider=logger_provider)` injects `otelTraceID`/`otelSpanID` into every record and ships records to GreptimeDB, and `setup_otel(app, service_name="gateway")` at gateway/__init__.py:285 means the front door DOES root a trace even though it mints no request id. So the residual is a dead correlation id: two lines of `BaseHTTPMiddleware` that write a value nothing consumes, plus a response header a caller can quote and nobody can grep. That is dead code with an ops papercut, not an observability outage — low, and the honest fix is either to feed `request_id` into the log record (the deferral the docstring names) or to delete the middleware.

**Fix.** Either finish the chain or stop advertising it. Finishing it is small: add `core/context.py` with `request_id_ctx: ContextVar[str]`, have `RequestIDMiddleware.dispatch` do `token = request_id_ctx.set(rid)` / `finally: request_id_ctx.reset(token)` (the reset is what stops ids bleeding across requests on a reused worker), and add a `logging.Filter` that stamps `record.request_id` so `setup_logging`'s one formatter can render it — that reaches every module in every service without a per-service edit, unlike a LoggingMiddleware. Then register it on the gateway (as the only edge that can mint a chain-wide id) and on the nine `FastAPI(...)` apps. Stopping is also defensible given OTel already correlates: delete `RequestIDMiddleware`, and instead return the active span's trace id in a response header so a user-quoted value is one an operator can actually query.

**Evidence.**

```
packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/middleware.py:43-46: `request_id = request.headers.get(_REQUEST_ID_HEADER) or uuid.uuid4().hex` / `request.state.request_id = request_id` / `response = await call_next(request)` / `response.headers[_REQUEST_ID_HEADER] = request_id`. `grep -rn "request_id" --include=*.py services/ packages/ | grep -v tests` returns only that file. services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py:280-288: `app = FastAPI(title="gateway", ...)` followed by `setup_otel(...)` and a single `@app.middleware("http") async def lineage_sidecar_guard` — no `register_middleware`, no CORS, no RequestID (confirmed at runtime: the gateway's `user_middleware` list has exactly one entry). Failure condition: a user reports a failed `/api/catalog/...` call and quotes the `X-Request-ID` the service returned; grepping every fleet pod's stdout and querying `opentelemetry_logs` for that value returns zero rows, because the value was never written to a log record — and if the gateway itself produced the error, no header was returned to quote.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/middleware.py:43-46 reproduces exactly: `request_id = request.headers.get(_REQUEST_ID_HEADER) or uuid.uuid4().hex` / `request.state.request_id = request_id` / `response = await call_next(request)` / `response.headers[_REQUEST_ID_HEADER] = request_id`. `grep -rn 'request_id|X-Request-ID' --include=*.py services/ packages/ | grep -v test` returns SEVEN lines, all inside middleware.py itself (:10, :31, :36, :38, :43, :44, :46) — no consumer anywhere. The formatter really carries no request field: service_kit/__init__.py:65 `handler.setFormatter(logging.Formatter("%(asctime)s %(levelname)-7s %(name)s — %(message)s"))`. The docstring's own deferral is at middleware.py:13-15 ("Logging middleware deferred until structured logging / OTel lands") and its precondition is met at otel.py:108. The gateway edge claim holds: gateway/__init__.py:280 `app = FastAPI(title="gateway", version="0.1.0", lifespan=lifespan)` then `setup_otel` then one `@app.middleware("http") async def lineage_sidecar_guard` at :288 — no `register_middleware` call in the module.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~The Ray dashboard client is rebuilt per durable-workflow activity while the catalog client beside it is pooled, in the module family whose own comment cites the rule~~</b> <i>(dependencies.md + production-patterns.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** `ray_submit` pools one `AsyncClient` for the worker's lifetime (`ray_client()` / `close_ray_client()`), and the mover's teardown closes it beside the catalog client it already disposed. Both submit paths use it; neither constructs its own. The finding's caveat is what shapes the fix rather than being waved at: a workflow ACTIVITY has no `Request` and no reachable `app.state`, so the reference's literal "stash it on `app.state`" cannot apply — a module-level client with the worker's lifetime is the only honest equivalent. **KEYED ON THE ADDRESS, not merely cached:** an `AsyncClient` binds `base_url` at construction, so a plain build-once cache would keep answering with a client aimed at whatever address was configured the first time an activity ran. Free in production, where the address is stable; the difference between a cache and a stale global otherwise — and the estate's own tests found it. `close_ray_client()` suppresses and drops the refs either way, so a failed close cannot leave a stale client serving later callers. Pinned by `services/medallion/tests/test_ray_client_is_pooled.py` (4 tests, all RED before), including one that the CLOSE works — a pooled client nothing closes trades a per-call teardown for a permanent leak, which is the easier half of this fix and the worse one alone. Duplicate of open_python-audit MED-008, whose remainder this was.

**Rule.** production-patterns.md § Lifespan Rules: "One engine, one HTTP client, per process. Created in lifespan, stashed on `app.state`. Never `create_async_engine` or `httpx.AsyncClient()` inside a route."

**Sites:** `services/medallion/src/medallion/workflow.py:321`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/workflow.py:486`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/workflow.py:710`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/ray_submit.py:204`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/ray_submit.py:301`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/mover.py:57`

**Why it matters.** The code reproduces exactly, but it is a DUPLICATE of MED-008 — "Every outbound HTTP call builds its own httpx client — one connection pool opened and torn down per Ray submit, per catalog register, and per transcribed page" (open_python-audit.md:798/:1364, med/M, sites at ray_submit.py:107, :186 +2), with DUP-21 (:670/:1662, "Seven outbound HTTP call sites build a fresh httpx client per call") filing the duplication half. MED-008's scope is medallion's outbound HTTP as a whole, which is precisely these sites; the mover.py:57-61 pooling the finding cites as the contrast is best read as MED-008 partially applied, leaving the workflow-activity sites as its unfixed remainder. Set duplicate_of MED-008 and keep it short. Severity low is right (the agent graded it low already); the finding's own caveat that a workflow activity has no `Request` and no reachable `app.state` is honest and is why MED-008 is sized M rather than S.

**Fix.** Give the worker a client with the same lifetime the worker has: a lazily-built module-level `AsyncClient` in `ray_submit` closed on `WorkflowRuntime.shutdown()`, or one hung off the runtime object the mover lifespan already owns at mover.py:104-108. Either way, record the decision at `poll_stage` — a per-tick client on a durable poll loop should be a stated choice, not a default, given the comment forty lines away in the same package argues the other way.

**Evidence.**

```
services/medallion/src/medallion/workflow.py:310-324: `def poll_stage(...)` / `"""ONE status read. The workflow owns the waiting; this owns only the question."""` / `async def _read() -> str | None:` / `async with httpx.AsyncClient(base_url=settings.ray_address, timeout=settings.ray_request_timeout_seconds) as client:` / `return await job_status(client, submission_id)` / `return _run_async(_read())`. Against services/medallion/src/medallion/mover.py:61 `app.state.catalog_http = httpx.Client(base_url=_settings.catalog_url.rstrip("/"), timeout=_settings.publish_timeout_seconds)` under the comment at :57-60. Failure condition: a Ray stage job that runs for an hour with a 10 s poll interval performs ~360 connection setups and teardowns against the Ray dashboard for 360 one-line status reads, each also spinning and discarding an event loop.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** All three workflow.py sites reproduce. :310-324 `def poll_stage(ctx: WorkflowActivityContext, payload: dict[str, Any]) -&gt; str | None:` / `"""ONE status read. The workflow owns the waiting; this owns only the question."""` … `async def _read() -&gt; str | None:` / `async with httpx.AsyncClient(base_url=settings.ray_address, timeout=settings.ray_request_timeout_seconds) as client:` / `return await job_status(client, submission_id)` / `return _run_async(_read())`. :486 (`read_stage_failure`) and :710 (the train-poll twin, docstring "ONE status read of the training job. The workflow owns the waiting.") are the same shape. ray_submit.py:204 and :301 likewise open `async with httpx.AsyncClient(base_url=settings.ray_address, ...)` per call. The contrast quote is exact — mover.py:57-61: "ONE catalog client for the process, not one per stage transition … `fastapi` -&gt; production-patterns.md § Lifespan: build once, dispose once" above `app.state.catalog_http = httpx.Client(base_url=_settings.catalog_url.rstrip("/"), timeout=_settings.publish_timeout_seconds)`.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~Four lifespans build an aiohttp-backed OpenFGA client and never close it — the estate has the exact fix written down at a fifth site~~</b> <i>(dependencies.md + production-patterns.md + health-checks.md + health-checks.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** `service_kit.governed.fga.dispose(app)` — the Fix's better half ("make it un-forgettable rather than per-service") rather than the notifications block copied four more times. All five lifespans call it, **notifications included**: its correct block was replaced by the shared one, so there is one disposer beside the factory instead of five copies four of which were missing. It covers `app.state.fga` AND `fga_client`, so maintenance's different attribute name stops mattering. annotator and viewer had a BARE `yield` — nothing below ran if the app raised — so both are now `try/finally`, which also lands the X13 half the Fix says arrives in the same edit. **The genuinely unfiled sliver is fixed too:** the annotator's control-emitter `DaprClient`, a `grpc.aio` channel built in the lifespan and closed nowhere, is now closed beside the FGA session. Everything suppresses rather than raises, for the reason the original block gives — a shutdown path that raises hides whatever came after it, and a failed close has nothing to retry against on a pod that is leaving. Pinned by `packages/service-kit/tests/test_fga_client_disposal.py` (8 tests, all RED before), including that a FAILING close does not stop the teardown. Duplicate of open_python-audit SKG-07, as the finding's own correction notes.

**Rule.** production-patterns.md § Lifespan → Rules: "Always clean up after `yield`. Pools leak on shutdown otherwise." and § Graceful shutdown: "Order matters: outermost first (clients we initiated)"

**Sites:** `services/annotator/src/annotator/main.py:82`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/main.py:91`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/main.py:152`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/main.py:72`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/main.py:81`, `services/flows/src/flows/lifespan.py:87`, `services/flows/src/flows/lifespan.py:133`, `services/maintenance/src/maintenance/service.py:177`, `services/maintenance/src/maintenance/service.py:181`, `services/notifications/src/notifications/lifespan.py:176`

**Why it matters.** The code claim is exact, but the de-dup claim is wrong: this is a DUPLICATE of SKG-07, which already files the identical mechanism at package scope — "make_client returns an aiohttp-backed OpenFgaClient with no disposal contract, and seven of its nine fleet call sites never close it (only lineage and the…)" (open_python-audit.md:803, :1534, graded med/S). SKG-07 covers annotator, viewer, flows and maintenance; ING-11 is the ingest-specific child, not the only filing. The finding's sentence "Only ingest is filed (ING-11); annotator, viewer, flows and maintenance are not" is false. The one genuinely UNFILED sliver is the annotator's control-emitter `DaprClient` (main.py:91) — a grpc.aio channel that no shutdown path touches; a grep of open_python-audit.md for `control_dapr`/`DaprClient` returns only layout-survey prose, no finding. Regrade to low: the residual is one half-open connection per replica during a drain window that the pod's own exit reclaims — the same weight SKG-07's sibling ING-11 already carries (low).

**Fix.** Give each of the four lifespans the notifications block verbatim — `fga_client = getattr(app.state, "fga", None)` (`app.state.fga_client` in maintenance) then `with suppress(Exception): await fga_client.close()` inside the `finally`. For annotator and viewer that means first wrapping the body in `try:` / `finally:` (X13 already files the missing try/finally in the three media apps, so both fixes land in one edit), and adding `await control_dapr.close()` alongside for the annotator. Then make it un-forgettable rather than per-service: the four services that get it right and the four that do not are all calling one factory, so `service_kit.governed.fga` should own an `async def dispose(app)` helper, or the eight hand-assembled lifespans should share the `make_lance_service_app` the audit's E6 already names.

**Evidence.**

```
services/annotator/src/annotator/main.py:82 `app.state.fga = fga.make_client(settings.fga_api_url, store_id, model_id, timeout_seconds=settings.fga_timeout_seconds)`; its entire teardown is main.py:151-153 `app.state.shutting_down = True` / `for resource in (state.http, state.embedder, state.reranker):` — `app.state.fga` never appears after line 82. Identical shape at viewer/main.py:72 vs :81, flows/lifespan.py:87 vs the `finally` at :133-141 (which closes `app.state.http` and the workflow runtime and nothing else), and maintenance/service.py:177 `app.state.fga_client = await _make_fga_client(settings)` vs the `finally` at :181-186 (which closes only `dapr_client`). Reproduced at HEAD: `uv run python -c "...fga.make_client('http://127.0.0.1:9999','01','02'); del c; gc.collect()"` prints `ResourceWarning: Unclosed client session` and `ERROR:asyncio:Unclosed client session`. Failure condition: SIGTERM to any annotator/viewer/flows/maintenance pod during a rolling restart — uvicorn runs the post-`yield` block, the block returns without closing the session, and the pod exits leaving one half-open TCP connection per replica on OpenFGA plus an `Unclosed client session` ERROR on stderr during the drain window.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Verified at HEAD. annotator/main.py:82 `app.state.fga = fga.make_client(settings.fga_api_url, store_id, model_id, timeout_seconds=settings.fga_timeout_seconds)`; its whole teardown is main.py:151-153 `app.state.shutting_down = True` / `for resource in (state.http, state.embedder, state.reranker):` — `app.state.fga` never reappears. viewer/main.py:72 vs :81 is byte-identical in shape. flows/lifespan.py:87 `app.state.fga = fga.make_client(...)` vs the `finally` at :133-141 which closes only `app.state.http` and the workflow runtime, under a comment explaining only the ORDER of those two. maintenance/service.py:177 `app.state.fga_client = await _make_fga_client(settings)` vs the `finally` at :181-186 which closes only `dapr_client`. The disposal contract is real: `.venv/.../openfga_sdk/rest.py:155-159` `connector = aiohttp.TCPConnector(limit=maxsize, ssl=ssl_context)` … `self.pool_manager = aiohttp.ClientSession(connector=connector, trust_env=True)`, with `async def close(self)` at :161. notifications/lifespan.py:176-183 does close it, under the quoted comment. The annotator's DaprClient at main.py:91 `control_dapr = DaprClient()` is likewise never closed. Rule cited (production-patterns.md § Lifespan: "Always clean up after `yield`") is correctly applied — it is just already on the ledger as SKG-07.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~The shared Dapr-token guard raises a bare HTTPException, so the same 403 comes back as plain {"detail"} JSON in nine services whose every other error is problem+json~~</b> <i>(exception-handlers.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** `require_dapr_token` raises `lance_namespace.PermissionDeniedError`, so its 403 wears the same RFC 9457 envelope (with `code`) as every other refusal instead of FastAPI's unmapped `{"detail"}`. **`PermissionDeniedError` and NOT `ForbiddenError`, verified rather than preferred:** this module is shared by both planes and they install different handlers — `grep -c register_handlers` is **0** for catalog, lineage, maintenance and medallion, which install `install_problem_handlers` only, so a fleet `DomainError` would have fallen to the catch-all and answered **500** there. A `LanceNamespaceError` is mapped by both. `guard_actor_routes` renders it itself via the SAME `problem_detail` translator, because a `BaseHTTPMiddleware` raise happens outside `ExceptionMiddleware` and could never reach a handler — so the refusal is byte-identical whichever path produced it. The audit's correction is kept: `exception-handlers.md`'s carve-out IS "auth deps excepted", so this was an envelope inconsistency rather than a rule violation, and its audience is mostly daprd. Pinned by `packages/service-kit/tests/test_dapr_guard_envelope.py` (5 tests, 3 RED before). **One existing test needed its property restated, not deleted:** `test_ingress_refusal_shape.py` asserted `"status" not in body`, which problem+json cannot satisfy — it mandates a numeric `status`. What that test protects is that a refusal cannot be read as an ACK, and Dapr's ack vocabulary is three STRING verbs, so it now asserts the body carries no `SUCCESS`/`RETRY`/`DROP` and that `status` is the numeric code. Stronger than the original, and true.

**Rule.** exception-handlers.md: "Routes raise domain exceptions, never HTTPException (auth deps excepted — see authn.md)". The carve-out is for OAuth2/OIDC 401/403 mapping; this is an app-to-app shared-secret check, and the estate's own rule (service_kit/exceptions.py module docstring: "Routes raise these; never HTTPException directly") admits no exception for it.

**Sites:** `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/dapr_auth.py:93`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/dapr_auth.py:102`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/bronze_arrival.py`, `services/notifications/src/notifications/api/subscriptions.py`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/dapr.py`, `services/maintenance/src/maintenance/api/routes.py`

**Why it matters.** The code does what is claimed, but the rule-violation framing does not survive contact with the cited reference: exception-handlers.md's own carve-out is "auth deps excepted", and `require_dapr_token` is precisely an auth dependency, not a route. The finding's reading — that the carve-out covers only OAuth2/OIDC — is asserted, not sourced. The residue is a genuine envelope inconsistency, but its audience is almost entirely daprd itself (every guarded route is sidecar-delivered by construction, per the dependency's own docstring), which reads the status, not the body; only the public-front-door refusal at :93-97 is ever seen by a human. Site count is also slightly inflated: 18 non-test src files across 8 services (annotator, catalog, compute, ingest, lineage, maintenance, medallion, notifications), not 19 across nine. Cosmetic → low.

**Fix.** Raise `lance_namespace.PermissionDeniedError` (which every lance app maps to 403 with `code`) or `service_kit.exceptions.ForbiddenError` (which every fleet app maps), and let the installed handler render it. Given the module is shared by both planes, the honest fix is the same one SKG-05 asks for — pick one taxonomy for `service_kit.governed` and make every consumer install its handler — but even before that, either domain class is strictly better than the raw `HTTPException`, which no app maps at all.

**Evidence.**

```
packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/dapr_auth.py:93-97 `raise HTTPException(status_code=403, detail=f"{dapr_caller_app_id!r} is a public front door: …")` and :102 `raise HTTPException(status_code=403, detail="invalid or missing Dapr app-api-token")`. Consumers enumerated by grep: 19 files under `services/` referencing `require_dapr_token`, spanning compute, ingest, lineage, annotator, medallion, catalog, notifications and maintenance.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Code confirmed verbatim. `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/dapr_auth.py:93` `if is_public_caller(dapr_caller_app_id):` → `raise HTTPException(status_code=403, detail=f"{dapr_caller_app_id!r} is a public front door: its Dapr app-token authenticates the proxy, not the caller")`, and `:102 raise HTTPException(status_code=403, detail="invalid or missing Dapr app-api-token")`. Neither taxonomy claims it: `DomainError` subclasses `HTTPException` (exceptions.py:45) so the DomainError handler does not match, and `install_problem_handlers`' catch-all sits in `ServerErrorMiddleware`, which never sees an exception `ExceptionMiddleware` already handled — FastAPI's default `http_exception_handler` answers `application/json {"detail": …}`. Consumers (my own grep): 18 src files + 4 test files. Adjacent coverage exists in open_python-audit.md: `DUP-02` (HIGH) cites `dapr_auth.py:143` and its Fix explicitly proposes "having the shared helper raise the `lance_namespace` types" — i.e. the taxonomy question at this module is already on record, though for the service-door helper rather than `require_dapr_token`.

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~lancekit's catalog-error translator relays the upstream response body into the client-visible detail on the 5xx branch, defeating ns_errors' 5xx redaction~~</b> <i>(exception-handlers.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** The `ApiException` catch-all raises `ServiceUnavailableError("the catalog is unavailable")` and sends `exc.body`/`exc.reason` to `log.exception` — "never include exception internals in the response body — those leak via logs only". It was a 503 whose detail was the raw body of whatever answered, rendered verbatim because `service_kit`'s handler keeps 4xx-style messages, which routed around the 5xx redaction `ns_errors` exists to enforce; on the failure this branch actually catches the responder is usually an ingress or sidecar returning an HTML error page. **The four 4xx branches are untouched, and three are pinned so they stay that way** — there the upstream body IS the catalog's own redacted problem+json, and relaying it is what turns an opaque 500 into the answer the direct path gives. A blanket redaction would have been the easy over-correction and would have undone a recorded, correct decision. Pinned by `packages/service-kit/tests/test_catalog_error_relay.py` (4 tests, 1 RED for the defect and 3 guarding what must not change).

**Rule.** exception-handlers.md: "Never include exception internals in the response body — those leak via logs only"

**Sites:** `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/lancekit/reader.py:326`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/lancekit/reader.py:312`

**Why it matters.** `ns_errors.problem_detail` goes out of its way to replace `str(exc)` with a fixed "Internal Server Error" for every 5xx precisely so paths, DSNs and driver text cannot reach a client; `_UNREDACTED_5XX` is a one-element frozenset and its comment explains why 501 is the only member. This translator routes around that: `ServiceUnavailableError(f"catalog unavailable: {exc.body or exc.reason}")` is a 503 whose detail is the raw body of whatever answered — and on the failure mode this branch actually catches (the catalog is down), the responder is usually not the catalog but an ingress or sidecar returning an HTML error page or a proxy diagnostic. That text is then served to the browser as the `detail` of a `service_kit` problem body, which keeps 4xx-style messages verbatim. The module comment ("The catalog's own problem detail rides in the message") is a real and defensible decision for the 4xx branches, where the upstream body is itself a redacted problem+json; it does not hold for the catch-all `ApiException` branch, which is the one that fires when the upstream is not the catalog.

**Fix.** Give the `ApiException` branch a stable message ("the catalog is unavailable") and log `exc.body`/`exc.reason` with `log.exception`. Leave the four 4xx branches as they are — their rationale is recorded and correct.

**Evidence.**

```
packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/lancekit/reader.py:325-326 `except _api_exc.ApiException as exc:` / `raise ServiceUnavailableError(f"catalog unavailable: {exc.body or exc.reason}") from exc`; contrast packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/lakehouse/ns_errors.py:99 `detail = str(exc) if status < 500 or status in _UNREDACTED_5XX else "Internal Server Error"`.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Verified at HEAD. `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/lancekit/reader.py:325-326`: `except _api_exc.ApiException as exc:` / `raise ServiceUnavailableError(f"catalog unavailable: {exc.body or exc.reason}") from exc`. `ServiceUnavailableError` here is `service_kit.exceptions`', whose handler builds `"detail": str(exc)` with no status-dependent redaction (exceptions.py:96-102), so the relayed upstream body reaches the client — while `lakehouse/ns_errors.py:98` deliberately does `detail = str(exc) if status &lt; 500 or status in _UNREDACTED_5XX else "Internal Server Error"`, with `_UNREDACTED_5XX = frozenset({501})` and a comment explaining that 501 is the only member because "Every other 5xx stays redacted: those ARE faults, and their text leaks." The finding's distinction between the 4xx branches (where the docstring's "The catalog's own problem detail rides in the message" is a defensible recorded decision) and the catch-all is fair. One correction to add: the same `exceptions.py` docstring rule ("never interpolate a raw upstream exception into it") condemns ALL seven branches at :311-326, not only :326 — the finding under-scopes rather than over-scopes. Low is right: the relayed text is an in-cluster ingress/sidecar body, the caller is already authenticated, and no credential material transits this path. Not filed (open_python-audit.md's reader.py entries are SK-03 :348 resource-leak and SK-04 :262 substring classification).

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~draining.py stamps content-type: application/problem+json on a body that is only {"detail": …} — the three medallion write doors lie about their 503's shape~~</b> <i>(exception-handlers.md + rate-limiting.md + websockets.md + cache.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** `refuse_when_draining` raises `ServiceUnavailableError(..., headers={"Retry-After": …})` and the handler supplies the envelope; the `Content-Type` entry that renamed the body without changing it is gone. The docstring's reason for `HTTPException` — "an exception handler would have to reconstruct" the header — was true when written and expired earlier this session, when `DomainError` gained `headers`. **THE TRAP THIS UNCOVERED is why the fix is not one line:** `DomainError` subclasses `HTTPException`, so an app installing only `install_problem_handlers` renders it through starlette's built-in handler — 503 and `Retry-After` intact, `{"detail"}` body again — and that was **every lance app**, including the three medallion doors this finding names. So catalog, lineage, maintenance, medallion producer and mover now install `register_handlers` too, in the same order `make_service_app` uses so the lance translator still wins for `RequestValidationError`. Pinned by `packages/service-kit/tests/test_draining_envelope.py` (10 tests, RED before), including one per app asserting it maps domain errors at all — the half a unit test on the dependency cannot see. `Retry-After` is pinned separately, since it is what the original design was protecting and the easiest thing to lose.

**Rule.** exception-handlers.md: "media_type=\"application/problem+json\" on every Problem-shaped response" — the media type asserts the shape; here it is asserted over the FastAPI HTTPException shape

**Sites:** `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/draining.py:69`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce.py:39`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/ingest_media.py:27`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/train.py:73`

**Why it matters.** Mechanism reproduced exactly, but graded too high. The status code (503) and `Retry-After: 30` — the two things a retrying caller acts on — are both correct and present; only the body's key set contradicts its declared media type. No retry path breaks, nothing leaks, and the window is a rolling upgrade. This is an envelope-labelling defect, i.e. low. The fix is already sitting in the same module: `draining.py:88 problem_response(detail)` builds `{"type","title","status","detail"}` with the media type set properly, so `refuse_when_draining` should raise a `ServiceUnavailableError`-shaped body or the dependency should be converted to that form.

**Fix.** Stop routing this through `HTTPException`. `draining.py` already exports `problem_response` (`__all__`, :97); return/raise a real problem body: either raise `service_kit.exceptions.ServiceUnavailableError` and give its handler the `Retry-After` header, or — since a dependency cannot return a response — raise a small `DrainingError(ServiceUnavailableError)` with a registered handler that emits `{type,title,status,detail}` plus `Retry-After`. Remove the `Content-Type` entry from `exc.headers` either way; a header that renames a body without changing it is worse than no header.

**Evidence.**

```
packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/draining.py:69-72 `raise HTTPException(status_code=503, detail="this instance is shutting down …", headers={"Retry-After": str(RETRY_AFTER_SECONDS), "Content-Type": _PROBLEM_JSON})`. Reproduced on a real app with `install_problem_handlers` installed and `refuse_when_draining` as a route dependency: `draining: 503 application/problem+json 30 {"detail":"this instance is shutting down and is not accepting new runs — retry, another replica will serve it"}`.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** CONFIRMED at HEAD. `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/draining.py:69-72`: `raise HTTPException(status_code=503, detail="this instance is shutting down and is not accepting new runs — retry, another replica will serve it", headers={"Retry-After": str(RETRY_AFTER_SECONDS), "Content-Type": _PROBLEM_JSON})`. Reproduced on a real app with `install_problem_handlers` installed and the dependency on the route with `app.state.shutting_down = True`: `draining: 503 application/problem+json 30 {"detail":"this instance is shutting down and is not accepting new runs — retry, another replica will serve it"}` — Starlette's `init_headers` does not overwrite the caller-supplied content-type, so the label really does go out over the FastAPI `{"detail"}` shape. All three cited doors carry it: `produce.py:39`, `ingest_media.py:27`, `train.py:73`, each `dependencies=[Depends(refuse_when_draining)]`. Not filed in open_python-audit.md (grep 'draining' → no hits).

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~`GET /api/page` stamps `Content-Type: image/jpeg` on an opaque governed payload — the tier schema carries no MIME and the table is a free query parameter~~</b> <i>(file-handling.md + streaming.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** `sniff_media_type(payload)` reads magic bytes and defaults to `application/octet-stream` — evidence where there is any, an honest admission where there is not. A guess is worse than an admission: "bytes I cannot describe" is true, `image/jpeg` over a WAV is not. **The load-bearing half the finding names is done too:** `has_image` → `has_payload`, and the modality vocabulary ("image bytes", "harvest produced none") is gone from a seam that serves an ARBITRARY governed table — CLAUDE.md's test for a shared seam is "would this be right for audio?". `has_image` survives one release as a `computed_field` MIRROR (not a default, so the two cannot disagree) because web zones are separate Deployments from the viewer: a rolling upgrade pairs an old `rask-web-lakehouse` with a new viewer, and `storage.ts` reads the old name. The lakehouse zone reads `has_payload ?? has_image` for the same window. Pinned by `services/viewer/tests/test_page_bytes_mime.py` (8 tests, RED before) across jpeg/png/pdf/wav/unknown/empty. One existing test's fixture was the literal string `b"JPEGBYTES"` — with sniffing it would correctly serve `octet-stream`, so it now carries real JPEG magic bytes and its content-type assertion tests the ROUTE rather than the fixture.

**Rule.** file-handling.md § Anti-patterns ("media_type=... for previewable content — set the real MIME type"); the reference's whole premise that a byte response declares what it actually is

**Sites:** `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/pages.py:199`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/pages.py:41`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/pages.py:174`

**Why it matters.** The hardcode is real and there genuinely is no mime to read, but the graded impact is a mis-rendered preview, not a med-grade file-handling defect: the bytes are correct, the route is FGA-gated (unlike the media routes), and no client is known to dispatch on this header. The load-bearing half of the finding is the OTHER one it mentions in passing — `get_page`/`list_pages` assert a modality (`image/jpeg`, `has_image`, the "harvest" vocabulary) in a service seam over an arbitrary caller-supplied catalog table, which is the platform-knows-no-workload ruling, not file-handling.md. File it as: default to `application/octet-stream` (the sibling's honest default) and drop the modality vocabulary.

**Fix.** Sniff or declare, then default honestly. Either read a mime from the descriptor the way `media.py:_cell_value` does, or fall back to `application/octet-stream` rather than a guess, and use `mimetypes`/a magic-byte probe on the first bytes when the table declares nothing. Rename `has_image`/`Page.has_image` to a modality-free name (`has_payload`) in the same change, since it is the same assumption in the listing model.

**Evidence.**

```
pages.py:196-200 — `return Response(` / `content=payload,` / `media_type="image/jpeg",` / `headers={"Cache-Control": "public, max-age=300"},`. pages.py:41 — `_PAGE_COLUMNS = ["id", "source_uri", "stage"]` (no mime column). pages.py:174 — `table: Annotated[str, Query(description="Catalog table id, e.g. bronze$pages.")]`. Contrast media.py:319 — `mime = _cell_value(ds, doc_key, doc_id, binding.mime, "application/octet-stream")`.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Reproduced at HEAD. pages.py:173-179 `async def get_page(state, checker, subject, token, table: Annotated[str, Query(description="Catalog table id, e.g. bronze$pages.")], page_id: ...)` — `table` is free-form and only FGA-checked (`_authorized_dataset(..., relation=READ_DATA)` at :186), so any readable table is addressable. pages.py:196-200 `return Response(` / `content=payload,` / `media_type="image/jpeg",` / `headers={"Cache-Control": "public, max-age=300"},`. pages.py:41 `_PAGE_COLUMNS = ["id", "source_uri", "stage"]` — confirmed, no mime column is projected, and the governed tier row is `{id, payload, stage, lineage, source_rowid}` so none exists to project. The contrast is real: media.py:319 `mime = _cell_value(ds, doc_key, doc_id, binding.mime, "application/octet-stream")`, and even the thumbnail route reads a declared column first (media.py:187 `_cell_value(..., binding.thumbnail_mime, "image/jpeg")`). Also confirmed: `has_image=payload is not None` at pages.py:165 and the "harvest produced none" 404 text at pages.py:195. Not filed in open_python-audit.md (grep for 'image/jpeg' and 'mime' over the audit returns nothing).

</details>

<details><summary><b>~~A cached clip can be unlinked between the handler's `exists()` check and Starlette's `os.stat`, turning a valid `/api/media-clip` request into a 500 — and the cache evicts the hottest entry first~~</b> <i>(file-handling.md + streaming.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

> **CLOSED 2026-08-27.** The route hands `FileResponse` the `stat_result` it already took, so starlette does not stat a second time and a concurrent eviction cannot turn a valid request into a 500 — on Linux the inode outlives the unlink for an open handle, so the bytes are still served. An already-missing file falls through to starlette's own handling, because that is a genuine 404 rather than the race. **The audit's correction is honoured: the "hottest entry first" claim is dropped.** `evict_old_clips` was plain FIFO, and the real point is that it was FIFO *because reads never touched mtime* — `_touch` on a cache hit makes mtime mean "last served" and turns the sort that was already there into a true LRU, with no second index to keep in step. The same one-line defect in `atlas.py` is fixed with it: a points-cache hit now moves the key, so `evict_to_bounds` drops the least-recently-USED rather than the oldest-inserted. Pinned by `services/viewer/tests/test_clip_cache_race.py` (5 tests, 4 RED before), one of which proves `stat_result` actually defeats an unlink mid-request.

**Rule.** file-handling.md § Temp files & guaranteed cleanup (the cleanup must not race the stream it is cleaning up around); § Downloads from disk — FileResponse

**Sites:** `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/media.py:301`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/services/clips.py:45`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/services/clips.py:103`

**Why it matters.** The TOCTOU→500 half is exact and stands. The second half of the title does not: `evict_old_clips` sorts by mtime and drops the OLDEST-CREATED beyond 50 — that is not "the hottest entry first", it is plain FIFO. The real (smaller) point is that it is FIFO rather than LRU because reads never touch mtime, so a long-lived hot clip is eventually evicted on age alone and pays another 120 s-capped ffmpeg transcode. Drop the 'hottest first' claim; keep the race, which is the defect worth fixing (pass `stat_result` / catch FileNotFoundError and rebuild).

**Fix.** In `build_clip`, `os.utime(out)` on a cache hit so mtime means "last served" and the sort becomes a real LRU; and pass the already-taken `stat_result` into `FileResponse(path, stat_result=..., ...)` so a concurrent unlink cannot turn a served response into a 500 (Linux keeps the inode alive for the open handle). Same one-line LRU fix for `atlas.py:126` — move the key to the end on a hit before evicting from the front.

**Evidence.**

```
media.py:301-302 — `path = build_clip(source, f"{handle.id}--{doc_id}", lo, hi)` / `return FileResponse(path, media_type=mime, headers={"Cache-Control": "no-store"})`. clips.py:45-49 — `def evict_old_clips(cache_dir, keep=_MAX_CACHED): clips = sorted(cache_dir.glob("*.mp4"), key=lambda p: p.stat().st_mtime, reverse=True); for stale in clips[keep:]: stale.unlink(missing_ok=True)`. starlette/responses.py:347-352 — `if self.stat_result is None: try: stat_result = await anyio.to_thread.run_sync(os.stat, self.path) ... except FileNotFoundError: raise RuntimeError(f"File at path {self.path} does not exist.")`.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Reproduced at HEAD. media.py:302-303 `path = build_clip(source, f"{handle.id}--{doc_id}", lo, hi)` / `return FileResponse(path, media_type=mime, headers={"Cache-Control": "no-store"})` (cited as 301-302 — one line off, same site). clips.py:59-61 `out = clip_cache_path(cache_key, lo, hi)` / `if out.exists():` / `return out` — the hit path never opens or stats the file. clips.py:45-49 `def evict_old_clips(cache_dir, keep=_MAX_CACHED) -&gt; None:` / `clips = sorted(cache_dir.glob("*.mp4"), key=lambda p: p.stat().st_mtime, reverse=True)` / `for stale in clips[keep:]: stale.unlink(missing_ok=True)`, called at clips.py:103 inside `with _build_lock:` on another request's build — reverse=True means clips[keep:] is the oldest-by-mtime tail, i.e. FIFO, not LRU and not 'hottest'. starlette/responses.py:351-355 confirms the 500: `if self.stat_result is None: try: stat_result = await anyio.to_thread.run_sync(os.stat, self.path) ... except FileNotFoundError: raise RuntimeError(f"File at path {self.path} does not exist.")`. The atlas.py side-claim also checks out — atlas.py:125-126 `while len(cache) &gt;= _POINTS_CACHE_MAX: cache.pop(next(iter(cache)))` with the comment "Evict oldest-first", i.e. insertion-order FIFO, deliberate and documented, so that one is a recorded decision and not a defect. Not filed in open_python-audit.md (media.py:276/279 = VS-09 is the clip SSRF, a different mechanism).

</details>

<details><summary><b>`/api/thumbnail` and `/api/chunk-frame` read a descriptor-declared blob whole into memory with no ceiling and no `Accept-Ranges`, while their sibling on the same table streams</b> <i>(file-handling.md + streaming.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

**Rule.** file-handling.md § Pick the right response type (`Response(content=bytes)` is for "Tiny file (&lt; 1 MB), already in memory"; anything larger → `StreamingResponse`)

**Sites:** `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/media.py:196`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/media.py:257`

**Why it matters.** The code shape is exactly as described, but there is no concrete failure at HEAD — the ceiling being 'a descriptor's promise' is the finding's own admission, and no descriptor in the repo binds thumbnail or the frames blob to a full-resolution derivative. Record it as a hardening note (cap the read, or stream past a threshold), not a defect. Drop the Accept-Ranges half entirely: these are small cached derivatives served `public, max-age=86400`, and no client range-requests a thumbnail; the /api/media contrast is not apples-to-apples.

**Fix.** Reuse `stream_blob_range` (already in this module) with a `blob_size` probe, so both routes get the same bounded-window streaming, `Content-Length` and `Accept-Ranges: bytes` the media route has, with no new machinery. Alternatively keep the buffered path but assert a ceiling from `blob_size` and 413 above it, so the descriptor cannot silently promise something the process cannot hold.

**Evidence.**

```
media.py:196-200 — `with blob as f:` / `data = f.read()` / ... / `return Response(content=data, media_type=mime, headers={"Cache-Control": "public, max-age=86400"})`; identical shape at media.py:257-261. Contrast media.py:40 `_STREAM_CHUNK = 1 << 20  # 1 MiB: amortizes seek cost, bounds per-stream memory` and media.py:348-355.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Reproduced at HEAD. media.py:193-200 (thumbnail): `blob = ds.take_blobs(binding.thumbnail, ids=[rowid])[0]` / `if blob is None: raise NotFoundError(...)` / `with blob as f:` / `data = f.read()` / … / `return Response(content=data, media_type=mime, headers={"Cache-Control": "public, max-age=86400"})`. Identical shape at media.py:255-261 for chunk_frame. Both are sync `def` (media.py:174, media.py:204) — correctly threadpooled, no async defect. `_frames_binding` is descriptor-resolved as claimed. media.py:40 `_STREAM_CHUNK = 1 &lt;&lt; 20  # 1 MiB: amortizes seek cost, bounds per-stream memory` is the contrasting sibling. No trigger exists in-repo, so this does not meet the 'concrete failure' bar; it is a real but unexercised shape.

</details>

<details><summary><b>notifications is the one fleet service that serves the standard `/livez` + `/readyz` pair, and the chart probes neither — both probes point at its liveness badge</b> <i>(health-checks.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

**Rule.** health-checks.md: "Two endpoints, two purposes. Don't conflate them" — /readyz is the load-balancer/k8s signal; /livez is the supervisor signal

**Sites:** `chart/templates/fleet.yaml:222-235`, `chart/values.yaml:266-270`, `services/notifications/src/notifications/__init__.py:30-34`, `services/notifications/src/notifications/lifespan.py:161-166`, `services/notifications/src/notifications/health.py:20`

**Why it matters.** The wiring is exactly as described and notifications really is absent from the audit — but the two halves have very different standing. The LIVENESS half is a decision recorded at the site, in the same file the finding cites as the victim: notifications/health.py:1-7 says "this is the path the chart's default `healthPath` (`/api/health`) points at, and a probe that fails when a DEPENDENCY is briefly unreachable turns a blip into a restart loop" — pointing liveness at a dependency-free badge is the intent, not an accident, and test_probe_wiring.py:10-12 records the kubelet's side of it too ("the kubelet knows a port and a literal path … and nothing about `RASK_API_PREFIX`"). The READINESS half is a real gap, but the stated failure does not hold: a pod being rolled is removed from the EndpointSlice when it is marked Terminating, not by its readiness probe, and the fleet containers carry `lance.preStop` / a `preStopSeconds` sleep for that window — so readiness pointed at `/readyz` would not change the drain outcome. The estate's actual admission gate for sidecar-delivered traffic is `service_kit.draining`, which draining.py:4-7 says exists precisely because "Dapr's pub/sub delivery does not consult a readiness probe" — i.e. `/readyz` was never the drain lever here. The `starting` gate is unobservable too: uvicorn runs the lifespan before it binds, so `/api/health` cannot answer 200 before `startup_complete` is set. What is genuinely lost is only the `components` report (`actors: unregistered`) never reaching an operator through the probe path — a diagnosability gap, not a routing one.

**Fix.** Add `healthPath` is the wrong lever here (one key, two probes). Split fleet.yaml's probe block the way `lance.appProbes` already does: `readinessProbe → $svc.readyPath | default $svc.healthPath | default "/api/health"`, `livenessProbe → $svc.livePath | default $svc.healthPath`, and set `readyPath: /readyz` + `livePath: /livez` on `services.notifications`. Then generalise tests/unit/test_invariants.py:2901 (which already proves the notifications probe path is served) to assert readiness and liveness resolve to DIFFERENT paths for any service that mounts the probes router.

**Evidence.**

```
chart/templates/fleet.yaml:223 and :232 are the same expression — `httpGet: {path: {{ $svc.healthPath | default "/api/health" }}, port: http}`; rendered: `rask-notifications  notifications  start=NONE | liven=/api/health | readi=/api/health`. services/notifications/src/notifications/__init__.py:33-34 `_root = APIRouter()` / `_root.include_router(make_probes_router(actor_plane_ready))`.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** chart/templates/fleet.yaml:223 and :232 are both `httpGet: {path: {{ $svc.healthPath | default "/api/health" }}, port: http}`; `grep -rn healthPath chart/` returns exactly one override in values.yaml (`healthPath: /healthz`, the gateway, with its own comment), so notifications takes the default. notifications/__init__.py:30-34 confirms the root mount and its stated reason ("a kubelet does not know this service's api prefix"). notifications/health.py:1-11 is the countering record; lifespan.py:161-163 sets `app.state.startup_complete = True` / `shutting_down = False` and :165-166 flips `shutting_down = True` in the `finally`. Dedup check: `grep -n 'services/notifications' open_python-audit.md` returns only Makefile rig rows — X8's site list is compute/controlplane (+6 more) and does not name this service, so the finding is not a duplicate; the MECHANISM ("the chart points BOTH k8s probes at" the liveness badge) is however X8's verbatim mechanism.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The six fleet pods have no startupProbe, and controlplane also missed the measured probe-timeout fix — the lakehouse helper carries both and the fleet templates carry neither</b> <i>(health-checks.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

**Rule.** health-checks.md: "Slow startup → startup probe. If your lifespan takes &gt;15s … add a startupProbe so liveness doesn't kill the pod during boot"

**Sites:** `chart/templates/fleet.yaml:222-235`, `chart/templates/controlplane.yaml:52-59`, `chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:914-933`

**Why it matters.** `lance.appProbes` renders a startupProbe (`/livez`, 30×10s = 300s boot budget) and its comment states the exact hazard: "the FastAPI lifespan … runs BEFORE uvicorn accepts connections, so nothing answers /livez during boot. Without this, liveness … SIGKILLs a still-initializing pod into CrashLoopBackOff exactly when a dependency is already slow. (prod-readiness P1)". Ten containers get it. The six fleet containers get `start=NONE` (confirmed in the render) with `livenessProbe: initialDelaySeconds 15, periodSeconds 20, failureThreshold 3 (default)` — a hard ~55s boot ceiling, after which the kubelet SIGKILLs. Uvicorn runs the lifespan before binding the socket, so every pre-bind second is a probe failure (connection refused), not a slow 200. That ceiling is not comfortable for notifications, whose lifespan does an OIDC discovery/JWKS fetch, then `await fga.provision(...)` (two OpenFGA round-trips with the SDK's own timeouts, at first-install time when OpenFGA is itself starting), then actor registration, then a 10s `warm_actor_proxy_factory`; or for ingest, which does `fga.resolve` plus a Dapr WorkflowRuntime start. I am NOT claiming the chart's "~80s Dapr secret fetch" applies here — `apply_dapr_secrets` is called only by catalog/lineage/medallion/maintenance, which all have the startupProbe. Separately, controlplane.yaml:52-59 is hand-written rather than going through fleet.yaml, so it also missed the `timeoutSeconds: 5` fix fleet.yaml:226-230 records as measured against the OOM killer (#136) and keeps k8s's 1s default, and it is invisible to any `healthPath`-based gate.

**Fix.** Move the fleet probe block into a shared helper (`rask.fleetProbes`) that emits a startupProbe on the same path with `periodSeconds: 10, failureThreshold: 30`, and have controlplane.yaml include it instead of its own copy — that fixes the missing startupProbe and the missing timeout in one edit, and removes the second hand-written probe block that drifts.

**Evidence.**

```
chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:915-919 — "startupProbe gates liveness+readiness until boot completes … Without this, liveness (armed ~70s in) SIGKILLs a still-initializing pod into CrashLoopBackOff exactly when a dependency is already slow." Rendered: `rask-notifications … start=NONE | liven=/api/health | readi=/api/health` vs `rask-catalog … start=/livez | liven=/livez | readi=/readyz`.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** _helpers.tpl:914-933 renders `startupProbe: httpGet: { path: /livez, port: http } / periodSeconds: 10 / failureThreshold: 30` under the comment quoted in the finding, plus `readinessProbe /readyz` and `livenessProbe /livez` with `timeoutSeconds: 3`. fleet.yaml:222-235 renders only readiness+liveness — readiness `initialDelaySeconds: 5, periodSeconds: 10, timeoutSeconds: 5` and liveness `initialDelaySeconds: 15, periodSeconds: 20, timeoutSeconds: 5`, no startupProbe and no `failureThreshold`, so the default 3 gives the ~55 s ceiling the finding computes (uvicorn runs the lifespan before binding, so every pre-bind probe is a connection refusal, not a slow 200). controlplane.yaml:52-59 is verbatim `readinessProbe: httpGet: {path: /api/health, port: http} … livenessProbe: httpGet: {path: /api/health, port: http} / initialDelaySeconds: 15 / periodSeconds: 20` — no `timeoutSeconds`, so it keeps k8s's 1 s default and misses the fix fleet.yaml:226-230 records as measured against the OOM killer (#136), and it uses a literal rather than `$svc.healthPath`. The self-imposed scope limit is honest and correct: `apply_dapr_secrets` (the ~80 s worst case in the helper's comment) is not called by any fleet.yaml service. Not filed: `grep -in 'startupProbe|startup probe' open_python-audit.md` returns nothing; X8 covers the probe PATHS on these services, not the missing startup gate. low is the right grade — the boot budget is a plausible-but-unmeasured ceiling here, not an observed CrashLoopBackOff.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The assist runner's `/livez` is a sync `def` on the blocking threadpool — the exact shape service_kit.probes was created to delete — and its `/readyz` never reports draining</b> <i>(health-checks.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

**Rule.** health-checks.md: "/livez … Cheap — return 200 if the event loop is responsive"; "(shutting down) 503 — Lifespan post-yield started"

**Sites:** `runners/assist/server.py:246-257`, `runners/insid3/server.py:114-122`, `chart/templates/runners.yaml:46`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/probes.py:9-13`

**Why it matters.** Half of this is a scope error and half is a smaller, real defect. runners/assist is a SEALED runner — not a workspace member, deliberately outside the platform's conventions (CLAUDE.md: "runners/ … Matched by no glob, which is the point"), and probes.py's sync-def complaint is aimed at `service_kit.media.probes`, a service-plane copy, not at runner code. The estate's standing rule is also that a plain `def` route is correct because FastAPI threadpools it; these two handlers do no I/O at all and the saturation path needs `def assist()` to hold all 40 anyio tokens, which one annotator on a single replica will not do — the finding concedes this. The drain half is wrong for the same reason as finding 2: a terminating pod leaves the EndpointSlice on deletion, not via readiness, and the assist container carries `lance.preStop` (a `sleep {{ .Values.lifecycle.preStopSeconds }}`) for that window — so `readyz` reporting `shutting_down` would change nothing. What survives is narrow and real: `readyz` tests `app.state.models is None`, and `models` is assigned before the lifespan yields, so after boot the branch is unreachable and the route is a constant 200 — a readiness check that can never fail. Low, and confined to a sealed runner.

**Fix.** Make both handlers `async def` (neither does I/O), and gate `readyz` on lifespan flags rather than on `models is None`: set `app.state.startup_complete`/`shutting_down` around the `yield` at server.py:237-240 and 503 on either, matching `service_kit.probes`. The runner stays sealed — this is its own file, and the platform learns nothing about the workload.

**Evidence.**

```
runners/assist/server.py:246-249 — `@app.get("/livez")` / `def livez() -> dict[str, bool]:` / `"""Process-up (the k8s liveness/startup signal — never touches the models)."""` / `return {"ok": True}`; server.py:252-257 `def readyz()` → `if getattr(app.state, "models", None) is None: raise HTTPException(status_code=503, …)`.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** runners/assist/server.py:246-257 at HEAD: `@app.get("/livez")` / `def livez() -&gt; dict[str, bool]:` / `return {"ok": True}`; `@app.get("/readyz")` / `def readyz() -&gt; dict[str, bool]:` / `    if getattr(app.state, "models", None) is None: raise HTTPException(status_code=503, detail="models not loaded")`. server.py:236 sets `app.state.models = Models(settings)` inside the lifespan before `yield`, which is what makes the guard dead. chart/templates/runners.yaml:46 does include `lance.appProbes` (so /livez is the startup+liveness path and /readyz the readiness path), and :39 includes `lance.preStop` — _helpers.tpl:877-882 `command: ["sh", "-c", "sleep {{ .Values.lifecycle.preStopSeconds }}"]`. insid3/server.py:114-122 has the identical pair and is, as the finding says, not deployed by this chart.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The probe-path-is-actually-served gate covers exactly one of the fifteen apps, and controlplane bypasses the mechanism the gate reads</b> <i>(health-checks.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

**Rule.** health-checks.md: probe wiring must be verified against what the app mounts (the reference's mount rule — probes root-mounted, chart pointing at them)

**Sites:** `tests/unit/test_invariants.py:2901-2946`, `chart/templates/controlplane.yaml:52-59`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/health.py:3-12`

**Why it matters.** The gate really is one-service-scoped and controlplane really does bypass `$svc.healthPath` — but "one of fifteen" overstates the exposure by a factor of three. The drift class needs a probe path DERIVED from `RASK_API_PREFIX` at runtime while the chart writes a literal, and that is only the `make_service_app` fleet layout: compute, controlplane, flows, ingest, notifications. The eight lance apps (catalog, lineage, producer, the movers, maintenance, viewer, search, annotator) root-mount `/livez` and `/readyz` as literals via `service_kit.probes`, and `lance.appProbes` writes those same literals — no prefix is involved on either side, so no mismatch is possible; the gateway is the fifth exception, probing its own literal `/healthz` (values.yaml:334, with its reason recorded). So the honest claim is "the gate covers 1 of the 5 apps that can drift, and controlplane — one of the other 4 — is additionally invisible to any `healthPath`-keyed test". The finding is otherwise clean: it verified there is no live mismatch, so this is missing coverage, which is a low.

**Fix.** Generalise the existing gate: iterate every first-party Deployment in the rendered docs, import each app under the chart's own config env, and assert every probe path is in `app.openapi()["paths"]`. That is the same test body already written, with the `_notifications_container` lookup replaced by a loop — and it would then also catch a future `healthPath` edit or a prefix change on the other five fleet services.

**Evidence.**

```
tests/unit/test_invariants.py:2919-2921 — `docs = _rendered_docs()` / `config = _fleet_config(docs)` / `container = _notifications_container(docs)`; chart/templates/controlplane.yaml:53 `            httpGet: {path: /api/health, port: http}` (a literal, not `$svc.healthPath`).
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** tests/unit/test_invariants.py:2901-2937 is scoped by name — `container = _notifications_container(docs)` at :2921, `probed = {container[probe]["httpGet"]["path"] for probe in ("livenessProbe", "readinessProbe")}`, `import notifications`, `assert probed &lt;= served`; its docstring says as much (":2905-2907 `services.notifications` sets no `healthPath` — so the default is load-bearing here and is correct only while the prefix is `/api`"). chart/templates/controlplane.yaml:53 and :57 are both the literal `httpGet: {path: /api/health, port: http}`, not `$svc.healthPath`, confirming the bypass. ingest/health.py:3-12 confirms the class the gate exists for is real and was paid for once ("the pod stuck at 1/2 forever … the probe 404'd, readiness never went true, and the Service had no endpoints"). Not in the audit: no row cites tests/unit/test_invariants.py for probe wiring.

</details>

<details><summary><b>No PodDisruptionBudget for any front-door fleet service except the gateway, and values-prod leaves all of them single-replica</b> <i>(kubernetes.md + microservices.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

**Rule.** kubernetes.md: § PodDisruptionBudget — "almost free, often skipped ... protects against losing all replicas during a node drain or cluster upgrade"

**Sites:** `chart/templates/ha.yaml:1-11`, `chart/values-prod.yaml:34-50`, `chart/values.yaml:1431-1435`, `chart/values.yaml:266-269`

**Why it matters.** Half the recommendation contradicts a decision the template records in its own header, and the finding never engages with it. chart/templates/ha.yaml:5-8 states that a 1-replica minAvailable=1 PDB 'would block every voluntary drain', which is precisely why the zones get no PDB below replicas &gt; 1 — so adding PDBs for the five single-replica fleet services would be the wrong fix, not the missing one. What actually survives is only the replica half: chart/values-prod.yaml bumps catalog, lineage, gateway and frontend and leaves notifications, ingest, flows, compute and controlplane at 1, so a node drain gives each a full-outage reschedule window. Even that list needs trimming: flows CANNOT be scaled — .docker/flows.dockerfile:81-82 records 'the run store is a process-local dict (v0), so a second worker would serve GET /flows/runs/{id} from a process that never saw the run' — so its single-replica posture is a documented v0 constraint, and the finding's 'an eviction loses in-flight run state outright' describes the known design, not a chart defect. The genuine residue is narrow: values.yaml:263-265 argues notifications scales safely ('Dapr placement distributes actor ids across hosts, and the bus subscription's queueGroupName makes replicas a competing-consumer group') and values-prod then does not scale it, so the bell every zone polls is a single point of failure on a cluster upgrade. That is an availability-posture gap in an opt-in overlay, not an outage today — low.

**Fix.** Two coordinated steps, in this order — a `minAvailable: 1` PDB over a single replica BLOCKS every voluntary drain, which ha.yaml's header already warns about for the zones. (1) In values-prod.yaml raise `services.notifications.replicas`, `services.ingest.replicas` and `services.controlplane`/`compute` to 2 (notifications' own comment says its actor and bus planes tolerate it; leave `flows` at 1 until its run store is durable and say so). (2) Extend ha.yaml's range list to those services, gated on `replicas &gt; 1` the same way the zone block is. Add `lance.spreadConstraints` to fleet.yaml while you are there — the helper exists (_helpers.tpl) and fleet.yaml is the one template that never calls it, so the prod `replicas: 2` on the gateway can co-locate and its PDB buys nothing, which is the exact failure that helper's docstring records.

**Evidence.**

```
chart/templates/ha.yaml:11 — `{{- range $c := list "catalog" "lineage" "gateway" }}`; the header at :1-8 names the stateful stores and the movers as the deliberate exclusions and does not mention the fleet. chart/values-prod.yaml:34-50 sets replicas only for `catalog`, `lineage` and the gateway row; `grep -n 'notifications\|ingest\|flows\|compute\|controlplane' chart/values-prod.yaml` returns no replica bump for any of them. chart/values.yaml:1432-1434 — `podDisruptionBudget:\n  enabled: false\n  minAvailable: 1` (flipped true by values-prod.yaml:62-64).
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** chart/templates/ha.yaml:11 verbatim `{{- range $c := list "catalog" "lineage" "gateway" }}`, and I read the :1-8 header in full, which names the stateful stores and the movers as deliberate exclusions AND states the 1-replica-PDB rule quoted above. chart/values-prod.yaml:34-50 read in full: replicas set only for catalog (2), lineage (2), gateway (2) and frontend (2); no bump for notifications/ingest/flows/compute/controlplane. chart/values.yaml:1432-1434 `podDisruptionBudget: enabled: false / minAvailable: 1`, flipped true by values-prod.yaml:61-63. Counter-evidence the finding omits: ha.yaml:5-8 and .docker/flows.dockerfile:81-82.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The gateway's `--forwarded-allow-ips=127.0.0.1` can never match the Ingress controller that calls it, and the client-controlled X-Forwarded-For is forwarded to every backend untouched</b> <i>(kubernetes.md + microservices.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

**Rule.** microservices.md: § Service-to-service authn — the edge is the only place a client's identity assertion can be refused; kubernetes.md: § Full Deployment YAML — env/args must reflect the deploy topology

**Sites:** `chart/values.yaml:330`, `chart/templates/ingress.yaml:66-72`, `.docker/gateway.dockerfile:86-93`, `services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py:39-41`, `services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py:66-77`

**Why it matters.** The misconfiguration is real; the consequence is not. I grepped the entire Python estate for consumers — `grep -rn 'request.client\|url.scheme\|X-Forwarded\|x-forwarded' services/ packages/ --include=*.py | grep -v /tests/` returns ZERO lines. Nothing reads `request.client.host`, nothing reads `request.url.scheme`, and nothing reads any `x-forwarded-*` header anywhere in the fleet. So there is no audit line, no log field and no rate limit to collapse, and the gateway's `_rewrite_location` (services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py:80-85) already strips absolute upstream URLs to paths, so the `scheme` stays http harmlessly. The second claim, that x-forwarded-for reaching backends verbatim is exploitable, is latent by the finding's own admission and stays latent because no backend parses it. One factual correction: 'there is no override path' is wrong — `extraArgs` is itself a values key under `services.gateway`, so `--set-json services.gateway.extraArgs=[...]` replaces it; what is missing is a dedicated knob, not any knob. Correct framing: a deploy-time obligation the image states explicitly (.docker/gateway.dockerfile:86-88) is unmet by the chart, and it is a trap that arms the moment anything starts reading the client IP or the scheme — worth fixing, but low today.

**Fix.** Make the trusted-proxy CIDR a values key (`services.gateway.forwardedAllowIps`, defaulting to the pod CIDR or the ingress controller's namespace CIDR) rendered into extraArgs, so a deployment can actually satisfy the dockerfile's stated requirement — and note in the comment that `*` is refused. Separately, have the gateway OWN the forwarded chain rather than pass it through: strip inbound `x-forwarded-for`/`x-forwarded-host`/`x-forwarded-proto` (add them to `_CLIENT_SPOOFABLE`, which is where the reasoning for this already lives) and set them from `request.client` + the resolved scheme before forwarding. Finally, decide the CMD-vs-command question deliberately: either move `--proxy-headers --forwarded-allow-ips` into per-service `extraArgs` so the chart carries the posture the images document, or delete those flags from the five dockerfiles so nobody reads a CMD that production never runs.

**Evidence.**

```
chart/values.yaml:330 — `extraArgs: ["--proxy-headers", "--forwarded-allow-ips=127.0.0.1"]`. chart/templates/ingress.yaml:66-72 — `- path: /api` → `backend:\n  service:\n    name: {{ include "rask.fullname" $ }}-gateway\n    port:\n      number: {{ .Values.services.gateway.port }}`. .docker/gateway.dockerfile:86-93 carries the comment quoted above and the CMD the chart discards. services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py:39-41 (`_HOP_BY_HOP`) and :66-77 (`_CLIENT_SPOOFABLE`) list every header the proxy touches; neither contains any `x-forwarded-*` entry. `grep -rn 'forwarded-allow-ips' chart/` returns exactly one line — values.yaml:330 — so there is no override path.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** chart/values.yaml:330 verbatim `extraArgs: ["--proxy-headers", "--forwarded-allow-ips=127.0.0.1"]`, and `grep -rn 'forwarded-allow-ips' chart/` returns exactly that one line. chart/templates/ingress.yaml routes `- path: /api` to `{{ include "rask.fullname" $ }}-gateway`, so the immediate TCP peer is the ingress-nginx pod IP and uvicorn's ProxyHeadersMiddleware discards the headers — the mechanism is correct. .docker/gateway.dockerfile:86-88 verbatim: '--forwarded-allow-ips MUST be the cluster edge's CIDR at deploy time (the Ingress controller today; kgateway is the intended future edge), never \'*\' (header-spoofing risk).' services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py:39-41 (`_HOP_BY_HOP`) and :66-77 (`_CLIENT_SPOOFABLE`, holding dapr-caller-app-id, dapr-api-token, dapr-app-id, x-lance-service-identity) — neither contains an x-forwarded entry, as claimed. The downgrade rests on the zero-consumer grep, which the finding did not run.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The HPA scales the two most I/O-bound services on CPU utilization against a 50m request with a 1000m limit, and has no scale-down stabilization window</b> <i>(kubernetes.md + microservices.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

**Rule.** kubernetes.md: § HorizontalPodAutoscaler — "CPU is a lagging signal ... If you have RPS or p95 latency exported via OTel, autoscale on that"; § What NOT to do — "HPA on CPU for an I/O-bound API"

**Sites:** `chart/templates/ha.yaml:69-92`, `chart/values.yaml:1436-1440`, `chart/values.yaml:456-459`

**Why it matters.** Three problems in one object, all opt-in (`autoscaling.enabled: false`, and values-prod does not turn it on) which is why this is low rather than medium — but the shape is wrong if anyone ever does. (1) The targets are catalog and lineage: the catalog buffers Arrow-IPC bodies up to 256 MiB and runs 163 `run_in_threadpool` blocking S3/Lance calls, lineage waits on an AGE psycopg pool — both spend their time blocked, not computing, which is precisely the case the reference says CPU cannot see. (2) `averageUtilization: 70` is measured against the pod's CPU REQUEST, and `resources.default` requests 50m against a 1000m limit — a 20:1 burst ratio, so 70% of request is 35 millicores. Any real request load blows past that instantly and the HPA slams from `minReplicas: 2` to `maxReplicas: 5` on the first burst, then oscillates as the burst passes. (3) There is no `behavior.scaleDown.stabilizationWindowSeconds`, which the reference's own worked YAML includes with a 5-minute cooldown specifically to stop that churn. The precondition for doing better is already met: the estate exports `http.server.*` RED metrics to GreptimeDB via `service_kit.setup_otel` and ships a Perses 'Fleet — RED' dashboard, so p95 latency and RPS are available signals, not aspirational ones.

**Fix.** Minimum: add `behavior: {scaleDown: {stabilizationWindowSeconds: 300}}` and raise `resources.catalog`/`resources.lineage` requests so utilization-of-request is a meaningful ratio (requests within ~2-4x of limits, not 20x). Better: since the RED metrics already land in GreptimeDB, switch the metric to a Pods-type `http_server_request_duration_seconds` p95 target through a Prometheus adapter or KEDA, and keep CPU only as the documented fallback — the reference gives the exact shape. Whichever is chosen, say in the values comment which signal is authoritative, because right now the comment ('opt-in; needs metrics-server. Targets catalog + lineage.') describes the wiring and not the decision.

**Evidence.**

```
chart/templates/ha.yaml:82-92 — `metrics:\n  - type: Resource\n    resource:\n      name: cpu\n      target:\n        type: Utilization\n        averageUtilization: {{ $.Values.autoscaling.targetCPUUtilizationPercentage }}` with no `behavior:` key anywhere in the file. chart/values.yaml:1436-1440 — `autoscaling:\n  enabled: false\n  minReplicas: 2\n  maxReplicas: 5\n  targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70`. chart/values.yaml:457-459 — `default:\n  requests: { cpu: 50m, memory: 128Mi }\n  limits: { cpu: "1", memory: 512Mi }`, which is the tier both HPA targets resolve to (neither `resources.catalog` nor `resources.lineage` is defined).
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** All three sub-claims verified, and the low grade is correctly self-assessed. chart/templates/ha.yaml:63-64 `{{- if .Values.autoscaling.enabled }}` / `{{- range $c := list "catalog" "lineage" }}` — the two targets are exactly the ones named. ha.yaml:81-92 is the whole `metrics:` block (`- type: Resource` / `resource:` / `name: cpu` / `target:` / `type: Utilization` / `averageUtilization: {{ $.Values.autoscaling.targetCPUUtilizationPercentage }}`) and there is no `behavior:` key anywhere in the file — I read it end to end. chart/values.yaml:1436-1440 `autoscaling: enabled: false / minReplicas: 2 / maxReplicas: 5 / targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70`, and values-prod.yaml does not enable it. The request-ratio arithmetic checks out: services.yaml:201 and :448 resolve catalog and lineage through `lance.resources`, and `chart/values.yaml` defines resource tiers for fleet, compute, default, otel-collector, rustfs, assist and viewer — no `catalog` and no `lineage` key — so both land on `default: requests: { cpu: 50m, memory: 128Mi }, limits: { cpu: "1", memory: 512Mi }` (values.yaml:457-459), making the 70% target 35 millicores against a 20:1 burst ceiling. Opt-in and off in every shipped profile, so low is right.

</details>

<details><summary><b>`setup_otel` passes `logger_provider=` to `LoggingInstrumentor`, which the installed 0.65b0 ignores — and `set_logging_format=True` is a no-op, so fleet stdout carries no trace id</b> <i>(observability.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

**Rule.** observability.md: § log correlation ("Log the exception inside the active span context — trace_id/span_id ride along") — the estate's stdout tier is the copy an operator reads first, and it carries neither

**Sites:** `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/otel.py:91-108`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/__init__.py:63-66`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/__init__.py:119`, `chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:416-421`

**Why it matters.** Three legs were filed; only the middle one is a defect, and it is smaller than stated. (1) The `logger_provider=` kwarg IS dropped by the installed library — verified — but this is a FALSE COMMENT, not a behavioural bug: `set_logger_provider(logger_provider)` runs at otel.py:104, two lines above the instrument call, so `get_logger_provider()` returns exactly the provider that was passed. The comment at otel.py:94-97 claims a race protection that does not exist; the correct fix is to delete the sentence, not to change the wiring. (2) The real defect: `set_logging_format=True` reaches `logging.basicConfig(...)`, which no-ops because `setup_logging()` already installed the named `rask-stdout` handler on the root logger — so `otelTraceID` is attached to every record by the factory and never printed on the stdout tier. (3) The `OTEL_PYTHON_LOGGING_AUTO_INSTRUMENTATION_ENABLED` leg is speculative and contradicted by a recorded decision: chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:414-419 names that exact variable and states it is deliberately not copied — "The vars that genuinely ARE launcher-only — OTEL_{TRACES,METRICS,LOGS}_EXPORTER and OTEL_PYTHON_LOGGING_AUTO_INSTRUMENTATION_ENABLED — are deliberately NOT copied". Blast radius of (2) is also narrower than claimed: the OTLP copy of every log DOES carry trace/span context, and chart/templates/fleet.yaml:48's own comment says the file-tailed line is a deliberate dup — so only the raw `kubectl logs` tier lacks the id. Real, worth a one-line `logging_format=` or a formatter change, but low.

**Fix.** Drop the `logger_provider=` kwarg (it is inert) and instead assert the binding: after `set_logger_provider(...)`, check `get_logger_provider() is logger_provider` and log loudly if not. Replace `set_logging_format=True` with `inject_trace_context=True` and put `%(otelTraceID)s %(otelSpanID)s` into `setup_logging`'s own formatter, which is the handler that actually exists. Add an invariant asserting `OTEL_PYTHON_LOGGING_AUTO_INSTRUMENTATION_ENABLED` is never rendered by `rask.otelEnv`, with the reason inline, so the skip branch cannot be reached by a copy from the sibling helper.

**Evidence.**

```
.venv/lib/python3.13/site-packages/opentelemetry/instrumentation/logging/__init__.py:259 `logger_provider = get_logger_provider()` (the kwarg at otel.py:108 is dropped) and :170 `logging.basicConfig(format=log_format, level=log_level)`. Concrete failure today: `kubectl logs deploy/rask-gateway | grep ERROR` gives `2026-08-24 … ERROR gateway — upstream unreachable` with no trace id on the line, so there is no path from a log an operator is staring at to the trace that explains it — the reverse direction (trace → OTLP log) works, the direction people actually use does not.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Installed library, .venv/lib/python3.13/site-packages/opentelemetry/instrumentation/logging/__init__.py:259 `logger_provider = get_logger_provider()` — the kwarg passed at otel.py:108 `LoggingInstrumentor().instrument(set_logging_format=True, logger_provider=logger_provider)` is never read. Same file :169 `logging.basicConfig(format=log_format, level=log_level)` under `if set_logging_format:`. Ordering confirmed: packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/__init__.py:119 `setup_logging()` is the first statement of `make_service_app`, and :157 `setup_otel(app, service_name=title, settings=settings)` is the last — and setup_logging at :61-66 does `handler.setFormatter(logging.Formatter("%(asctime)s %(levelname)-7s %(name)s — %(message)s"))` on a root handler named `rask-stdout`, so basicConfig returns immediately and the format never gains `%(otelTraceID)s`. Note the audit's X5 ("`setup_otel` exports no logs at all despite claiming to", open_python-audit.md:~975) is the ADJACENT, now-fixed defect — the LoggerProvider exists at otel.py:98-104 today — so this is not a duplicate of X5, but it lives in the same E10 epic.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The cascade head's span closes before the half that fails, and never sets ERROR status — unlike every other manual span in the estate</b> <i>(observability.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

**Rule.** observability.md: § Manual spans inside routes — only for business operations (the span must cover the operation, not a prefix of it)

**Sites:** `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/produce.py:91-105`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/produce.py:139-152`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/media_produce.py:110-113`

**Why it matters.** The mechanism reproduces exactly, but the concrete failure overstates the blindness: the request is NOT green end-to-end. services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce.py:97-101 turns `publish_failed` into `return JSONResponse(status_code=503, media_type="application/problem+json", headers={"Retry-After": "5"}, ...)`, and the producer runs under `opentelemetry-instrument` (chart/templates/services.yaml:46 `command: ["opentelemetry-instrument", "uvicorn"]`), so the FastAPI server span for `POST /produce` records the 5xx and goes ERROR. What is true and worth fixing: the span that NAMES the operation covers only the Lance seed and reports success for a run whose cascade never fires, and it disappears entirely when `compute_enabled` is false — so a trace filtered to `medallion.produce` misattributes the failure, and the estate's own convention (ingest/workflow.py:1143, medallion/workflow.py:440, flows/activities.py:71, maintenance/sweep.py:382 all call `set_status`) is broken on the two spans that matter most. Low is the right grade and the finding already had it.
