**Fix.** Move the `with tracer.start_as_current_span("medallion.produce")` to wrap the whole function body (seed, build, publish), keeping the seed-only attributes where they are, and add `span.set_status(Status(StatusCode.ERROR, ...))` in the `except` at :150 next to the existing `log.warning("medallion_produce_failed", ...)` — matching the pattern flows/activities.py:69-71 documents. Same for `medallion.ingest_media`.

**Evidence.**

```
produce.py:91 `with tracer.start_as_current_span("medallion.produce") as span:` … :105 `span.set_attribute("lance.size_bytes", result.size_bytes)`; :106 `bronze_event = build_run_event(` is dedented out of the span; :150-152 `except Exception as exc: log.warning("medallion_produce_failed", ...); return {"status": "publish_failed", "token": token}`. Concrete failure: NATS is unreachable, `POST /produce` returns 503, no cascade runs — and the trace shows a green 15 ms `medallion.produce` span with a normal row count, because the span ended nine lines above the failure.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** services/medallion/src/medallion/services/produce.py:88-105 reproduces verbatim: `if settings.compute_enabled and bronze_uri:` / `with tracer.start_as_current_span("medallion.produce") as span:` … ending at :105 `span.set_attribute("lance.size_bytes", result.size_bytes)`. :106 `bronze_event = build_run_event(` is dedented out of the `with`, and the failing leg is at :130-152 — `await outbox.publish_lineage_with_outbox(...)` inside `try:` with `except Exception as exc: log.warning("medallion_produce_failed", ...); return {"status": "publish_failed", "token": token}`. The comment at :125-129 confirms the stake in the estate's own words: "The cascade HEAD is this bronze-write lineage event: medallion-producer's own /bronze-arrival subscription reacts to it and publishes the medallion.bronze trigger". `grep -rn set_status services/medallion/src/medallion/services/produce.py services/medallion/src/medallion/services/media_produce.py` → no hits; media_produce.py:110-113 has the identical shape.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Custom span attributes split one concept across two namespaces: the OpenLineage run id is `lance.ingest.run_id` in one service and `lance.lineage.run_id` in another</b> <i>(observability.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

**Rule.** observability.md: § Manual spans ("See the `otel` skill references/signals.md for the full naming rules") — attribute keys must be stable across the services that emit the same concept

**Sites:** `services/ingest/src/ingest/workflow.py:1137-1138`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/transform.py:520`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/produce.py:103-105`, `services/maintenance/src/maintenance/services/sweep.py:287`

**Why it matters.** The central premise — "the SAME OpenLineage run id ... one identifier, two keys" — is FALSE, and with it the stated failure. `lance.ingest.run_id` carries the ingest harvest's run id (`spec.run_id`); `lance.lineage.run_id` carries a run id MINTED IN THE MOVER: transform.py:506 calls `promotion_lineage(...)`, which at promotion.py:50-60 builds a fresh `build_run_event(...)` and returns `LineageDoc.from_run_event(RunEvent.model_validate(identity_event), ...)` — a new run, not the ingest one. So an operator querying `lance.ingest.run_id = '…'` would not match the transform spans even if both keys were spelled identically; the join the finding wants does not exist at any key name, and renaming would not create it. The residue is real but purely cosmetic: the mover stamps its OWN run under a `lance.lineage.*` prefix while every sibling attribute it sets on the same span uses `lance.medallion.*` (transition, compute, ray_phase, ray_submission_id) — a misleading namespace segment worth one rename, nothing more. The second half ("five attributes sit at the top of the `lance.` namespace ... the producer, the mover and the sweep are indistinguishable by attribute key alone") should be dropped: OTel attributes are scoped by their span, and `medallion.produce`, `medallion.transform` and `compaction.compact` distinguish them by name — that is ordinary practice, not a defect.

**Fix.** Settle on one key for the run id — `lance.lineage.run_id`, since it is the OpenLineage identity, not the emitting service's — and change ingest/workflow.py:1137 to match. Give the five bare `lance.*` attributes their service segment (or, better, promote the three write-result ones to a shared `lance.write.{version,row_count,size_bytes}` set the producer, mover and sweep all use, since they describe the same Lance commit). Pin it with an invariant over `set_attribute("…")` literals, in the shape of the existing alert-name gate.

**Evidence.**

```
services/ingest/src/ingest/workflow.py:1137 `span.set_attribute("lance.ingest.run_id", spec.run_id)` vs services/medallion/src/medallion/services/transform.py:520 `span.set_attribute("lance.lineage.run_id", lineage_doc.run_id)`. Concrete failure: an operator tracing a cascade from an ingest run id queries `lance.ingest.run_id = '…'` and gets the ingest spans only — the medallion transform spans for the same run, which are the ones that explain where it stalled, do not match and read as absent.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Full inventory at HEAD (`grep -rn 'set_attribute("lance\.' services/ packages/ --include=*.py | grep -v tests`) confirms the two keys exist verbatim — services/ingest/src/ingest/workflow.py:1137 `span.set_attribute("lance.ingest.run_id", spec.run_id)` and services/medallion/src/medallion/services/transform.py:520 `span.set_attribute("lance.lineage.run_id", lineage_doc.run_id)` — but services/medallion/src/medallion/services/promotion.py:50 `identity_event = build_run_event(` and :61 `return LineageDoc.from_run_event(RunEvent.model_validate(identity_event), ...)` show `lineage_doc.run_id` is a newly-minted promotion run, not the ingest run. The docstring at promotion.py:48 says so directly: "A consumer holds the dataset ... and joins back to the graph on ``run_id``" — i.e. the join is the LINEAGE GRAPH's job, which is where the ingest→promotion edge actually lives, not a span attribute equality. Immediately adjacent, transform.py:519/538/565/567 all use `lance.medallion.*` on the same span, which is what makes :520's `lance.lineage.*` an inconsistency rather than a scheme.

</details>

<details><summary><b>`GET /v1/model` truncates the listing at `limit` with no cursor, no `total` and no `capped` flag — the 101st model is invisible and unreachable</b> <i>(pagination.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

**Rule.** pagination.md: 'Return `pages`, `has_next`, `has_prev` in the envelope; clients don't compute them' — and the `Page[Item]` envelope section. A slice that reports neither a total nor a continuation is truncation, not pagination.

**Sites:** `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/models.py:86`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/models.py:102`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/schemas.py:609`

**Why it matters.** The shape is real — `ModelsListResponse` carries only `models`, and there is no cursor param — but two load-bearing claims in the finding are wrong. (a) 'unreachable' is false: `limit` accepts up to 1000, so models 101-150 in the finding's own scenario ARE reachable with `?limit=1000`. (b) The site carries a recorded decision the finding treats as the defect: the docstring states '``limit`` bounds the per-request dataset opens (one per listed model); truncation is deterministic (name-sorted first ``limit``)' — deterministic name-sorted truncation is a chosen shape, not an oversight, and the one-dataset-open-per-model cost is the stated reason the ceiling exists. What survives is a genuine but cosmetic envelope gap: no `total`/`capped` to tell the client the answer was cut, where three siblings in the estate do provide it. Low.

**Fix.** Reuse `_paginate` from `tables.py:197`, which already implements exactly the right keyset over a sorted name list and already ships in this service: sort `names`, run the FGA filter, then `names, page_token = _paginate(names, page_token, limit)` and add `page_token: str | None = None` to both the signature and `ModelsListResponse`. Failing that, at minimum add `total: int` and `capped: bool` so the truncation is at least visible, matching lineage's `EstateGraph`.

**Evidence.**

```
services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/models.py:86 — `    limit: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1, le=1000)] = 100,` (no cursor param anywhere in the signature, lines 81-87). models.py:102 — `return ModelsListResponse(models=await run_in_threadpool(_summaries, settings, so, names[:limit]))`. services/catalog/src/catalog/schemas.py:609-610 — `class ModelsListResponse(BaseModel):` / `    models: list[ModelSummary]`. Failure: with 150 registered models, `GET /api/catalog/v1/model` returns 100 `ModelSummary` rows and a 200; the frontend model plane renders 100 and there is no field on the response, and no query parameter on the route, that can reach or even count models 101-150.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Verified at HEAD. services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/models.py:86 `    limit: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1, le=1000)] = 100,` — signature lines 81-87 carry no cursor/offset/page_token. models.py:102 `return ModelsListResponse(models=await run_in_threadpool(_summaries, settings, so, names[:limit]))` (threadpooled — not the async trap), with the FGA filter at models.py:100 applied first, as claimed. services/catalog/src/catalog/schemas.py:609-610 `class ModelsListResponse(BaseModel):` / `    models: list[ModelSummary]` — confirmed, single field. The sibling contrast also holds: lineage discovery.py:126-131 returns `total=total, capped=total &gt; limit`. No audit entry cites catalog endpoints/models.py.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The estate's only offset-paginated route has no MAX_OFFSET guard and runs an unconditional `count_rows()` on a Lance table</b> <i>(pagination.md, ADJUSTED)</i></summary>

**Rule.** pagination.md § 'Approximate counts + MAX_OFFSET guard' — `MAX_OFFSET = 10_000` and `get_pagination_guarded`; plus the anti-patterns 'Offsetting into the millions' and '`SELECT COUNT(*)` on every list call against a 10M-row table'.

**Sites:** `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/system.py:153`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/system.py:171`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/system.py:172`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/system.py:173`

**Why it matters.** Half the finding holds and half is a mis-mapped SQL anti-pattern. Real: `page: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1)] = 1` has no `le`, so the derived offset is unbounded, and `grep -rn 'MAX_OFFSET' services/ packages/` returns zero — nothing in the estate implements the reference's guard. Not established: the `count_rows()` half is NOT the '`SELECT COUNT(*)` on every list call' anti-pattern — an unfiltered Lance `count_rows()` is answered from fragment metadata, not a table scan, so it does not carry the cost the reference is warning about; and the stated failure ('pins a threadpool worker for the duration of a full scan') is asserted, not demonstrated — Lance can resolve a plain limit/offset scan from per-fragment row counts. Downgrade to low: a missing `le` on one page parameter, with an unproven cost model.

**Fix.** Apply the reference's `get_pagination_guarded` shape inline: raise `InvalidInputError` (the Lance-plane taxonomy this service already uses) when `(page - 1) * per_page &gt; 10_000`, with a message pointing at the keyset alternative. For `total`, gate the count — the gallery only needs it on page 1; on subsequent pages the client already has it, so `total = ds.count_rows() if page == 1 else None` removes a full-table count from every scroll tick. Longer term this route wants the keyset the estate uses everywhere else (`after_doc_key`), since a document gallery has a natural total order.

**Evidence.**

```
services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/system.py:151-154 — `def documents(state: StateDep, page: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1)] = 1, per_page: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1, le=100)] = 24, dataset: DatasetParam = None) -> dict[str, Any]:` — `ge=1` and no `le`. :171-174 — `total = ds.count_rows()` / `offset = max(0, (page - 1) * per_page)` / `tbl = ds.to_table(columns=columns, limit=per_page, offset=offset)` / `return {"total": total, "page": page, "docs": tbl.to_pylist()}`. Failure: `GET /api/explorer/documents?page=100000000&per_page=24` is accepted (no 422) and asks Lance to skip 2.4e9 rows; the request pins a threadpool worker for the duration of a full scan and returns `{"total": N, "page": 100000000, "docs": []}`. Unauthenticated-adjacent: this route takes no `CheckerDep` (it is one of the 25 ungated viewer routes counted under VS-03), so any caller reaching the gateway can issue it.
```

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Verified at HEAD. services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/system.py:150-154 — `@router.get("/documents")` / `def documents(state: StateDep, page: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1)] = 1, per_page: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1, le=100)] = 24, dataset: DatasetParam = None) -&gt; dict[str, Any]:` — note this is a plain `def`, so the blocking Lance work is correctly threadpooled (the finding does not misreport this). :171-174 `total = ds.count_rows()` / `offset = max(0, (page - 1) * per_page)` / `tbl = ds.to_table(columns=columns, limit=per_page, offset=offset)` / `return {"total": total, "page": page, "docs": tbl.to_pylist()}` — quoted accurately. `grep -rn 'MAX_OFFSET' services/ packages/` → 0 hits, confirmed. Ungated confirmed: no `CheckerDep`/`CurrentSubject` in the signature (consistent with the audit's VS-03 count of 25 ungated viewer routes); reachable as `/api/explorer/documents` via the gateway row `("/api/explorer", "/api", *viewer)`. No audit entry cites viewer system.py.

</details>

<details><summary><b>No shared `PaginationParams` dependency or `Page[Item]` envelope: eight page-parameter vocabularies, seven ceilings, and six routes whose real bound is invisible in OpenAPI</b> <i>(pagination.md, CONFIRMED)</i></summary>

**Rule.** pagination.md § '`PaginationParams` — reusable dep': 'One `BaseModel` + one factory dep, used across every list endpoint'; and § 'Paginated response envelope (generic, PEP 695)': `class Page[Item](BaseModel)`.

**Sites:** `services/lineage/src/lineage/api/v1/endpoints/discovery.py:43`, `services/lineage/src/lineage/api/v1/endpoints/runs.py:120`, `services/notifications/src/notifications/api/inbox.py:56`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/access_admin.py:170`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/tables.py:146`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/system.py:153`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/events.py:57`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/api.py:428`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/graph.py:463`

**Why it matters.** Measured at HEAD: `grep -rn 'PaginationParams|Page\[|build_page|get_pagination' services/ packages/` (non-test) returns **zero**. Every paginated route reinvented the wire shape. Eight distinct vocabularies — `limit`+`offset`, `limit`+`after`, `limit`+`cursor`, `page_size`+`continuation`, `page_token`+`limit`, `page`+`per_page`, `since`, bare `limit`. Seven ceilings — 100 (`/documents` per_page, notifications `INBOX_PAGE_LIMIT_MAX`, catalog `_MAX_PAGE_SIZE`), 200 (annotator versions, `/search/similar`), 500 (lineage `_MAX_LIMIT`, `_EVENTS_RETURN`, dlq, viewer `/pages`), 1000 (lineage `/readers`, catalog `/v1/model`), viewer graph `_MAX_LIMIT`=1000, plus three with none. And six routes clamp in the handler or service layer instead of declaring `Query(ge=…, le=…)`, so the OpenAPI the gateway aggregates and the frontend generates against advertises an unbounded integer while the real bound is buried: `ingest/api.py:437 records = await store.recent(min(max(limit, 0), 200))`, `viewer/graph.py:475 limit = max(1, min(limit, _MAX_LIMIT))`, `viewer/voice.py:86 n: int = 20` clamped at `voice_service.py:417 n = max(1, min(n, _MAX_N))`, `search/spec.py:101-104` clamped at 200, plus `graph.py:_enforce_limit`. A caller reading the schema cannot tell a bounded route from an unbounded one, which is exactly how the two genuinely unbounded ones above (`catalog/versions.py:55`, and previously `compute/routes.py:66`, already filed as COMPUTE-UNBOUNDED-QUERY-PARAMS) went unnoticed. `catalog/versions.py:55` and the `page_token`/`limit` trio are the routes where the invisible bound is in fact absent.

**Fix.** Put one `PaginationParams` BaseModel + `PaginationDep` factory in `packages/service-kit` (it already owns `make_service_app`, `SettingsDep` and the middleware, so it is the right home), with `page_size: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1, le=100, alias='limit')]` and a `KeysetParams` sibling for the cursor routes, plus a `Page[Item]` / `CursorPage[Item]` generic. Then converge in this order, cheapest first: (1) declare the six body-clamped routes' real ceilings in `Query(...)` so the schema stops lying — this is a one-line edit each and changes no behaviour; (2) bound the three genuinely unbounded ones; (3) migrate new routes to the shared dep. Do NOT retrofit the spec-shaped ones — catalog's `ListTablesResponse`/`ListNamespacesResponse` `page_token` is the Lance Namespace spec's wire contract, not local drift, and must keep its shape.

**Evidence.**

```
Zero hits: `grep -rn 'PaginationParams\|Page\[\|build_page' --include=*.py services/ packages/ | grep -v tests` → 0. The eight vocabularies, verbatim: lineage/discovery.py:43-44 `offset: Annotated[int, Query(ge=0)] = 0,` / `limit: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1, le=_MAX_LIMIT)] = 100,`; lineage/runs.py:120-122 `after: Annotated[int | None, Query(ge=1)] = None,` / `limit: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1, le=_EVENTS_RETURN)] = _EVENTS_RETURN,`; notifications/api/inbox.py:56-57 `limit: Annotated[int | None, Query(ge=1, le=INBOX_PAGE_LIMIT_MAX, ...)] = None,` / `cursor: Annotated[str | None, Query(min_length=1, ...)] = None,`; catalog/access_admin.py:170-171 `page_size: Annotated[int | None, Query(ge=1, le=_MAX_PAGE_SIZE)] = None,` / `continuation: Annotated[str | None, Query(...)] = None,`; catalog/tables.py:146-147 `page_token: str | None = None,` / `limit: int | None = None,`; viewer/system.py:153-154 `page: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1)] = 1,` / `per_page: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1, le=100)] = 24,`; catalog/events.py:57 `since: Annotated[int, Query(ge=0, ...)] = 0,`; ingest/api.py:428 `limit: int = 50,`. Failure: a frontend generating a typed client from the gateway's aggregated OpenAPI sees `limit`, `page_size`, `per_page` and `n` as four different concepts with four different caps across seven services, and sees `ingest limit`, `viewer graph limit` and `catalog history limit` as identically unbounded when only the last one actually is.
```

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Every measurement re-run at HEAD and every quoted signature verified verbatim. `grep -rn 'PaginationParams\|Page\[\|build_page\|get_pagination' --include=*.py services/ packages/ | grep -v test` → zero output, confirmed. The vocabularies, read from the files: lineage/discovery.py:43-44 `offset: Annotated[int, Query(ge=0)] = 0,` / `limit: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1, le=_MAX_LIMIT)] = 100,`; lineage/runs.py:120-121 `after: Annotated[int | None, Query(ge=1)] = None,` / `limit: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1, le=_EVENTS_RETURN)] = _EVENTS_RETURN,`; notifications/api/inbox.py:56-57 `limit: Annotated[int | None, Query(ge=1, le=INBOX_PAGE_LIMIT_MAX, description=_LIMIT_HELP)] = None,` / `cursor: Annotated[str | None, Query(min_length=1, …)] = None,`; catalog/access_admin.py:170-171 `page_size: Annotated[int | None, Query(ge=1, le=_MAX_PAGE_SIZE)] = None,` / `continuation: …`; catalog/tables.py:146-147 `page_token: str | None = None,` / `limit: int | None = None,`; viewer/system.py:152-153 `page` / `per_page`; catalog/events.py:57 `since: Annotated[int, Query(ge=0, …)] = 0,`; ingest/api.py:428 `limit: int = 50,` clamped downstream at ingest/api.py:452 `records = await store.recent(min(max(limit, 0), 200))`; viewer/graph.py:463 `def get_subgraph(state: StateDep, entity_id: str | None = None, limit: int = 150, …)` clamped at :475 `limit = max(1, min(limit, _MAX_LIMIT))`. The cross-reference to COMPUTE-UNBOUNDED-QUERY-PARAMS is real (open_python-audit.md:710, low). Low is the right grade — this is a consistency/DX observation with no concrete data or availability failure of its own; its two named genuinely-unbounded routes are the separate findings above.

</details>

---

## Appendix A — what each lane found the estate to be

### `conventions` — core-conventions.md + anti-patterns.md

This estate is unusually disciplined on this reference pair, and the honest headline is that most of the lane came back CLEAN by measurement, not by sampling. RootModel: zero. `json_encoders`: zero (and zero `@field_serializer` too — nothing here has ever needed custom serialization). `ORJSONResponse`/`UJSONResponse`: zero. Ellipsis defaults: zero. Field constraints contradicting their defaults: zero — all 27 constrained-with-default fields are correctly spelled `T | None = Field(default=None, ge=…)`, which is the exact shape the anti-pattern row asks for. `python-jose`, `async_asgi_testclient`, `@app.on_event`, default-arg `Depends()`: all zero. The two `@app.api_route(methods=[…])` multiplexers (gateway `/api/{path:path}`, compute's Ray-Serve proxy) are transparent reverse proxies with no `if request.method` ladder and a module docstring saying the choice is deliberate — sanctioned, not drift. The async/sync sweep is where the real story is, and it is a story of a fix that mostly landed: four of the five E2 high/medium items I could re-verify at HEAD are FIXED (ING-02 now `await asyncio.to_thread(verifier.verify, raw)`; ANN-01, VS-01 and VS-02 all now route their blocking bodies through `run_in_threadpool`; F-LIN-05's lineage `security.py` has no `async def` left at all), three are still open at shifted line numbers, and exactly one genuinely new blocking-on-the-loop site survived a precise AST sweep over every `async def` in `services/` and `packages/` — the medallion producer's OIDC door, which is the *other half of a duplicated function* (audit DUP-03) that the ING-02 fix never travelled to. That is the shape of the remaining risk here: not a service-wide misunderstanding, but a copy that the fix missed. The one thing the prior audit never touched at all is `response_model=`, which appears 21 times declaring exactly the same type the function already returns — a pure-redundancy pattern that has propagated across seven of thirteen services and is now the estate's default reflex for a typed route.

*Checked clean:* `RootModel — zero occurrences estate-wide in services/ and packages/ (core-conventions § Do not use Pydantic RootModel)`, `model_config json_encoders — zero occurrences (anti-patterns: deprecated in Pydantic v2). Note the corollary: @field_serializer / model_serializer / PlainSerializer are ALSO zero, i.e. no model here has ever needed custom serialization, so there is nothing to migrate`, `ORJSONResponse / UJSONResponse / orjson / ujson — zero occurrences (core-conventions § Performance)`, `Ellipsis defaults (Field(...), Query(...), = ...) — zero occurrences in non-test code (core-conventions § Do not use Ellipsis)`, `Field constraints contradicting their defaults — zero. All 27 sites carrying gt/ge/lt/le/min_length/max_length/pattern alongside a default are spelled `T | None = Field(default=None, …)`, the exact fix the anti-pattern row prescribes; no numeric default violates its own bound`, `One HTTP operation per function — no multiplexed application route exists. The two api_route(methods=[…]) sites (services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py:317, services/compute/src/compute/proxy.py:58-59) are transparent proxies that forward request.method verbatim with no if/else ladder, and compute/proxy.py:3-4 documents the choice; sanctioned per the estate's read-the-comments rule`, `prefix / tags on the router rather than include_router() — no production violation. The only include_router(prefix=…) outside tests is packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/__init__.py:148, which injects the runtime-configurable settings.api_prefix and cannot live on a module-level APIRouter`, `Annotated-style params and dependencies — zero default-arg `= Depends(...)` estate-wide (re-verified by grep; confirms the prior audit's claim still holds at HEAD)`, `Legacy Optional[…] / Union[…] — zero occurrences (re-verified)`, ``import requests` — one occurrence, packages/ray-kit/src/ray_kit/dashboard.py:23, used ONLY for the exception type in RAY_TRANSIENT_ERRORS (line 79) because the Ray Job SDK raises requests exceptions internally. No requests.get/post call exists anywhere; the substantive anti-pattern (sync requests inside async def) is absent. This slightly corrects the prior audit's flat 'zero import requests' claim`, `python-jose and async_asgi_testclient — zero occurrences`, `@app.on_event — zero occurrences; every one of the 15 apps uses an @asynccontextmanager lifespan`, `span.record_exception — zero live uses; the only two occurrences (services/notifications/src/notifications/api/fanout.py:169, services/flows/src/flows/activities.py:69) are comments explaining why it is NOT used`, `SDK-side trace sampling (TraceIdRatioBased) — zero occurrences`, `Middleware hygiene — both pure-ASGI middlewares (catalog/api/body_limit.py:35, catalog/api/load_shed.py:66) open with the required `scope["type"] != "http"` passthrough; no middleware reads request.body(); no ContextVar is set in middleware; no httpx client is constructed inside a dispatch()`, `Sync DaprClient inside async def — zero. Every async construction site (medallion mover.py:56 / producer.py:71, catalog main.py:141/180, annotator main.py:94, maintenance service.py:156, medallion workflow.py x6) imports from dapr.aio.clients`, `Sync httpx.Client called from an async route without a threadpool — zero remaining. All three medallion transform.py catalog_http uses (lines ~790, ~838) go through run_in_threadpool; annotator jobs.py routes are plain `def`; viewer system.py /health is plain `def``, `Blocking work in a plain `def` route — not reported anywhere, per the standing ruling (FastAPI threadpools these). controlplane/routes.py:37, annotator/annotations/wire.py:39 and the catalog's 163 run_in_threadpool calls are the correct shape and were left alone`

### `di-lifespan` — dependencies.md + production-patterns.md

Read both references in full before opening code. The honest picture: this estate's DI layer is genuinely strong on the things references/dependencies.md actually prescribes, and weak in exactly one structural place — the boundary between "a dependency" and "a module-level function that happens to be called from a dependency". Every dependency in the estate is an `Annotated[T, Depends(...)]` alias (zero bare `Depends()`, zero default-arg `Depends()`), every one of them reads a resource that a lifespan put on `app.state` through a tiny `Request`-reading wrapper, and there is not a single `yield` dependency anywhere — which is correct here rather than a gap, because the P7a ruling removed the app relational DB and nothing else needs per-request cleanup, so the whole "yield + scope + cleanup ordering" half of dependencies.md has no surface to land on. `app.dependency_overrides` IS the test seam and is used properly (30+ files, overriding at the registration key via `X.__metadata__[0].dependency`), and `tests/unit/test_auth_deps_resolve.py` exists specifically because the estate already caught itself hiding a 422 behind an override — that is a codebase that understands the rule. Per-request caching is respected: `make_auth_deps` is called exactly once per service so `authenticate` resolves once per request no matter how many gates reach for it, and I found no site that needs `use_cache=False` (none exists, correctly). Class-based deps: zero. Lifespans are `@asynccontextmanager` everywhere, `app.state` is the carrier everywhere, and nothing constructs a client at import time except `ingest.create_app()` — which uvicorn's `--factory` runs inside the loop, so the aiohttp `TCPConnector` there does not blow up (I checked; it raises `RuntimeError: no running event loop` outside one). Where it goes wrong is the shutdown half and the *edges*: four lifespans build an aiohttp-backed OpenFGA session and drop it on the floor, while `notifications/lifespan.py:176-183` carries a five-line comment describing precisely the failure the other four still have. Middleware is the thinnest area — the reference's CORS→RequestID→Timing→Logging chain exists only in `make_service_app` (5 of 14 apps), the Logging leg was deferred and never revisited, the ContextVar half was never built, and the resulting `X-Request-ID` is returned to callers while being readable in exactly zero log lines; the nine hand-assembled apps register no request-scoped middleware at all, and the gateway — the one public front door — registers none either while serving an unauthenticated aggregated Swagger of the whole estate at `/api/docs`. Two calibration corrections worth recording: the brief's "15 apps" is 14 served module-level `app` objects (the 15th is the `create_viewer_app`/`create_search_app` test-seam factories, already filed as X12), and the standing "zero `import requests`" is `packages/ray-kit/src/ray_kit/dashboard.py:23` — a legitimate one, imported only to name `requests.exceptions.RequestException` in the `RAY_TRANSIENT_ERRORS` tuple, no HTTP client involved. I engaged with the inline comments rather than reporting their surface: the catalog's in-place mutation of its `lru_cache`d Settings (catalog-api-17) is NOT an isolated shape — `lineage/core/config.py:216` and `medallion/core/config.py:485` carry the identical `apply_dapr_secrets(settings)` splice, and both docstrings say so explicitly ("Symmetric with … lineage / the catalog lifespan"). It is a recorded convention with a stated rationale (fail-closed OpenBao sole-source, sync fetch run through `run_in_threadpool`), all three call it from the lifespan before any client reads the secret, and no fourth service copied it without saying so — so I did not re-file it as drift.

*Checked clean:* `yield-dependencies and cleanup ordering — ZERO generator dependencies exist estate-wide (scanned every module containing `Depends(`; the ~45 distinct dependency callables are all plain `def`/`async def` returns). This is correct rather than a gap: with no app relational DB (P7a) and no Redis, nothing in the request path owns a resource needing per-request release, so dependencies.md's `scope="request"` / `scope="function"` distinction has no surface here. Every app-scoped resource is a lifespan resource instead, which is the right layer.`, `Per-request dependency caching is intact and load-bearing: `make_auth_deps(settings_dep)` is called exactly once per service (viewer/api/security.py:30, flows/security.py:32, notifications/api/security.py:29), so `authenticate` is one callable per app and FastAPI resolves it once per request no matter how many gates reach for it.
