### `errors` — exception-handlers.md

The estate does not have two error systems — it has four response envelopes, and which one a client gets depends on the app AND the layer the failure came from. Measured per app: **register_handlers only** (DomainError + a 422 override, and NO `Exception` catch-all) in compute, controlplane, flows, notifications — all five make_service_app apps except ingest; **install_problem_handlers only** (LanceNamespaceError + 422 + an `Exception` catch-all) in catalog, lineage, maintenance, medallion/producer, medallion/mover; **BOTH** in viewer, search, annotator, ingest; **NEITHER** in gateway. That is 14 production apps (9 direct `FastAPI(...)` + 5 via the factory) plus the two test-seam factories `create_viewer_app`/`create_search_app`, which install only `register_handlers` (already filed as X12). The mixing epic E4 names is real and mostly already filed, so my value is elsewhere: the four envelopes. (1) `ns_errors` problem+json with the spec's numeric `code` — the nine lance apps' handled errors. (2) `service_kit.exceptions` problem+json without `code` — the fleet apps, and, in the lance apps, any `service_kit` DomainError, which is an `HTTPException` subclass and so falls to FastAPI's default handler and comes back as bare `{"detail": …}` with `application/json`. (3) `text/plain` "Internal Server Error" — the four apps with no `Exception` catch-all, verified by reproduction. (4) seven hand-built problem+json bodies (two catalog middlewares, catalog maintenance mode, three medallion routes, flows' `RunRefused`) that carry the right media type but omit `code`, plus `service_kit/draining.py`, which stamps `content-type: application/problem+json` on a body that is `{"detail": …}` and nothing else. On the reference's other rules the estate is genuinely good: every app but the gateway overrides `RequestValidationError` into the errors-array shape; the one catch-all (`ns_errors.handle_unexpected_error`) does the right thing — logs the traceback, returns a fixed generic detail — and `_UNREDACTED_5XX` is a carefully reasoned exception, not sloppiness; logging lives in the handlers, not the routes; `Retry-After` accompanies every 429/503 that promises one; no handler puts a stack trace in a body. The leaks that exist are on the raise side, not the handler side — a `{exc}` interpolated into a domain-error detail that the handler then serves verbatim. On `except Exception` around route bodies: there are 262 in `services/` + `packages/`, and the audit has already filed the load-bearing ones (VS-06, VS-07, ING-06, MAINT-05/06, F-LIN-11, catalog-api-08, SK-20, ratch-010, FLOWS-NODE-ESCAPE); I checked the ones the audit does not cite and found flows' inline-lane degrade at routes.py:190 to be a documented, reasoned decision rather than a defect, so I am not claiming that surface clean, only that I found nothing new above the filed set.

*Checked clean:* `RequestValidationError override present and correctly shaped: 13 of the 14 production apps override it into the {type,title,status,errors[{field,message,type}]} shape the reference prescribes — via register_handlers (exceptions.py:121) or install_problem_handlers (ns_errors.py:135) or both. The gateway is the only app left on FastAPI's default verbose 422 body, which is inside the already-filed GW-NO-PROBLEM-JSON.`, `Catch-all Exception handler does NOT hide bugs where one exists: ns_errors.handle_unexpected_error (:148) calls log.exception with method/path before returning a fixed generic body, and Starlette re-raises past ServerErrorMiddleware so OTel still records the span error. The problem is the four apps that have no catch-all at all, not the behaviour of the one that does.`, `No handler leaks a stack trace or exception internals into a response body. All four handler bodies (exceptions.py:111/:121, ns_errors.py:123/:135/:148) emit only fixed fields; ns_errors.problem_detail redacts every 5xx detail to "Internal Server Error" with a single, explicitly reasoned exception for 501 capability answers (_UNREDACTED_5XX, ns_errors.py:88). Leaks in this estate come from `{exc}` interpolated at the raise site, not from the handlers.`, `Logging happens inside the handlers, not the routes — exceptions.py:113 logs >=500 with exc_info; ns_errors.py:129-133 splits log.info for 501 from log.exception for real 5xx. Neither installer asks routes to log.`, `Retry-After accompanies every response that promises retry: load_shed.py:99 (429), maintenance_mode.py:42 (503), draining.py:71 (503), produce.py:101 / ingest_media.py:77 / train.py:55 (503). No RateLimitError-class hierarchy exists, but the header contract the reference pins is honoured wherever a 429/503 is produced.`, `The lance ErrorCode -> HTTP status map is complete: all 24 SDK codes are present in ns_errors._STATUS, including the branch codes 22/23 that a prior audit caught missing, and a completeness test pins the enum.`, `Legacy Optional[]/Union[] in services/ + packages/: zero (grep confirmed).`, `Default-arg `= Depends(...)` in services/ + packages/: zero (grep confirmed); the estate uses Annotated aliases throughout.`, `@app.on_event: zero — the three grep hits are a test helper named _promotion_event, not the decorator.`, `Pydantic v1 idiom and BackgroundTasks-for-pageable-work: no hits in the error-handling surface.`, `Not clean but out of this lane, recorded so it is not lost: `import requests` is NOT zero — packages/ray-kit/src/ray_kit/dashboard.py:23 has one; and boto3 is imported outside packages/storage in three files (catalog/core/vending.py, catalog/services/warehouses.py, service_kit/lakehouse/records.py). Neither is an error-contract defect; flagging for whichever lane owns them.`

### `health` — health-checks.md

The estate is bimodal, and the split is architectural, not accidental. Ten apps (catalog, lineage, maintenance, medallion producer + the 3 mover deployments, viewer, search, annotator) mount `service_kit.probes` at the root and get `/livez` + `/readyz`; the chart's `lance.appProbes` helper (chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:914) wires them correctly — startupProbe→/livez, liveness→/livez, readiness→/readyz — and I rendered the chart to confirm every one of those 16 first-party containers probes a path its app actually serves. The other six (compute, controlplane, flows, ingest, notifications, gateway) serve one liveness route each (`/api/health`, or `/healthz` for the gateway) and the chart points BOTH probes at it; `open_python-audit.md`'s X8 already files that for four of them. What X8 does not cover, and what this lane found, is the substance underneath: `/readyz` in this estate is almost never a readiness check. `_serve` (probes.py:37) maps `degraded` to 503, so there is no 200-with-degraded state, and two services (annotator, notifications) say so in their own docstrings and deliberately report a broken component as `ready` rather than take the 503. lineage's `_graph_ready` is the ONLY readiness check in the estate that touches a dependency (an AGE pool connection + a real Cypher). Nothing anywhere probes S3/RustFS, Lance, OpenFGA or the Dapr sidecar from readiness — viewer and search log a dependency failure and then set `startup_complete = True` two lines later. Liveness is clean everywhere: no liveness probe touches a dependency, and no chart liveness probe is pointed at a readiness route. The `shutting_down` flag is set by 9 of 15 lifespans and is genuinely read by `/readyz` and by `service_kit.draining`, but only medallion's producer applies the drain gate to its doors — the three mover pods, which run the actual cascade transform, accept a stage trigger while terminating. `open_python-audit.md` has zero hits on `chart/templates/*`, on `services/notifications`, and on `runners/`, so the chart cross-check and those two services are entirely fresh ground.

*Checked clean:* `Liveness never touches a dependency (health-checks.md rule 1) — verified on all 16 first-party probe endpoints: every /livez, /api/health and /healthz returns a static Liveness()/{ok:true} with no I/O, and no chart livenessProbe is pointed at /readyz or any dependency-checking route.`, `No chart probe points at a path its app does not serve — rendered the full chart (helm template, 39,705 lines) and cross-checked all 16 first-party containers' startup/liveness/readiness paths against each app's mounted routers. Zero mismatches: the /api/health probes land because chart/values.yaml:67 sets RASK_API_PREFIX=/api, which is what make_service_app mounts under.`, `Readiness is per-request fresh — no @lru_cache, TTL or memoisation on any readyz handler or ready_check (_namespace_ready, _graph_ready, _actor_plane_ready, actor_plane_ready).`, `Probes are mounted WITHOUT the API version prefix — /livez + /readyz are root-mounted on all 10 apps that have them; notifications deliberately uses make_service_app's proxy_router slot for exactly this (__init__.py:30-34).`, `Probe handlers are async def — every service_kit-sourced probe and every /api/health handler is async, so a probe cannot queue behind the blocking threadpool. The one sync-def liveness in a fleet app (service_kit.media.probes) was already deleted; the only survivors are two sealed runners (see finding).`, `viewer's sync `def health` (services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/system.py:35) is CORRECT, not drift: it does blocking httpx pings, it is a frontend badge rather than a k8s probe, and no chart probe points at it.`, `database.md pooling/health content does not apply: no relational DB in any fleet service. lineage's AGE store is the sanctioned exception and it is the ONE service whose readiness actually opens a pool connection and runs a query.`, `Calibration re-verified in one grep: zero legacy Optional[/Union[, zero `= Depends(` defaults, zero @app.on_event across services/ and packages/. One `import requests` exists (packages/ray-kit/src/ray_kit/dashboard.py:23) but is used solely for `requests.exceptions.RequestException` in RAY_TRANSIENT_ERRORS (line 79) — the Ray Job SDK's own transport type; there is no requests.get/post call anywhere.`, `Probe traffic is kept out of the trace stream, and the gate for it is rendered-chart-driven rather than a hardcoded list (tests/unit/test_invariants.py:1880).`

### `files-streaming` — file-handling.md + streaming.md

The byte surface here is small and, where it was written on purpose, genuinely good: the catalog's `GET /v1/table/{id}/blobs` (data.py:611-689) is a textbook range-aware streamer — 206 + Content-Range, strong ETag, If-Range downgrade, RFC-9110-correct "ignore what we don't support" rather than a guessy 416, bounded windows iterated off the loop; the viewer's `/api/media` (media.py:305-356) is the same shape over Lance `take_blobs` handles, and `service_kit/media/middleware.py` deliberately refuses `BaseHTTPMiddleware` because it would buffer those 206 bodies. Path traversal is a non-issue by construction: no route joins a request value onto a filesystem path — media/annotator routes go through `validate_doc_key`'s descriptor regex whitelist, ingest's `confine_to_local_root`/`_under` resolve-then-compare correctly, and lineage's only `StaticFiles` mount is a fixed package directory. The real weakness is the INGRESS side, and it is systemic rather than per-route: `BodySizeLimitMiddleware` exists in exactly one of the fifteen apps (catalog), the gateway — the single front door every browser request crosses — buffers each proxied body whole with no cap at all, and the two multipart upload routes (viewer voice, search image) bound only their own `read()` while Starlette has already spooled the entire part into `/tmp` (an unsized emptyDir) before the handler is entered. The second weakness is an error-path one the reference's temp-file section anticipates: exceptions raised INSIDE a streaming body iterator or after a cache eviction escape the app's exception handlers and become a truncated 200 or a bare 500. Two calibration notes: `import requests` is not literally zero — `packages/ray-kit/src/ray_kit/dashboard.py:23` has one, but only to name `requests.exceptions.RequestException` in the `RAY_TRANSIENT_ERRORS` tuple, so the spirit of the ruling holds; and `annotator/tasks.py:452` runs a blocking pyarrow IPC decode of the just-read body inline in an `async def`, which is the async lane's rule (adjacent to ANN-01's sites but a different mechanism), so I have left it here rather than filing it against these two references.

*Checked clean:* `Path-traversal on any path built from a request value: zero sites. No route in the estate joins a request value onto a filesystem path. Media/annotator/frames routes key on `service_kit.lancekit.keys.validate_doc_key` (whitelist regex from the descriptor, keys.py:17-28) and `chunk_key_filter` (int-cast positional fields); `viewer/services/clips.py:38-42` builds its cache name from the dataset stem + that whitelisted key; ingest's `confine_to_local_root` (adapters.py:52-64) and `_under` (adapters.py:117-122) both `resolve()` BEFORE comparing and document why; `lineage/main.py:196-198` mounts StaticFiles on a fixed `Path(__file__).parent/'static'`.`, `Range support where it matters: catalog `/v1/table/{id}/blobs` (data.py:611-689) and viewer `/api/media` (media.py:305-356) both implement 206 + Content-Range + Accept-Ranges + correct 416, and both correctly IGNORE (200) an unsupported/multi-range header per RFC 9110 §14.2 instead of erroring. `/api/media-clip` delegates range handling to `FileResponse`, which Starlette 1.3.1 implements natively (responses.py:360-380).`, ``UploadFile` never read whole into memory: both upload routes use a bounded read — `voice.py:142` `await file.read(_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES + 1)` and `search/api/v1/router.py:234` `await image.read(_MAX_IMAGE_BYTES + 1)`. Zero uncapped `await file.read()` in the estate. (The bound is defeated upstream by the multipart spool — see finding 1 — but the route-level rule itself is honoured.)`, ``BackgroundTasks` used for nothing at all, temp cleanup included — zero occurrences outside comments that explicitly forbid it (`catalog/core/lineage_emit.py:9,712`, `catalog/api/v1/endpoints/data.py:267,528`, `tables.py:430`). The estate awaits its post-response work inline on purpose.`, ``@app.on_event`: zero. `open(path,'rb').read()` returned from a route: zero. `aiofiles`: zero (correct — `FileResponse` is used where bytes are on disk, which the reference prefers). `import requests` used for an actual HTTP call: zero (the single import at `packages/ray-kit/src/ray_kit/dashboard.py:23` exists only to build the `RAY_TRANSIENT_ERRORS` exception tuple at line 79; every real call is httpx).`, `Gateway response streaming is correct: `gateway/__init__.py:343-359` sends upstream with `stream=True`, re-emits `upstream_resp.aiter_raw()` (RAW, so a `Content-Encoding` it forwards still matches the bytes) and closes via `BackgroundTask(aclose)` — no response-side buffering, and Range/Content-Range/Accept-Ranges pass through untouched because `_HOP_BY_HOP` does not list them.`, `Temp files are written to `tempfile.gettempdir()`, never the app working directory (`viewer/services/clips.py:29`, `voice_service.py:541-543`, `ingest/runtime.py:158`), and the ffmpeg failure/timeout paths in `clips.py:93-101` both `tmp.unlink(missing_ok=True)` before raising — the error-path cleanup the reference asks for is present.`, `Streaming-reference conformance for SSE/JSON-Lines: not applicable — the estate has zero SSE and zero JSON-Lines routes. Every streaming body here is a byte stream, which is the file-handling reference's territory.`

### `pagination` — pagination.md

Of 118 `@router.get`/`@app.get` routes at HEAD, roughly 45 are collection routes and **14 declare a bounded page parameter** (`Annotated[..., Query(ge=…, le=…)]`). There is **no shared `PaginationParams` dependency and no generic `Page[Item]` envelope anywhere in the estate** — `grep -rn 'PaginationParams|Page\[|build_page'` over `services/` + `packages/` returns 0 — so every paginated route invented its own vocabulary: `limit`+`offset` (lineage/discovery.py:43), `limit`+`after` (lineage/runs.py:120), `limit`+`cursor` (notifications/inbox.py:56), `page_size`+`continuation` (catalog/access_admin.py:170), `page_token`+`limit` (catalog/tables.py:146, namespaces.py:172,630), `page`+`per_page` (viewer/system.py:153), `since` (catalog/events.py:57), and bare `limit` in nine more places. Ceilings measured: 100, 200, 500, 1000, and three with none at all. The estate is **not offset-shaped** — it is mostly keyset/cursor, which is the right instinct for a Lance/AGE/S3 backing store, and `MAX_OFFSET` is absent because only one route (`/documents`) does true offset paging. `services/notifications/api/inbox.py` + `api/cursor.py` is a textbook implementation of the reference's cursor section — opaque base64, padding-stripped, unmintable cursors refused as 400, `le=INBOX_PAGE_LIMIT_MAX`, deliberate reasoning about `next_cursor` on a fully-filtered page — and nothing else in the estate reuses it. The characteristic defect here is not "no pagination"; it is **truncation without a continuation**: five governed catalog listings intersect against a single `fga.list_objects` call that the OpenFGA server silently caps, `/v1/model` slices with no `total` and no cursor, and the two fastest-growing lineage collections (`/runs`, `/jobs`) accept no page parameter at all. One calibration correction, offered honestly rather than pursued: the brief says `import requests` is zero estate-wide; it is one — `packages/ray-kit/src/ray_kit/dashboard.py:23` — outside this lane, so I did not chase it.

*Checked clean:* `Legacy `Optional[...]` / `Union[...]` on pagination params: 0 hits across services/ + packages/ (non-test).`, `Default-arg `= Depends(...)` in any route signature: 0 hits — every dependency is `Annotated[T, Depends(...)]`.`, ``@dataclass` for filter/sort/pagination params (reference's project-wide ban): 0 — the four `@dataclass` uses in the estate are value objects already filed as ANN-15 / CAT-CORE-16 / PS-14, none of them request params.`, ``BackgroundTasks` used for pageable work: 0 — the six textual hits are all comments in catalog explaining why lineage emits are inline-awaited instead (lineage_emit.py:9,712-713; data.py:267,528; tables.py:430).`, ``@app.on_event`: 0 hits.`, `Cursor opacity where a cursor exists: notifications mints an opaque, padding-stripped urlsafe-base64 cursor (api/cursor.py:30,42) and refuses one it did not mint with a 400, exactly as the reference prescribes; lineage's `?after=` is an integer keyset over `seq`, which the reference sanctions for internal services.`, `Deterministic secondary sort on every keyset cursor: catalog's `_paginate` (tables.py:197-209) operates on `sorted(set(names))` so the name IS the total order; lineage `/events` keys on the monotonic `seq`; notifications keys on `(occurred_at, notification_id)` — the reference's 'ordering only by created_at' anti-pattern does not occur.`, `Search top-n clamping: `SearchSpec` clamps `n` and `rerank_n` to 200 with a recorded reason (spec.py:101-109) rather than rejecting, and `/search/similar` declares `Query(ge=1, le=200)` (search/api/v1/router.py:306) — no unbounded k reaches the vector plane.`, `Write-side row bounds: `POST /produce`'s `rows` is `Query(ge=1, le=1_000_000)` with an inline note explaining why an unbounded value would be 'a request that never comes back rather than an error a caller can read' (medallion/api/produce.py:55-58).`, `Sync-vs-async on paginated routes: every route that slices a Lance table or walks the object store is either a plain `def` (viewer/system.py:151 `documents`, viewer/graph.py:462 `get_subgraph`) or pushes the blocking half through `run_in_threadpool` (catalog/versions.py:88, catalog/tables.py:186) — the reference's 'sync ORM call in an async route' anti-pattern does not appear on any paginated route (objects.py is the exception and is already filed as VS-01).`, ``MAX_OFFSET`: absent estate-wide, but only one route (`viewer /documents`) does true offset paging, so this is a one-route gap (reported) rather than an estate-wide one.`

### `authn-authz` — authn.md + authz.md

This estate is unusually strong at the authn/authz CORE and unusually uneven at its EDGES. The core is genuinely reference-grade: `service_kit/governed/oidc.py` is PyJWT + PyJWKClient with a local asymmetric-algorithm allowlist (HS*/none rejected to defeat alg-confusion), required exp/iat/aud/sub, issuer pinned to the discovery document, HTTPS enforcement, split-horizon discovery, and every crypto error mapped to one opaque UnauthenticatedError — it beats the reference file it is measured against. `governed/fga.py` fails CLOSED on every read and write path (tenacity retry, then ServiceUnavailableError → 503, never a silent allow or an empty list that reads as "nobody"), `governed/deps.py` encodes the three-outcome checker with 503 for enabled-but-unwired, and `dapr_auth.py` carries a real measured bypass (gateway-laundered `dapr-caller-app-id`) with the fix at the edge plus the door. Identity is never taken from an unverified header for AUTHORIZATION anywhere: SK-01's `X-User` survives at exactly its three filed sites and is attribution-only, no new copies appeared, and the one other caller-supplied identity header (`x-lance-originator`) is a documented targeting hint re-authorized at delivery. The unevenness is at the perimeter. THREE of the fifteen apps have no authn/authz code path at all — `search` (filed as X6), and `compute` and `controlplane`, which are filed nowhere and are both publicly proxied by the gateway. Router-level `dependencies=[...]` is used exactly where the reference asks for it in the catalog, in lineage's per-name routers and in the annotator's task plane; everywhere else the gate is hand-written per handler, which is how `GET /promotions/{id}` ended up with a conditional that skips authorization for an anonymous caller while its sibling write refuses one. `batch_check` is used correctly in lineage and notifications and is not used in the viewer's dataset filter; that family is otherwise already filed (ING-05, catalog-api-12, F-LIN-13). The single most consequential thing this lane found that no existing finding covers is not a missing gate at all: `lancekit.open_reader`/`open_writer` fall back to the estate's service credential whenever a request arrives without a bearer, which is the confused deputy the same functions' docstrings name as the thing to avoid — and the non-request caller those docstrings cite as the reason for the fallback does not exist in the tree. One calibration correction for whoever reads this next: `import requests` is NOT zero — `packages/ray-kit/src/ray_kit/dashboard.py:23` has one (outside this lane's scope, flagged so it is not recorded clean elsewhere).

*Checked clean:* `OIDC signature verification is REAL and correct estate-wide — service_kit/governed/oidc.py:234-254 calls jwt.decode with the JWKS-resolved signing key, options={"require": ["exp","iat","aud","sub"], "verify_exp": True, "verify_iat": True, "verify_aud": True, "verify_iss": True}, issuer pinned to the discovery document's own issuer (with a mismatch check at :193 against a tampered discovery doc), and configurable leeway. There is exactly one verifier in the estate; no service hand-rolls JWT crypto.`, `Algorithm confusion is defended: DEFAULT_ALLOWED_ALGORITHMS (oidc.py:54-61) is asymmetric-only, _safe_algorithms (:155-168) intersects it with the provider's advertised set and can only narrow it, and none/HS* are unreachable. An empty intersection raises rather than falling back.`, `Library choice matches house style: pyjwt[crypto]>=2.8, httpx for discovery, zero python-jose and zero passlib in the tree (the only two 'jose' hits are comments in oidc.py explaining why it is NOT used).`, `Token payloads are Pydantic, never dict — IDToken (oidc.py:64-84) with extra='allow', and a pydantic ValidationError on a signed-but-malformed payload is deliberately caught inside the 401 mapping (:255-260) so it cannot escape as a 500.`, `Crypto/JWT errors never leak: every jwt.PyJWTError / PyJWKClientError / ValidationError becomes one opaque UnauthenticatedError("Invalid or expired token"); HTTPBearer(auto_error=False) is used everywhere so 401s render as the service's own problem+json rather than Starlette's default with a library-shaped WWW-Authenticate.`, `JWKS caching is correct: PyJWKClient(jwks_uri, cache_jwk_set=True, max_cached_keys=16) per provider, wrapped in a per-issuer discovery cache with a configurable TTL (LANCE_OIDC_CACHE_TTL, default 3600) and lazy refresh; multi-issuer selection reads the unverified iss only to PICK a provider and then proves it by verification (_provider_for, :212-227).`, `No identity is taken from an unverified header for an AUTHORIZATION decision anywhere. SK-01's X-User survives at exactly its three filed sites (media/deps.py:29, annotations/save.py:58, annotations/tags.py:95) and is attribution-only; a full `Header(` sweep across services/ and packages/ found no new copies, and @rask/api's BFF forwards only an explicit header allowlist so the browser path cannot reach it. current_subject (deps.py:99-106) documents the absence of a header fallback as deliberate.`, `The one other caller-supplied identity header, x-lance-originator (catalog/api/dependencies.py:262), is a recorded decision rather than drift: originator_hint (:238-256) length-bounds it, rejects usersets/wildcards/role literals via is_person_subject, and the notifications plane re-derives visibility per recipient at delivery — it authorizes nothing.`, `The gateway strips every trust header a client could forge — _CLIENT_SPOOFABLE = dapr-caller-app-id, dapr-api-token, dapr-app-id, x-lance-service-identity (gateway/__init__.py:66-77, applied at :340) — and the strip is pinned by services/gateway/tests/test_spoofable_headers.py, including case-insensitivity and a test that pins FastAPI binding the FIRST duplicate, which is the whole reason the strip is required.`, `The shared APP_API_TOKEN service credential is compared with secrets.compare_digest on bytes (dapr_auth.py:101), refuses public front doors unconditionally and independently of the token (:93-97), and is enforced closed at startup by assert_app_token_configured in lineage, medallion producer + mover, catalog and maintenance. ING-01 (ingest missing that call) remains the sole exception and is already filed.`, `The service door asks two questions, not one: the allowlist is checked BEFORE the credential store so it cannot be an enumeration oracle, privileged subjects require a dedicated credential and a missing one FAILS CLOSED rather than falling back to the shared token, and a secret-store outage raises SecretStoreUnreadable → 503 rather than collapsing into the 401 for 'not provisioned' (dapr_auth.py:237-293).`, `DaprActor's root-mounted callback surface is guarded in both services that mount it — guard_actor_routes(app) at annotator/main.py:196 and notifications/lifespan.py:51, with packages/service-kit/tests/test_actor_route_guard.py pinning it.`, `Fail-closed on FGA is the estate's default, not an aspiration: governed/fga.py maps ApiException + aiohttp.ClientError/TimeoutError/OSError to ServiceUnavailableError on check, batch_check, list_objects, list_users, expand, read_changes and the tuple writes (11 distinct `except _FAIL_CLOSED` sites), never to a False, an empty list or an empty tree. governed/deps.py:117-120, annotator/api/security.py:126-129, catalog/api/fga_deps.py:557-561, lineage/api/fga_deps.py:56-58 and notifications/api/visibility.py:110-116 all raise 503 for FGA-enabled-but-unwired rather than degrading to permissive.`, `The `fga_enabled and token is not None and client is not None` filters in the catalog (models.py:99, tables.py:189, warehouses.py:267/336, namespaces.py:666, credentials.py:62) are NOT fail-open: the router-level dependencies=[Depends(authorize)] at api/v1/router.py:40 already 401s a missing token and 503s a missing client whenever FGA is on, and models.py:10-11 records exactly that reasoning. All 26 catalog endpoint modules are mounted under that router; nothing bypasses it except the probes router.`, `batch_check is used where the reference asks for it: lineage's DatasetFilter.visible and enforce_output_authz (fga_deps.py:189/196/223), notifications' Visibility._filter (visibility.py:114), and the catalog's batch authorizer for write/create tiers (fga_deps.py:534/540). list_objects is used for user-scoped indexes (catalog tables.py:190, warehouses.py:268/337, models.py:100, me.py:107) and list_users/expand for the access-review surfaces — the full four-call vocabulary of authz.md § Beyond check.`, `Router-level dependencies=[...] is used where every route shares a gate: catalog api_router (authorize), lineage's datasets/columns/reconcile/governance read routers (require_metadata_access + audit_read) and its write_router (require_write_access), the annotator's task router (require_actor_plane) and jobs router (require_dapr_token), and every Dapr-delivered route in medallion, notifications, maintenance and compute's pruner.`, `Read/write asymmetry was checked service by service and is clean outside the medallion promotion pair: lineage gates /{name}/readers on the STRICTER require_write_access, flows gates GET /runs/{id} with the same relation as POST /runs and says why, notifications' inbox/prefs/watches are subject-scoped end to end, the catalog's members/projects/events/access_admin reads all carry an explicit require_relation, and maintenance's read-only reconcile cron is require_dapr_token-gated on the stated grounds that an unauthenticated trigger would be an estate-wide disclosure.`, `Provenance is bound to the verified principal, not the request body: lineage's enforce_author (fga_deps.py:96-104) overwrites the client-claimed author run facet with the token sub, and enforce_output_authz authorizes outputs, governed inputs AND the column-lineage upstreams (the third set was the hole the code documents closing) — plus it authorizes a run MUTATION by the data that run already wrote, so a public run id is not a rewrite handle.`, `Estate calibration re-verified in one pass and holds, with one exception: zero Optional[/Union[, zero `= Depends(` defaults, zero @app.on_event, zero python-jose/passlib. `import requests` is NOT zero — one site at packages/ray-kit/src/ray_kit/dashboard.py:23 (outside this lane; recorded so it is not re-recorded clean elsewhere).`, `database.md's pooling/session/Alembic content genuinely does not apply to this lane: no fleet service holds a relational session, and the only credential-bearing DB path (lineage's AGE psycopg pool) carries no authn/authz logic. Nothing in this lane was invented from it.`

### `observability` — observability.md

This estate is far from uninstrumented, and TODO.md's "missing telemetry all over the places" is only half right — what is missing is specific and structural, not general. The SDK layer is genuinely strong: `service_kit.setup_otel` (packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/otel.py) wires all three signals over OTLP/HTTP with atexit flushes because the fleet has no launcher, and six instrumentors (fastapi, httpx, logging, requests, grpc×2, aiohttp-client) each carry a comment recording the incident that added them. The chart is stronger still: four separate OTel env renderings all derived from one endpoint helper, `OTEL_PYTHON_FASTAPI_EXCLUDED_URLS` covering every probe path the render itself points at, and an invariant suite that pins probe exclusion, alert-group classification and the OTLP→Prometheus name mangling. Eight of the fourteen apps are launched under `opentelemetry-instrument` (the one lakehouse image ships the launcher via lineage's opentelemetry-distro), six run bare uvicorn under `setup_otel`; both paths are wired and neither is drift. What is dark is the layer the reference calls out as the one auto-instrumentation cannot reach. Eight of fourteen apps — gateway, catalog, lineage, viewer, search, annotator, compute, controlplane — open no manual span at all, and the estate's dominant I/O is exactly the kind Python cannot see for free: `pyarrow.fs.S3FileSystem` (Arrow C++), Lance's Rust object store, boto3/botocore and the kubernetes client's urllib3, none of which has an instrumentor in the lock. So the catalog's `drop_table` — nine I/O legs — renders as one HTTP span with two children. No `server_request_hook` exists anywhere, so the `X-Request-ID` the shared middleware mints and echoes to the client joins to nothing. And the metrics that do exist are largely unpageable: `chart/alerting/rules.yml` contains no `http.server` rule at all and no group for eight of the fourteen apps, including every app a browser actually talks to. The estate measures well and pages badly.

*Checked clean:* `Health-probe exclusion IS wired, contrary to what the code alone suggests: `setup_otel` passes no `excluded_urls`, but `chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:426` (rask.otelEnv) and :830 (lance.otelEnv) both render `OTEL_PYTHON_FASTAPI_EXCLUDED_URLS="/livez,/readyz,/healthz,/api/health,/metrics"`, the FastAPI instrumentor reads that itself with or without a launcher (verified at .venv/.../instrumentation/fastapi/__init__.py:275-278), the two lists are pinned byte-for-byte against each other, and `test_every_probe_path_the_chart_configures_is_excluded_from_tracing` derives the list from each pod's own probe paths. `RASK_API_PREFIX: "/api"` makes the fleet's health route `/api/health`, which is on the list. Not a defect.`, `Middleware ordering does NOT let short-circuit responses escape the span. I checked this specifically because the gateway registers its 403 blocklist middleware at __init__.py:288 AFTER `setup_otel` at :285, and the catalog adds body_limit/load_shed at main.py:258/262 after `FastAPI(...)` — under Starlette's insert(0) that would put both outside the OTel middleware. Installed instrumentor 0.65b0 does not use `add_middleware`: it patches `build_middleware_stack` (.venv/.../instrumentation/fastapi/__init__.py:291-390) so `OpenTelemetryMiddleware` wraps the entire user stack unconditionally. The gateway's 403s and the catalog's 413/429s are traced.`, `No hardcoded OTLP endpoint anywhere in Python — zero hits for `OTLPSpanExporter(endpoint=`, `4317`, `4318` in services/ or packages/. Every exporter reads `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_*` from the environment, exactly as the reference's anti-pattern table requires, and the chart derives all four env renderings from one `lance.otlpEndpoint` helper.`, `No SDK-side sampling — zero hits for `TraceIdRatioBased` or `sampler=`. Traces are always-on at the SDK with tail sampling left to the Collector, per the reference's anti-pattern table.`, ``service.name` is never set as a span attribute; it is a Resource attribute (otel.py:72 `Resource.create({"service.name": service_name})`) with `OTEL_SERVICE_NAME` rendered per pod by the chart.`, ``span.record_exception` is not called anywhere in production code. The two hits are comments at flows/activities.py:69 and notifications/fanout.py:169 that explicitly name it as deprecated and choose `set_status` + `log.exception` inside the active span instead — the exact remedy the reference's anti-pattern table prescribes.`, `Span status IS set on the manual spans that can fail, in four of the five instrumented services: ingest/workflow.py:1143, medallion/workflow.py:440, flows/activities.py:71, maintenance/sweep.py:382. (The two medallion produce spans are the exception, reported above.)`, `No span attribute or log line carries a token, credential or PII. Every `set_attribute` call in the estate was read: they carry versions, row counts, byte counts, dataset ids, transitions and submission ids — no subject, no email, no bearer. The `token=` fields in medallion's log lines are idempotency keys (`token = token or uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]`, media_produce.py:108), not credentials. `viewer/objects.py:91 extra={"secret": secret}` logs the secret's NAME in the Dapr store, not its value. The `audit()` stream (service-kit/governed/audit.py) carries `audit.subject` as an OIDC `sub` by deliberate compliance design and never reaches a span.`, `Metric label cardinality is disciplined estate-wide: every `.add()`/`.record()` call site uses a bounded closed set (status, outcome, transition, lane, kind, reason, topic, error.type). Run ids, dataset names and unit ids are explicitly kept off metrics — ingest/metrics.py:14 and flows/metrics.py:16 say so in their docstrings, and maintenance/core/lance_trace.py:65 rejects `index_uuid`/`part_id` as attributes for exactly this reason.`, `The fleet's missing-launcher problem is correctly solved rather than ignored: `setup_otel` registers atexit shutdown for all three batch processors (otel.py:114-116) with a comment explaining that `command: ["uvicorn"]` would otherwise discard the buffer at SIGTERM — the highest-value window there is.`, `Both entrypoint layouts reach instrumentation. The 5 `make_service_app` apps plus the gateway call `setup_otel`; the 8 lakehouse apps (catalog, lineage, medallion producer+mover, maintenance, viewer, search, annotator) run under `opentelemetry-instrument` (chart/templates/{services,medallion,maintenance,explorer}.yaml), whose launcher binary ships in the shared `.docker/rest-catalog.dockerfile` image via lineage's `opentelemetry-distro`. No app is uninstrumented at the HTTP-span level. `setup_otel(app, service_name="gateway")` with no `Settings` is a recorded, tested decision (otel.py:45-48, test_otel.py:148), not drift.`, `The estate calibration items, verified in one grep each and not re-reported: zero `Optional[`/`Union[`, zero `= Depends(` default args, zero `@app.on_event` (three hits are an unrelated `_promotion_event` test helper), zero `BackgroundTasks` in production code (six hits are comments explaining why it is refused), and the single `import requests` (packages/ray-kit/src/ray_kit/dashboard.py:23) is a real client that `RequestsInstrumentor` was deliberately added to cover.`

### `k8s-microservices` — kubernetes.md + microservices.md

This estate is a TWO-TIER deployment, and every k8s finding below is a symptom of that one fact. The "lance plane" (catalog, lineage, the medallion producer + three movers, maintenance) and the seven web zones are templated by services.yaml / medallion.yaml / frontends.yaml / maintenance.yaml and are essentially exemplary against kubernetes.md: `lance.securityContext` (runAsNonRoot + drop-ALL + RuntimeDefault + readOnlyRootFilesystem with a /tmp emptyDir), `lance.preStop`, `lance.appProbes` (`/livez` + `/readyz` + a 300 s startupProbe), an explicit `terminationGracePeriodSeconds`, and measured per-workload resource tiers. The "rask fleet" plane (gateway, compute, controlplane, flows, ingest, notifications) is templated by fleet.yaml + controlplane.yaml and receives NONE of the securityContext, no grace period, no startupProbe, and a readiness probe pointed at the same static liveness badge as the liveness probe — I verified this by rendering the chart, not by reading it (34 Deployments; the six fleet rows are the only first-party ones missing all four). The invariant test that exists to stop exactly this (`test_every_first_party_deployment_is_hardened`) checks only probes+preStop against a hand-maintained name tuple that omits controlplane, compute, flows, ingest and maintenance — so the gap is green. One-worker-per-pod is clean estate-wide and explicitly commented in five dockerfiles; requests/limits are present everywhere and measured. On the microservices half the estate is genuinely strong: synchronous hop depth never exceeds gateway→one service (catalog→lineage is Dapr pub/sub in-cluster, ingest→catalog is inside a workflow activity, the mover→catalog calls all go through `run_in_threadpool`), `require_dapr_token` guards every one of the ~20 sidecar-delivered routes, trace context is deliberately carried across the gRPC/Dapr and Ray boundaries with tests pinning it, and the Dapr Resiliency CR carries timeouts + status-matched retries with two recorded incidents in its comments. The real microservices defects are where the platform layer and the app layer disagree: an automatic 5xx retry aimed at write doors whose idempotency key is optional, and a drain gate applied to one of the two doors that needed it. One correction to open_python-audit.md's clean list while I am here: `import requests` is not zero — `packages/ray-kit/src/ray_kit/dashboard.py:23` has one, but it is used only to name `requests.exceptions.RequestException` in `RAY_TRANSIENT_ERRORS` (line 79), never as an HTTP client, and `service_kit.otel` instruments it deliberately; so the rule holds in substance and I did not file it.

*Checked clean:* `One uvicorn worker per pod — no `--workers N`, no gunicorn, no UvicornWorker anywhere in .docker/*.dockerfile, chart/, scripts/ or Makefile. Five dockerfiles carry an explicit `--workers intentionally unset` comment, and .docker/notifications.dockerfile:80-86 explains it as a CORRECTNESS bound (several ActorRuntimes behind one app-port). The chart overrides CMD with `command: ["uvicorn"]` + args and the only `extraArgs` in values.yaml is the gateway's `--proxy-headers` pair, so no values path can inject workers either.`, `Scaling is via `replicas:` — every Deployment renders one, using `hasKey|ternary` so an explicit `replicas: 0` is not swallowed (pinned by tests/unit/test_invariants.py::test_no_numeric_helm_default_can_swallow_an_explicit_zero).`, `resources.requests AND resources.limits present on all 34 rendered Deployments, with measured per-workload tiers (chart/values.yaml:443-488 — `compute` at 1536Mi after an observed exit-137 at 512Mi, rustfs at 2Gi after 33 OOMKills, otel-collector sized against its own hardcoded memory_limiter).`, `No self-installed SIGTERM/SIGINT handler in any app — zero `signal.signal` in services/ or packages/; uvicorn owns the signal and the lifespans' post-`yield` blocks do the cleanup.`, `No 503-during-shutdown middleware. The drain flag is read by `/readyz` and by two admission dependencies, never by a per-request middleware branch.`, `Liveness probes never touch a dependency. `/api/health` and `/livez` are both `async def ... -> Liveness()` with no I/O, and both carry an inline comment explaining why they are async rather than threadpooled.`, `readinessProbe timeoutSeconds is explicitly raised to 5 s on the fleet (fleet.yaml:227) with the measured reason recorded — k8s's 1 s default was making OOM kills present as probe failures on compute (#136).`, `Synchronous hop depth never exceeds two services. gateway→service is the only sync chain on the request path: catalog→lineage runs over Dapr pub/sub in-cluster (`LANCE_LINEAGE_TRANSPORT: dapr`, services.yaml:114), ingest→catalog happens inside a Dapr workflow activity (runtime.py:579), and the mover→catalog calls are all `run_in_threadpool`-wrapped (transform.py:805, :850).`, `Every sidecar-delivered route in the estate carries `require_dapr_token` — verified across catalog/api/dapr.py:36, lineage/api/dapr.py:31 + :42, lineage/api/reconcile_cron.py:46, medallion events.py:57 / bronze_arrival.py:48+:106 / promotions.py:289 / train.py:123 / dlq.py:33, notifications subscriptions.py:68+:112 / reconcile_cron.py:153 / dlq.py:36, compute/pruner.py:67, ingest/cron.py:69, maintenance/api/routes.py:176+:189, annotator jobs.py:47. No unguarded internal door found.`, `Trace context propagates across BOTH non-HTTP boundaries the estate has. Dapr: `service_kit/otel.py:148-151` installs both GrpcAioInstrumentorClient and GrpcInstrumentorClient with a comment explaining that installing one is worse than neither, and daprd has a real OTLP exporter (chart/templates/observability.yaml, pinned by test_dapr_sidecar_spans_actually_have_an_exporter). Ray: `ray_kit/submit.py:125` injects and all four scripts/ray_*_job.py extract, pinned by tests/unit/test_ray_trace_continuity.py.`, `Every sync cross-service HTTP call has a timeout. The one lifespan-built client with no client-level timeout (notifications/lifespan.py:119 `httpx.AsyncClient()`) is consumed only by LineageFeedClient and LineageCursorStore, and every one of their three call sites passes an explicit `timeout=self._timeout` (reconciler.py:257, :282, :307) under an outer `asyncio.timeout` budget (reconciler.py:350).`, `Retry policy lives in one wrapper, not sprinkled: the Dapr Resiliency CR owns invocation retries (dapr-resiliency.yaml:125-136) with `matching: 408,429,500-599` so 4xx is never retried, and no `for attempt in range(3)` loop exists in any service.`, `Dapr is used only for Workflow + pubsub.jetstream + secrets/state, not put in front of every primitive; no service mesh; no Kafka; no Redis.`, ``@app.on_event`: zero. Legacy `Optional[...]`/`Union[...]`: zero across services/ and packages/. Default-arg `Depends()`: zero (all `Annotated[T, Depends(...)]`).`, `No relational DB in a fleet service and no `DATABASE_URL` in the fleet configmap — database.md's pooling/Alembic content genuinely does not apply here; lineage's AGE pool is the sanctioned exception and is the one thing its `/readyz` ready_check probes.`

### `limits-realtime` — rate-limiting.md + websockets.md + cache.md

Honest shape of these three concerns at HEAD. (1) RATE LIMITING: the estate has none, anywhere. `slowapi` appears in no pyproject and no lockfile; there is no `Limiter`, no `key_func`, no `@limiter.limit`, no `RateLimitExceeded` handler, and no `limit-rps`/`limit-connections` annotation on the Ingress (`chart/values.yaml:1638-1648` sets only proxy-buffer/timeout/body-size). The ONE piece of admission control in 15 apps is the catalog's `WriteConcurrencyLimitMiddleware` (`services/catalog/src/catalog/api/load_shed.py`) — a concurrency cap, not a rate limit, scoped to three Arrow-IPC write suffixes on one service — plus its sibling `BodySizeLimitMiddleware`. Neither was generalized: `service_kit.middleware.register_middleware` adds only CORS + RequestID + Timing, so the five `make_service_app` services and the media trio have no body cap and no concurrency cap at all. That matters because the Ingress routes `/api` → gateway (`chart/templates/ingress.yaml:66`) and the gateway does no authn — it strips spoofable headers and proxies. The ingress docstring at lines 18-21 claims the root-absolute `/api/explorer|/api/search` side door was deliberately removed so unauthenticated edge traffic could not reach the media services; the `/api` → gateway rule plus the gateway's `/api/explorer`, `/api/explorer/search` and `/api/explorer/annotations` rows restore exactly that path one hop over. (2) WEBSOCKETS/PUSH: the Python estate exposes ZERO WebSocket and ZERO SSE endpoints — `grep` for `@…websocket`, `WebSocket`, `EventSourceResponse`, `sse_starlette`, `text/event-stream` across `services/` and `packages/` returns nothing but `StreamingResponse` (used for HTTP Range bodies and the gateway's re-stream). Every rule in websockets.md — auth-before-accept, heartbeat, ConnectionManager on app.state, NATS fan-out — is vacuously satisfied. Push exists one layer up, in the SvelteKit zones: `frontend/microfrontends/lakehouse/src/lib/live/feeds.remote.ts` uses `query.live` (SSE from the zone's own Bun server, not from Python), and its own comment records that thirteen files previously ran `setInterval(load, 5000)`. The substitute-polling that remains is real and concentrated in the `compute` zone: seven `setInterval` loops at 2000-5000 ms (`compute/src/lib/boards/*.svelte`, `routes/logviewer/+page.svelte:84,138`, `routes/jobs/[id]/+page.svelte:82`) all hitting the unauthenticated, unlimited `/api/ray/*` rows. (3) CACHING: no Redis by ruling, so every cache is in-process and per-pod. I found ten. Three are genuinely well built and I record them clean: the catalog's `warehouse_binding_cache` (positives-only, 300 s TTL, plus pub/sub broadcast eviction on unbind), the search `result_cache` (Lance-version-keyed so writes invalidate by construction, LRU under BOTH a count and a 64 MiB byte bound), and the compiled-FGA-model `lru_cache`s (immutable input). The rest split into recorded trades I am not re-litigating (`dapr_auth._secret_bundle` pins a service token until restart — its docstring says so and names the rollout cost; the viewer's `_creds` does the same and the audit already lists it), duplicates of open findings (`VS-04` search key omits `spec.table`, `VS-08` the Cypher engine global), and one live gap: the atlas `points_cache` bounds by ENTRY COUNT while holding multi-MB Arrow payloads — the exact defect the search cache was fixed for in #141 and that the fix was never carried across to.

*Checked clean:* `rate-limiting.md 'no rate limit on expensive routes' — checked estate-wide; nothing rate-limits, so this is reported as a finding rather than clean, but I verified there is also no HIDDEN limiter: zero slowapi/limits imports, zero @limiter.limit, zero RateLimitExceeded handlers, zero X-RateLimit-* headers in services/ or packages/`, `rate-limiting.md 'never rate-limit health checks' — CLEAN by construction: no limiter exists, and the catalog's load-shed middleware matches only POST + Arrow-IPC content-type + /create|/insert|/merge_insert, so /livez, /readyz and /api/health are never shed (services/catalog/src/catalog/api/load_shed.py:38-49)`, `rate-limiting.md 'in-process defaultdict rate limiter (article-style)' — CLEAN: nobody hand-rolled a token bucket or sliding window. The one admission control is a concurrency counter with an explicit single-threaded-loop atomicity argument, not a rate algorithm`, `rate-limiting.md 'returning a plain 429 with no headers' — CLEAN: the only 429 producer (load_shed.py:_reject) emits RFC 9457 problem+json AND a Retry-After header`, `websockets.md ALL RULES — vacuously clean: zero WebSocket endpoints in the Python estate (grep for @app.websocket/@router.websocket returns 0 across services/ and packages/). No auth-after-accept, no missing heartbeat, no in-process ConnectionManager, no per-message NATS subscription, no span.record_exception in a WS handler`, `websockets.md 'WebSocket for one-way push, use SSE instead' — CLEAN: no SSE in Python either; the only server-push in the estate is SvelteKit query.live from the zone servers, and the Ingress already carries the proxy-read-timeout: 3600 annotation that keeps an idle live stream open (chart/values.yaml:1640, pinned by tests/unit/test_invariants.py)`, `cache.md 'response-caching middleware' — CLEAN: no route-boundary or middleware response cache anywhere. Every cache found sits inside a service/handler function where the key is explicit, exactly as cache.md prescribes`, `cache.md 'mutation -> invalidation' for the catalog warehouse binding cache — CLEAN: positives-only (negatives are deliberately never cached to avoid wrong-bucket routing), a 300 s TTL floor (core/config.py:262), AND explicit pops on the three mutations (warehouses.py:534, 542, 760) broadcast cross-replica`, `cache.md 'key on everything that varies' for the search result cache VERSION signature — CLEAN: cache_key folds (dataset id, a table:version signature over row+fts+every vector-binding table, a sha256 of all spec knobs + sorted filters + the uploaded image digest). Any write bumps a Lance version and the stale entry becomes unreachable — no manual busting needed`, `cache.md 'is it bounded' for the search result cache — CLEAN: LRU under TWO bounds, entry count (256) and total bytes (64 MiB), with an entry larger than the whole budget returned uncached (result_cache.py:110-142)`, `in-process caches over IMMUTABLE inputs — CLEAN, no invalidation needed: catalog access.py:85/95, access_admin.py:67-113 and fga_deps.py:355 lru_cache the compiled OpenFGA model.json; service_kit/lakehouse/lance_session.py:34 @cache is an int-keyed process-wide lance.Session; controlplane routes.py:25 lru_cache(maxsize=1) is a k8s reader factory`, `annotator serve_discovery module cache — CLEAN enough: 15 s TTL, keyed on discovery_url whose cardinality is 1 (settings-derived), with an explicit reset_cache() test seam and a docstring stating the process-global choice. Worst case is 15 s of stale Serve routing after a redeploy`, `estate-calibration re-checks (one grep each): legacy Optional[]/Union[] = 0; default-arg Depends() = 0; @app.on_event = 0 (the 3 grep hits are a test helper named _promotion_event); requests-as-an-HTTP-client = 0 (the single 'import requests' at packages/ray-kit/src/ray_kit/dashboard.py:23 is used ONLY for requests.exceptions.RequestException in the RAY_TRANSIENT_ERRORS tuple at line 79, because the Ray SDK raises them)`

## Appendix B — findings already filed in `open_python-audit.md`

These reproduce at HEAD, but they belong to the existing audit and are tracked there. Listed so the
coverage of this sweep is visible and so nobody files them twice.

| Owning id | Lane | Confirmed at HEAD |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `catalog-api-18` | `conventions` | Still open at HEAD (line numbers have shifted): uncached blocking file read of the FGA model DSL inside an async route |
| `MAINT-07` | `conventions` | Still open at HEAD (cited at 704/710, now at 803): reconcile() constructs a boto3 client inside an async def when the lifespan-built one is absent |
| `VS-12` | `conventions` | Still open at HEAD: the viewer's catalog resolve builds a fresh httpx.Client per request while a pooled one sits on app.state |
| `catalog-api-01` | `errors` | catalog/members.py raises service_kit ConflictError — the same handler bypass filed for stores.py, at two sites the finding does not list |
| `X11` | `errors` | The dual-install apps' 422 body is decided by registration order — install_problem_handlers silently replaces register_handlers' override |
| `X8` | `health` | compute and flows log `startup_complete` without ever setting the flag, so `service_kit.draining` reads False on those pods forever |
| `GW-BUFFERS-REQUEST-BODY` | `files-streaming` | One of fifteen apps has a request-size guard, and the front door that every browser request crosses is not it — the catalog's 413 never fires for gate |
| `VS-10` | `files-streaming` | Unsanitized S3 key in `Content-Disposition` — a non-ASCII object key 500s on latin-1 header encoding |
| `VS-05` | `files-streaming` | `/api/page` and `/api/pages` materialize every page blob to serve one image |
| `ANN-12` | `files-streaming` | Arrow-IPC import reads and materializes an unbounded request body |
| `F-LIN-04` | `pagination` | lineage `/runs` and `/jobs` accept no page parameter at all, while four sibling routes in the same service are paginated |
| `ingest-flow-07` | `k8s-microservices` | No trace context crosses the ingest plane's NATS JetStream boundary — UnitTask.traceparent is declared and never written or read |
| `COMPUTE-UNBOUNDED-QUERY-PARAMS` | `limits-realtime` | tail/lines are forwarded to the Ray dashboard unbounded, defeating the very bound job_logs' docstring claims — and it is the route the compute zone po |
| `VS-04` | `limits-realtime` | Search result-cache key omits spec.table |

## Appendix C — the three references with no lane

`database.md`, `redis.md` and `project-template.md` were read in full and deliberately not given a lane.
The reasoning is recorded here because "we skipped it" and "it does not apply" are different claims and
only one of them is checkable:

- **`database.md` — applies to a surface of two, and both were checked by hand.**
  `services/lineage/src/lineage/core/age.py:48` is textbook-correct: `AsyncConnectionPool(..., open=False,
  min_size=1, max_size=8, check=AsyncConnectionPool.check_connection, kwargs={"options":
  "-c statement_timeout=..."})` — `check_connection` is psycopg's `pool_pre_ping`, and `open=False` means
  it is built in the lifespan, not at import. `packages/tracker/postgres.py:37` is not —
  `create_engine(_normalize_dsn(dsn), echo=False)` with no `pool_pre_ping`, no `pool_recycle`, no sizing —
  but `grep` for `PostgresTracker` outside the package returns nothing, and the existing audit already
  files that package three ways (`PS-09` zero consumers, `PS-10` `metadata.create_all` in the constructor,
  `PS-11` a long-lived `Session` holding a checked-out connection). No new lane would have added anything.
- **`redis.md` — the estate has no Redis by ruling**, so the wiring half does not apply. Its decision
  matrix and its in-process-cache anti-patterns DO, and those were folded into the `limits-realtime` lane
  (which is where the atlas `points_cache` finding comes from). One question it raises is genuinely
  unanswered anywhere in the estate and is filed as an open question below.
- **`project-template.md` — the layering rules it states are already the existing audit's epic E7**
  (`catalog-api-03`, "multi-step orchestration lives in endpoint functions instead of services/", and
  siblings). Re-running it as a lane would have produced duplicates by construction.

### Open question this sweep could not answer

**How is an OIDC bearer revoked before its `exp`?** `redis.md` § *Use 3 — JWT `jti` revocation list* is
the standard answer and the estate has no Redis. Nothing in the estate answers the question another way:
there is no revocation list, no `jti` check, and the service credential (`APP_API_TOKEN`) has no rotation
story either. For a leaked user token the current answer is "wait for expiry"; for a leaked service token
it is "rotate the secret and restart every consumer". Whether that is acceptable is an owner decision,
not a defect — but it should be a decision rather than an omission.
